| `RX_VK_WB_SPANS=0` | (Triforce, 11-24) A CPU read of GPU results waits for the newest write-back noted on any 4 KB page it touches, as before. By default a read that covers its first or last page only in part waits there for the write-backs over its own bytes: the game keeps its culling tables double-buffered, two slots back to back whose seam is one page, and each frame's read of one slot waited for the submission that was writing the other. Never a later submission than the page rule's; the captures' looks at a page are unchanged (`gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `wb_spans_on`, `tests/test_wb_spans.py`) |
| `RX_VK_BUFFER_BUDGET=0` | (Triforce, 11-24) A host-visible buffer (the staging arenas, the read-backs' buffers) is made where the allocator prefers it whatever that heap's budget, as before. By default it is first asked for within the budgets, so that the allocator takes the next memory type when the preferred heap is full: on an AMD APU without resizable BAR the preferred heap is the carve-out's 256 MB window, a load's staging overran it and the driver then refused every submit (`submit ... failed (-2)`, a black screen with the sound going on). Where no heap has room the buffer is made as before (`gpu/vk/vk_vma.cpp` `create_buffer`) |
# Hookshot (oot-recomp)

**Hookshot** is the port's name (as Skybound is TOTKPort's and BlueShell is mkw-recomp's); the maintainer's repository
folder stays `oot-recomp`. The game builds as `Triforce.exe` + `triforce_vk.dll` (`triforce` + `triforce_vk.so` on Linux).

A static recompilation of the Switch 2 *The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time* remake to native x86-64 Windows and Linux:
the game's code lifted to C (NotRexed's a64-kit, Rexcomp's AArch64 kit), the Horizon OS and NVN2 implemented
natively, the GPU work replayed on Vulkan. It is a fork of mkw-recomp (`..\mkw-recomp`, Mario Kart World), whose
paths it keeps so MKW's fixes apply here.

**No game files are in this repository.** The dump is read where it lies (`work/dump.txt`); everything made from it
(modules, image, lifted C, builds, runs) goes to `work/`, which git ignores.

Status: the game plays; the README's [status table](../readme.md#project-status) says how far. The design, its stages and
the lessons carried over from MKW: [design.md](design.md).

## Use

Requirements: Python 3 with `pip install -r requirements.txt` (`lz4`, `numpy`, `capstone`, `unicorn` 2.1,
`zstandard`, and the button-prompt packages); a compiler: on Windows llvm-mingw
(`HOOKSHOT_MINGW`, or an `llvm-mingw` folder in the clone), else emsdk's clang at `C:\emsdk` (or `EMSDK`) with Visual
Studio's C++ tools; on Linux clang and lld. For the Vulkan back end: distro Vulkan / SPIR-V / VMA headers under
`/usr`, or `HOOKSHOT_VULKAN_HEADERS` / `HOOKSHOT_SPIRV_HEADERS` / `HOOKSHOT_VMA_HEADERS`, or a CPM package cache
(`RX_CPM`, else `cpm` in the clone, else the old caches in mkw-recomp's `work/eg` or `..\Eglantine\.cache\cpm`).
Without those the standalone build stops. [CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md#development-setup) has the rest.

On Linux, `make_game.py` checks what the build needs before it reads or translates the game, and names every missing one
in one error: the Python modules `requirements.txt` pins (a source build), the renderer's headers (CPM caches first, then
`HOOKSHOT_*_HEADERS`, then `/usr/local` and `/usr`; not with the AppImage's prebuilt renderer), FFmpeg's libraries
(`HOOKSHOT_FFMPEG` with `lib/libavcodec.a`, else `pkg-config`), clang (and clang++), and a test program linked with lld
(`HOOKSHOT_SYSROOT` when set). The button-prompt packages stay optional; nothing is installed.

Run everything with `python -I` from this folder.

| Command | What |
|---|---|
| `python -I nx2/nup.py <file.nup> --extract work/dump` | the `.nup` dump package to `work/dump/exefs` + `romfs` (newest title; ZBIC NSOs made plain by `nx2/unpack.py`) |
| `python -I nx2/dump.py --dump <folder with exefs/ and romfs/> --set` | what the dump is (Switch 1 or 2, ids, modules, SDK, NVN); `--set` records it in `work/dump.txt` |
| `python -I nx2/lift.py [--write]` | image + discovery + coverage (`work/image/coverage.txt`); `--write` emits the C to `work/gen` |
| `python -I nx2/census.py --nx2` | instruction census per module, after the Switch 2 forms |
| `python -I nx2/test_insn.py [--per 6 --states 12]` | every instruction form the game uses, Unicorn vs lifted C |
| `python -I nx2/test_leaf.py [--count 3000 --states 4] [--calls]` | random whole functions (or call trees), Unicorn vs recompiled native code on the real image |
| `python -I nx2/lift.py --standalone --write` then `python -I nx2/build.py --standalone --jobs 4` | the standalone game: `work/sa/build/hookshot.exe` + `hookshot_vk.dll` |
| `powershell -File scripts/run-vk.ps1 [-Window] [-Seconds 120] [-Name x]` | a muted test run on Vulkan, with screenshots in `work/runs/<name>/shots` |
| `python -I scripts/prof.py <stacks file>` | a profile sampled with `HOS_PROFILE=<seconds>` + `HOS_SAMPLE=<thread>` + `HOS_SAMPLE_FILE=<stacks file>` (the sampler runs only with `HOS_PROFILE` set), named from the linker maps |

The dump can also come from `OOT_DUMP` (its folder) or `OOT_EXEFS`; `OOT_WORK` moves the work folder.

The built game's own arguments (`image.bin`, `--romfs`, `--user`, `--trace`, `--prepare-shaders`) are in the README's
[Running the game from the command line](../readme.md#running-the-game-from-the-command-line).

Scripted test input (`HOS_INPUT_SCRIPT` and `HOS_INPUT_LIVE`) is applied before the window-focus check. A log saying
the game window is not in front does not mean those presses were discarded. `HOS_HEADLESS` and
`HOS_WINDOW_NOACTIVATE` change window creation, not this input routing. The game must still reach a state that reads
and responds to the supplied controller input.

Physical keyboard and mouse input stays with the foreground window. Physical controllers can also play in the
background when the existing "Controller in background" option or `HOS_INPUT_BACKGROUND=1` enables them;
`HOS_INPUT_BACKGROUND=0` overrides that option. With `HOS_INPUT_EXCLUSIVE` set (to any value, `0` too) the script
alone drives the game until 5 s after its last press; without it the player's devices add to the script's presses. It
is not a focus override.

### Log levels

A log line is info unless it says otherwise: `hos_log` and the renderer's `logf` write it as before. `hos_log_at(HOS_LOG_TRACE,
...)` (`host/hos.h`) and `logf_at(log_trace, ...)` (`gpu/vk/vk_internal.h`) give it another level, written after the thread's
tag: `[hos host] [trace] vk: ...`, and likewise `[error]`, `[warn]` and `[debug]`. The log file keeps every line; a log viewer
can show info and above and keep the rest one step away, and one that does not read the tag shows the line with it. Per-item
lines a player cannot act on (one per program or pipeline, a decoder's details) are debug or trace, among them the movie
upscaler's networks a card leaves out (`vk: program ... not translated: a network left out on this card`, counted apart from
the 30 lines real translation failures get); `HOS_TRACE`'s calls are trace. `tests/test_log_levels.py` checks the tags.

## Switches for testing and debugging

The running game reads its switches from the environment or from `hookshot_env.txt` beside `image.bin` (one
`NAME=VALUE` a line, `#` for notes; `HOS_ENV_FILE=<path>` reads that file instead). The build reads its own from the
environment. The README has the ones players are asked to try (`RX_VK_ASTC_RGBA8`, `RX_VK_LOW_SPEC`,
`RX_VK_MOVIE_UPSCALE`, `RX_VK_SKIP_CODE`, `HOS_OOT_CHEATS`). There are several hundred more, most of them one-off
experiments: `grep -rn 'getenv("' host gpu/vk` lists them, and the comment where each is read says what it does.
"Set" below means any value, `0` included.

`RX_VK_MESH_NOCULL=<prefix,...>` turns off the culling in the task shaders whose native code key starts with one of
the prefixes. On a Steam Deck, `RX_VK_MESH_NOCULL=2c56dc1006942f74` avoids the same hang without leaving anything out:
that task shader walks a coarse depth grid cell by cell for every meshlet and ends with
`visible = (result & 1) | (c3[2320] & 4)`, bit 4 being the game's own "draw everything". The switch makes that answer
true, and dead code elimination removes the grid walk, so every meshlet is drawn and the rasterizer clips what is off
screen. It costs some GPU time on cards that cull quickly, so it is off unless set. The log says
"culling off, every meshlet drawn" for each task shader it changed.

### Running a test

| Switch | What it does |
|---|---|
| `HOS_HEADLESS` (set) | No window: the renderer draws offscreen, for screenshots and frame dumps |
| `HOS_WINDOW_SIZE=<w>x<h>` | The window's size in pixels (1280x720 by default, wider for an ultrawide picture; 64 to 16384 a side) |
| `HOS_WINDOW_NOACTIVATE` (set) | The window opens behind the others and does not take the focus (Windows) |
| `HOS_FULLSCREEN=1` | Starts in fullscreen; the Insert menu's Fullscreen row is then left alone |
| `HOS_ALT_MENU=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, after Casual Gamer's #dev-fixes post) A lone Alt is left to Windows, as before: it enters the menu loop on the window's thread, which during the start-up screen is the one waiting for the shaders, and the screen froze until a key was pressed. By default the window, which has no menu bar, answers that command itself (`host/display.c` `keymenu_dropped`, `tests/test_alt_menu.py`); Alt+Space's system menu, Alt+Enter, F10 and Alt+F4 are as they were |
| `HOS_FLIGHT_JUMP=B` | (Triforce, 11-24) The Debug tab's No-clip flight takes off and lands with a double-tap of B and climbs while B is held, whatever the game's Swap Jump Controls says, as before (`=X`: X always). By default it is the game's own Jump: X (the keyboard's Space), or B with Swap Jump Controls on, read from the game's option `OptionData.IsChangeJumpButton` at Link's update (`host/flight_jump.h`, `host/nn_hid.c` `flight_jump`, `tests/test_flight_jump.py`). With B always, a double-tap of the Jump the player knew never took off |
| `HOS_POUCH_LIVE=0` | (Triforce, 11-24) The Debug tab's Inventory sets an item's count in GameData alone, as before. By default the game's own pouch is given or loses the difference first, through the pouch manager's add (`f_8112d570`, the reason "Script") and remove (`f_833c4fa0`), as its event action OneShotAddOrRemovePouchContent does; the pouch then writes GameData itself, and GameData is set here only where the pouch refuses (the songs) (`host/pouch_live.h`, `host/hos_main.c` `oot_pouch_change`, `tests/test_pouch_live.py`). What the game does when it gives Link something is done for an item given here too: in a run on 11-24 the Kokiri Sword was equipped and the Goron's Bracelet and the Golden Gauntlets were worn at once (the gauntlets and the scales are the game's ability items, `ExpansionAbilityActor` `LiftableGrade` and `DiveDepth`). With GameData alone the pause menu showed them and none of that happened |
| `HOS_AUDIO=0` | No sound device is opened (the mixer still runs); `scripts/run-vk.ps1` sets it unless `-Sound` |
| `HOS_AUDIO_AUX=0` | (Triforce, 11-24) The game's Aux effects are accepted and passed through, as before: the mix they are on plays as it came. The default runs them: each 5 ms frame the mix's sent buffers go to the game's own effect code as whole frames (`ReadAuxSendBuffer`) and what it hands back (`WriteAuxReturnBuffer`) takes their place, silence when it has handed nothing back in time. Passed through, an effect's send was heard as one more dry source and the effect itself never. `HOS_AUDIO_EFFECTS=0` leaves them out too (`host/nn_audio.c` "Aux", `tests/test_audio_aux.py`) |
| `HOS_AUDIO_EFFECTS=0` | The game's plain reverb, delay and biquad filter effects on its mixes and its splitter destinations' biquad filters are accepted and left out, as before they were emulated (the sound unfiltered and at full level: the "Volcano eruption audio bugged" ticket); the I3DL2 reverb still runs. By default they run on the mix buffers in the order the game added them (`host/nn_audio.c` "mix effects"; `tests/test_audio_mix_effects.py`) |
| `HOS_LANGUAGE=<code>` | The game's language for this run, a code from `language.txt` |
| `HOS_OUTPUT_RES=<0..8>`, `HOS_OUTPUT_SIZE=<w>x<h>`, `HOS_HANDHELD=1`, `HOS_ASPECT=<ratio>` | Over the options file: the output resolution (as `output_res` below), Custom's size (as `output_width` and `output_height`), the handheld profile, and the picture's shape whatever the Output resolution and the Ultrawide row say (a ratio, width over height: 2.3889 is 3440x1440's; the picture is the output's height in that shape, kept to 3840x2160's pixels). The Insert menu shows `HOS_OUTPUT_SIZE` and `HOS_ASPECT` in the rows they set, which then have nothing to choose |
| `HOS_WIDE_UI=0\|1`, `HOS_WIDE_UI_LOG=<n>`, `HOS_HUD_MAX_RATIO=<ratio>` | (Triforce) The HUD's layout on a picture wider than 16:9 (`host/hos_wide_ui.c`, ozwaldorf's pane planner): with the Display tab's HUD position at Screen edges the HUD's side panes move out to the screen's edges, planned at each present from the panes as the game places them (`nx2/oot_wide.py` `UI_SITES`), so the row changes at once; with Menu sides at Extended the menus' side panes too. Middle, the default, plans nothing. Not on a picture narrower than 16:9. `HOS_WIDE_UI=0`: off, and Screen edges moves the HUD's panes as the layouts load (`host/prompts.c` `build_layouts`), as before; `=1`: Screen edges whatever the row. `HOS_WIDE_UI_LOG=<n>` logs the first n changes of the plan, each root layout and the plan's time; `HOS_HUD_MAX_RATIO` (12:5 by default, 0 none) is the widest the HUD's sides go |
| `HOS_DYNRES=0`, `HOS_REALTIME_STEP=0`, `HOS_TEXT_SPEED=<0..3>` | Over the options file: Resolution 100% (non-zero: Auto), Game speed Per frame (1: Real time), Text speed |
| `RX_VK_FIRST_START_AUTO=1`, `RX_VK_FIRST_START_AUTO=<MB>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PRESENT-46, after Source A's; off by default) A first start sized to the card: with no options file, `RX_VK_LOW_SPEC` not asked and `HOS_DYNRES` not set, a weak card starts with the Render resolution row at 50% in place of Auto, or at 33% for an integrated GPU beside 4 processor threads or fewer (`gpu/vk/first_start.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_menu.cpp` `menu_first_start`, called from `mkwvk_init` once the device is made). Weak is Source A's tier 1: an integrated GPU (a handheld's is one; the `SteamDeck` variable is not read, it took desktops for handhelds), a card without mesh shaders, or 4.5 GB of video memory or less. Only that row is set: the upscaler, the textures and the output stay, and nothing is saved, so each start chooses again until the player saves options. A number over 1 plays a card of that many MB (the lab: `=4096` on a large card). Not set or `=0`: as before. Off until the owner decides what a first start on a weak card gets; unmeasured (Source A states no gain and tried it on one RTX 4070 Ti). One `menu: first start:` line says the choice |
| `HOS_STEP_STEADY=0` | (Triforce, on by default since the lab's A/B; `=0` the mean of the last five as before) The game's real-time step kept steady, after NX2navi's steady step (`host/step_steady.h`, `host/nn_vi.c` `realtime_step`): the mean of the last 12 present intervals without their longest and shortest in place of the mean of the last five (`HOS_REALTIME_WINDOW`), the paced period itself where that mean is within 3% of it, and what the display showed and the game was not given repaid at a 64th a frame, 10% of the step at most (a quarter second owed at most). A cap the game holds steps it by the period exactly, and a hitch is paid back over most of a second instead of in the next five frames (a 50 ms frame at 240: 200 frames of at most 10% more, where the mean of five gave five steps of 13 ms). Game time still follows the clock |
| `RX_VK_FG_KEEP=1` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's PRESENT-18) Frame generation's images are kept once made, as before. The default lets them go after 120 presents in a row with frame generation off (about two seconds at 60, so a row stepped through keeps them): the built-in interpolation's frames and made-frame sets, which stayed in VRAM until a resize after it was turned off or left for NVIDIA's own, and AMD FSR 4 frame generation's four images of the picture without the HUD once that row is left. The display thread is drained and the GPU idle first; they are made again at the next frame that needs them. The log says how many MB went (`gpu/vk/framegen_idle.h`, `vk_framegen.cpp` `framegen_idle`) |
| `RX_VK_CAPTURE_TABLES=0` | (Triforce, 11-21, the upscaler audit's UPS-02) The stored draws' two tables as `std::unordered_map`, emptied and filled every frame, as before. While NVIDIA DLSS, AMD FSR 4 or AMD FSR 3.1 upscales, every plain draw into the scene stores its positions and is drawn again into the motion vectors; the count of a model's draws and the frame before's draws by their key took a node from the heap for every stored draw every frame (about 1,400 of each in the owner's log, whose status line said 0.65 ms of CPU a frame for the stored draws in all). The default keeps their memory from frame to frame, so a frame the size of the one before takes nothing from the heap, and gives the next frame's list of draws its room at once. The status line's `ms CPU a frame` is the measure (`gpu/vk/capture_tables.h`, `vk_temporal.cpp` `capture_motion`). Not built or run in the game when it was written |
| `RX_VK_REDRAW_STATE=0` | (Triforce, 11-21, the upscaler audit's UPS-03) Every stored draw drawn again into the motion vectors sets the primitive topology, the primitive restart and its index buffer before it draws, as before. The default sets each only where the draw's differs from what the pass has set so far (its first draw sets all three): about 900 draws a frame are drawn again in the owner's log, nearly all of them triangle lists without restart, so two commands a draw are left out, and the index buffer's where two draws in a row bind the same one at the same offset (`gpu/vk/redraw_state.h`, `vk_temporal.cpp` `capture_motion`). Not built or run in the game when it was written |
| `RX_VK_DLSS_MODE_SLACK=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PRESENT-38) DLSS's quality mode found strictly, as before: a render size is a mode's only inside the range NGX answers. Not set: a size outside a range by a rounding (a 64th of the range's largest size, 2 pixels at least: `gpu/vk/dlss_checks.h` `mode_for`) is that mode's, so the Render resolution row's 33% at 2560x1440 (854x480, NGX's is 853x480) draws with DLSS and not the game's own upscale; and the feature there is is let go only once a mode took the new size. The lab's `dlssstrict` phases switch it within a run |
| `RX_VK_DLSS_MODEL_SETTLE=<frames>` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's PRESENT-37) The DLSS model row steps K, M, L, NVIDIA's default and applies live: going from K to L made a feature for M on the way, tens of ms each, and NGX keeps a released feature's video memory for some seconds. A new preset is taken once the row has held it for `<frames>` frames, 30 when not set; the feature already made draws on meanwhile, and with no feature the row's preset is taken at once. `0`: at once, as before (`gpu/vk/dlss_checks.h` `PresetSettle`, `vk_dlss.cpp` `prepare`) |
| `RX_VK_DLSS_CNN_VRAM=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PRESENT-36, after Source A's and Source B's; off by default) On a card with less than 7 GB of video memory the DLSS model row's K (the default) asks NGX for preset E, NVIDIA's last CNN model, and the row reads "CNN (E)" (`gpu/vk/upscaler_rows.h` `cnn_for_vram`, `gpu/vk/vk_dlss.cpp` `wanted_preset`, `dlss_cnn_vram`). M, L and NVIDIA's default stay as chosen, `RX_VK_DLSS_PRESET` goes over it, and cards of 7 GB and more are not touched. Source A measured it on a 6 GB laptop RTX 3050 (1080p, DLSS Performance, one run a preset): 15.75 to 14.20 ms of GPU a frame, NGX's own memory 85 to 58 MB. Off here, as in Source B: preset E ghosts more on grass and thin objects at the smallest render sizes and NVIDIA marks it deprecated, so the picture is the owner's to look at first. A DLSS runtime without E falls back to NVIDIA's default; `RX_VK_DLSS_LOG=1` shows the preset NGX took. One `vk: NVIDIA DLSS: ... MB of video memory: the K row draws with preset E` line says it is in use; `RX_VK_TEST_CARD_MB=6144` plays a 6 GB card |
| `RX_VK_DLSS_FEATURE_MAX=1` | (Triforce, 11-21, the upscaler audit's UPS-01) NVIDIA DLSS's feature made for the largest render size its quality mode allows, whatever the Render resolution row says, as before. NGX answers that largest size as the output's own, so at a fixed share the feature was made for 2.25 times the pixels it was ever given at Quality (4 at Performance, 9 at Ultra Performance), and the chain's colour, depth and motion images with it; the owner's log has DLSS's own video memory at 1069 MB for such a feature with preset M, where NVIDIA's table has 456 MB at 4K, and NVIDIA's guide says the cost of presets L and M follows the size the feature is initialized for. The default makes a fixed share's feature for its own render size; the row's Auto (the game's dynamic resolution), and any run in which the render size is seen changing under one output, make it for the mode's whole range as before (one feature made again when that is first seen). DLAA's is the output's either way. The `feature N made` line says which, and the status line's `its own ... MB` and `its images ... MB` are the measure (`gpu/vk/dlss_feature.h`, `vk_dlss.cpp` `prepare`). Not built or run in the game when it was written |
| `RX_VK_DLSS_PREWARM=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PRESENT-32, after Source A's: its first step; off by default) NVIDIA DLSS at the output's size takes the scaled quality mode whose range holds that size, and only NVIDIA DLAA the one-size mode (`gpu/vk/dlss_checks.h` `dlaa_feature`, `gpu/vk/vk_dlss.cpp` `prepare`). With the Render resolution row at Auto the game starts at the output's size and lowers it some frames later: before, a one-size feature and then a second one made in play (Source A's save load-in: 119 ms, then 29.5 ms 17 frames on); with the switch one feature, for its mode's whole range. Off by default because DLSS at full size then draws with the scaled mode's network, not DLAA's: a picture to compare first. The rest of the entry is not built: making the feature, the chain's images and a first evaluation ahead in a light frame (a title or loading screen) needs the first gameplay upscale's sizes and colour kind before it has happened, which only a run with DLSS can settle. `tests/test_dlss_prewarm.py` runs the decisions over a load-in |
| `RX_VK_FRAMEGEN_REFRESH=1` | (Triforce, 11-21, the upscaler audit's UPS-05; off unless set) The Frame generation row's 2x to 8x make no more frames for each of the game's than the display's refreshes that fall in one frame of the game: the game's recent frame time over the display's period, rounded up, a tenth of a refresh forgiven, never more than the row's number and never fewer than the game's own frame. Without it the row's number is made whatever the display shows: the owner's log has 5x on a 165 Hz display with the game near 66 frames a second, 331 frames a second presented, each present call 2.2 to 3.0 ms (0.03 ms without frame generation) and the display thread's line `0 frames shown on time, 1655 late`. Auto and AMD FSR's row are not touched. A `vk: frame interpolation: N frame(s) for each of the game's` line says each change (`gpu/vk/framegen_refresh.h`, `vk_framegen.cpp` `framegen_record`). Not built or run in the game when it was written |
| `RX_VK_FG_TIMELINE=0` | With the Frame pacing row Off, a fixed frame generation multiplier's frames go out as they come again, all within a millisecond. On unless set to 0: a set with made frames is shown a step apart, each frame at its own time, whatever the row says (with the row on they always were): shown at once on a swapchain that does not wait for a refresh, the game's frame covered the made ones (`gpu/vk/vk_framegen.cpp` `display_main`). Not built or run in the game when it was written |
| `RX_VK_FRAMEGEN=0`, `RX_VK_FRAMEGEN_MULT=<0..9>`, `RX_VK_PACE=0`, `RX_VK_UPSCALE=<0..2>` | No frame generation whatever the menu says; over the options file: the Frame generation row's value (0 Off, 1 Auto, 2 to 8 the multiplier, 9 AMD FSR frame generation: a lab run's pin for each generator; `gpu/vk/framegen_rows.h` `env_row`), frame pacing, the upscaling filter |
| `RX_VK_FG_OWN_MOTION=0` | (Triforce, FG-4) Frame generation's motion the camera's alone, as before, for comparing. Without it, while NVIDIA DLSS or AMD FSR 4 upscale, the depth and motion frame generation takes (AMD's, NVIDIA DLSS frame generation, FSR 3.1's interpolation) are made from the upscaler chain's frame, with what moves by itself drawn into the motion (`gpu/vk/vk_temporal.cpp` `fg_inputs_from_chain`, `gpu/vk/vk_fsr3_inputs.cpp` `chain_motion`): also where the gameplay network does not translate on the card and the upscaler draws it in its place (RDNA 3), where they were never made before and AMD's frame generation made no frame |
| `RX_VK_DLSSG_OWN_MOTION=1` | (Triforce, 11-22) NVIDIA DLSS frame generation is given the upscaler chain's motion, as before (`RX_VK_FG_OWN_MOTION` above). By default it is given the camera's alone, from FSR's inputs: with the chain's the owner's flicker of the whole picture while the camera moved came back on 11-22, with frame generation at 2x and DLSS at 50%, and it stayed with the upscaler audit's commits set back; with `RX_VK_FG_OWN_MOTION=0` it was gone. DLSS frame generation finds what moves by itself with its own optical flow. AMD's frame generation and FSR 3.1's interpolation keep the chain's (`gpu/vk/fg_motion.h`, `vk_fsr3_inputs.cpp` `chain_motion`). Not built when it was written: the same as the run with `RX_VK_FG_OWN_MOTION=0`, for DLSS frame generation alone |
| `RX_VK_DLSSG_CLIP=0` | (Triforce, 11-20) NVIDIA DLSS frame generation is given the game's own camera matrices, as before, for comparing. Without it they are in the clip space of the depth it is given (FSR's copy, 1 at the near plane): the game's clip z runs from -1 at the near plane to 1 at the far one, and with its matrices that depth read as a clip z put what is far next to the camera, which showed as a flicker of the whole picture while the camera moved (`gpu/vk/dlss_math.h` `depth_clip_projection`, `gpu/vk/vk_dlssg.cpp` `frame`) |
| `RX_VK_FG_HUDLESS=0` | (Triforce, FG-2) No picture without the HUD is taken for AMD's frame generation, so its library makes no frame (the game's frame alone): a test. Without it the gameplay upscale's output is taken before the HUD is drawn into it, sRGB-encoded as the game's last pass encodes it, and given as `HUDLessColor` (`gpu/vk/vk_framegen.cpp` `framegen_hudless_note`, `hudless_for`); a present without it shows the game's frame alone; with HDR Native or RenoDX's RGBA16F scene AMD's library is not asked and the renderer's own makes the frame, as at 2x |
| `RX_VK_FRAMEGEN_FINE=0` | Frame generation's own made frames (Auto, 2x to 8x) the old way: a vector per 16x16 pixels in steps of 4 pixels, a blend of four of them per pixel, a pixel alike in both frames taken as still. Without it, a vector per 8x8 pixels to a fraction of a pixel, settled among its neighbours, and one motion per pixel (`gpu/vk/vk_framegen.cpp` `fine_vectors`, `shaders/fg_fine_*.comp`), which keeps moving straight lines straight and thin lines drawn (`tests/test_framegen_lines.py` measures both) |
| `RX_VK_PACE_SMOOTH=0` | (Triforce, on by default since the lab's A/B; `=0` off) The Frame pacing row on the plain present too, without frame generation: with the row on, a picture of the game's that comes sooner than seven eighths of the recent interval after the last one waits for that, its blit on the queue and the queue's lock let go; a late one goes at once, and none waits more than 50 ms (`gpu/vk/present_smooth.h`, after NX2navi's `pace_smooth`). Without it the row spaces only frame generation's frames, as before. With `RX_VK_PRESENT_THREAD=0` the present is on the game's presenting thread, so a wait also holds that thread's next frame: what that does to the rate under the cap is for a measurement before it is on. A `vk: frame pacing` line every 1200 pictures says how many waited and how long |
| `RX_VK_PRESENT_THREAD=1` | (off by default since the lab showed no gain) (Triforce, on by default; `=0` as before) The game's presents go out from a present thread of their own (`gpu/vk/present_thread.h`, after NX2navi's present thread): the presenting thread records and submits the blit as before (the frame fence, the swapchain's image, the overlays, the bug capture's copy and the frame log's: the same commands in the same order on the queue), then hands the pacing's wait (`RX_VK_PACE_SMOOTH`) and the present call to that thread and goes on to its next frame; the next present waits first until the one handed over is out, so one is in hand at most. Not with frame generation, NVIDIA DLSS frame generation or AMD Anti-Lag 2 (presents and stages of their own); NVIDIA Reflex's present markers carry their frame's id. The `vk: a frame's present` line every 120 presents says how many went out from it, the presenting thread's time in the present and how much of it was the wait for the present before, and the pacing's wait and the present call (on whichever thread); the stall line's present is the presenting thread's, with that wait named |
| `RX_VK_ACQUIRE_FENCE=0` | (Triforce, on by default; `=0` as before) The swapchain's image waited for on the CPU, not on the replay's queue (`gpu/vk/vk_replay.cpp` `present_image`, `image_free`): the acquire signals a fence, the present waits for it, at most 250 ms, before it records anything for the image, and the blit's submit waits for nothing. AMD's Windows driver returns from the acquire at once, the image not yet free: with the window covered (Alt+Tab, another window or OBS in front) the blit's wait for the acquire held the queue, every later submit queued behind it, and the game froze for as long as the cover. An image not free in time is held (`vk: swapchain image ... held`): no frame is shown, the game goes on, and the next presents look at it again without waiting until it is back. The wait counts as the pacing's in the present line. With DLSS frame generation the acquire's semaphore as before |
| `RX_VK_SURFACE_RECOVER=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PRESENT-45, after Source A's; on by default) A lost window surface is not handled, as before: after `VK_ERROR_SURFACE_LOST_KHR` (a display driver reset, a monitor change, a remote session) every acquire fails without a word and the picture stays frozen until a restart. Not set, an acquire, a present or a swapchain's creation that answers it lets go of what hangs on the surface, in order: the present thread's present out, the device idle, the overlays' views, the images' semaphores, the swapchain, then the surface (`gpu/vk/vk_main.cpp` `invalidate_lost_surface`); and the next present's `create_swapchain` first makes a surface for the window again with the startup's calls (`open_window_surface`), only while the window has a size (a minimized window waits), one try each 250 ms at most, with the presents' own queue chosen again for it. A loss seen on the present thread is only noted there and handled by the next present. Nothing changes while no surface is lost (every new statement is behind that result or a missing surface, and the startup's own code is as it was), nor while NVIDIA DLSS frame generation runs (the swapchain is Streamline's). `vk: the window's surface is lost` and `... is made again` lines say it. Never seen on a real driver here, and the window's path is not in the scripted (headless) runs: `tests/test_present_surface_lost.py` runs the code against a model of a driver that loses its surface at each call |
| `RX_VK_REFLEX_LIMIT=1` | (Triforce, off by default) With the NVIDIA Reflex row on and no frame generation, Reflex's sleep holds the Game frame rate cap (the sleep mode's minimum interval) where each frame starts, in place of the game's own wait; and with DLSS frame generation, Streamline's Reflex holds it the same way (`gpu/vk/vk_dlssg.cpp` `pace`; options_version 7: the Reflex frame limit row is gone, and this is its only switch until that path has been played on an RTX 40/50 card; `vk: dlssg: frame limit:` lines say who holds it): one wait a frame, timed by the driver (`gpu/vk/vk_reflex.cpp` `reflex_pace`, the rule `gpu/vk/dlssg_pace.h`'s; after NX2navi's `low_latency`). The game's own wait holds a movie, a frame RivaTuner holds, a window that showed nothing for 200 ms, and the rest of the run once over half of 60 frames came sooner than three quarters of the period, or when the driver refuses the interval. `vk: NVIDIA Reflex: frame limit:` lines say who holds it; the Full overlay's Frame limit line says NVIDIA Reflex after the rate. |
| `HOS_PACE_HANDOFF=0` | The game's own wait paces every frame, with DLSS frame generation's Reflex too and whether RivaTuner Statistics Server runs: the renderer is never asked whether another limiter holds a frame, and RivaTuner gets no limit from the game (`host/nn_vi.c` `pace_handed`, `gpu/vk/vk_pace.cpp`) |
| `HOS_MEM_QUERY_USED=0` | (Triforce, 11-19) `nn::os::QueryMemoryInfo` answers the memory in use as the program and the system's part only, as before. By default it is the program, the heap and every page the game mapped beyond it, at most the total, as Horizon answers (`host/hos_mem.c` `query_used`): the game's resource cache sizes what it may still take from it, and without it kept every area it ever loaded (12.5 GB of its 9809 MB after four areas). Needs the census on (`HOS_MEM_CENSUS`). `HOS_MEM_QUERY_SLACK_MB=<MB>` (1024 by default): that much less of the game's pages answered as in use, room for its cache beyond a Switch's (our game needs ~3.2 GB of regions at a save load where a Switch leaves ~2.8; with 0 it evicted in every scene) |
| `HOS_OBJ_CACHE=<n>` | (Triforce) The per-thread cache that finds a guest nn::os object (mutex, event, condition variable ...) by its address (`host/nn_os_sync.c` `obj_lookup_cached`, `tests/test_obj_cache_size.py`): by default 16,384 entries, four ways to a 64-byte set picked by murmur3's finalizer, the oldest way replaced; 256 KB a thread, allocated at its first lookup. The direct-mapped cache missed at nearly every step of the game's walk over some 4,000 mutexes 0x80 apart. n: 256 to 65,536 entries (down to a power of two); `0`: the direct-mapped 4,096 entries, as before. Every 10 s the log's `sync: object cache` line counts the lookups and misses |
| `HOS_NO_OBJ_CACHE=1` | No per-thread object cache at all (either kind): every nn::os call looks its object up in the shared table |
| `HOS_GUEST_MEMFD=0`, `=1` | (Linux) The guest window as a memfd's, its pages handed to the GPU as dma-bufs (udmabuf), where `/dev/udmabuf` can be opened: amdgpu checks every page of a host-pointer import again at each submission, 15-45 ms a submission in Kokiri. (Triforce, 11-21) Also where udmabuf's `size_limit_mb` cannot be read (Flatpak and other sandboxes hide `/sys/module`): its default, 64 MB, is assumed and the log says so; only a limit that reads 0 keeps the window anonymous. The log's `mem: the guest window is a memfd's` line. `=0`: anonymous memory, host-pointer imports; `=1`: the memfd all the same |
| `HOS_MEM_TRACE=1` | (Triforce, lab) Logs every address region and page call of the game with its size and caller (`memtrace:` lines; a region's with its call stack) and every `QueryMemoryInfo` answer |
| `RX_VK_WB_EXCL_CLIP=0` | (Triforce, 11-22; on by default with `RX_VK_CAP_ONLY_GPU=1`) A written binding is copied back to guest memory whole at the submission's end, as in 0.6.0. By default the parts of it in memory the capture leaves out (heap memory between the game's pools inside an imported chunk) are not: the collection does not stamp the CPU's writes there, so the mirror's bytes there are stale, and the write-back diff copied them over the game's newer ones. A pointer table's bare entry (its 64-byte head marked written, `gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp`) did so over a heap block 0x18 past its base: the 0.6.0 crashes at area changes in guest 0x803b44e0 (a block header of 0x9ed00000001 or 0x1aa88000013fe), 0x80a30a00 and 0x80a31030 (`gpu/vk/wb_excl_clip.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `gpu_buffer_bind`; `tests/test_wb_excl_clip.py`). The first 20 clipped write-backs are logged (`vk: write-back of ... not copied back`) |
| `HOS_FPS_STEADY=0`, `=1`, `=2` | (Triforce, off by default: the Frame pacing row's two Steady values, over them) 1 any rate, 2 steps of the display's refresh: the game's frames paced to a period this PC holds in the scene, never shorter than the Game frame rate cap's (`host/pace_steady.h`, `host/nn_vi.c` `steady_period`). The rate is chosen by probes (`pace_steady_probed`): 32 frames at the cap, unpaced, the last 24 of them measured, at the start, 2 s after a load, at least every 12 s, and when a quarter of the last 64 frames ran late (2 s after the last probe at the soonest, twice that each time a probe changes nothing, up to 12 s); the rate becomes the first the last three probes' 90th percentile fits in with 2% to spare where that is slower, or the first it fits in with 5% to spare where that is faster. A frame paced slower does more work (the lab's Market: 11.6 ms unpaced, 15.5 ms paced at 55 fps), so the frames' own measure as they run chased the rate down; frames late now only bring a probe on, and the rate is never under what a probe shows the PC holds. `HOS_FPS_STEADY_PROBE=0`: no probes, the rule below as before. Without probes: what a frame took is its present interval less the longest of the pacing's waits in it (the acquire's, on the presenting thread or another, and the present's hold), and what it needed the interval less all of them (OoT's main thread and Presentation Thread take turns; where the waits add up to the whole interval, the longest: `pace_steady_measure`); a quarter of the last 64 frames that took more than the period lowers it at once to the first rate their 90th percentile fits in with 2% to spare, and once it has held 3 s since a raise (6 s since a lowering, doubled for each lowering within 30 s of a raise, up to 120 s, and back to 6 s at a load) it rises to the fastest rate the 90th percentile of what the last 512 frames needed fits in with 5% to spare (one rate at a time where the game acquires on another thread). A frame that took over 2.5 times the median of the last 64 (hitches among them), a present interval over 250 ms, and the frames of the 2 s after such a load (the scene is not its measure yet) are left out; the first lowering after a load waits for 128 frames of the new scene and takes their 90th percentile. The acquire's wait starts the frames a period apart (the steady step then gives the game the period as its time step) and the present holds each picture of the game's to the period after the last, less a 64th of it (`gpu/vk/present_smooth.h` `present_smooth_paced_at`, with or without the Frame pacing row; not with frame generation). Another limiter holding the frames (RivaTuner, Reflex) puts the cap back while it does. `vi: steady frame rate:` lines at each change say what the frames needed, and every 5 s what the rule was given (what the frames took and needed at p50 and p90, the present interval's p50, the waits a frame by kind, the frames kept and left out by reason, how many of the last 64 kept were late, and the probes and what the last three took unpaced); the `vk: frame pacing` line counts the present's holds |
| `HOS_FPS_STEADY_PROBE=0` | (Triforce, with `HOS_FPS_STEADY` or the Steady frame rate row) No unpaced probes: the rate by the frames' own measure as they run, as before test31 (`host/pace_steady.h` `pace_steady_probed`, `host/nn_vi.c` `steady_period`); the frames' work grows when they are paced slower, and that rule slides down |
| `HOS_PACE_SPIN=0`, `HOS_PACE_SPIN=<microseconds>` | (Triforce) The game's own frame wait on the high-resolution timer for the whole wait, as before, which wakes up to about 0.5 ms after the frame's time. By default the timer takes the wait up to 0.6 ms before the frame's time and the rest is yielded away on the clock, so the frame starts on its time (`host/pace_wait.h`, `host/nn_vi.c` `pace_sleep`); a number is another margin. Every 5 s in which frames waited a `vi: frame waits` line says how long after their time the waits ended, on average and at most |
| `HOS_RTSS=0` | RivaTuner Statistics Server is left out entirely: nothing of it is read or loaded and its profile is not touched; a line `triforce_rtss_profile.txt` holds from a run that did not close is only reported in the log (`gpu/vk/rtss_limit.cpp`, *The options file* below) |
| `HOS_RTSS_DLSSG=1` | With DLSS frame generation RivaTuner is tried as the limiter too, at the cap times the multiplier in effect, with every safeguard (off: the game's own wait or Reflex, `gpu/vk/pace_rule.h` says why) |
| `RX_VK_DEVICE=<n>` | The graphics card listed as `vk: device <n>` in the log, over the menu's GPU device |
| `HOOKSHOT_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1` | No look for a newer release at the start |
| `HOS_SINGLE_INSTANCE=0` | (Triforce) No lock on the game folder: as before, a second copy of the game starts on a folder one already runs from. By default the first holds a lock named after its game folder (`host/hos_instance.h`), taken before the log is opened; a second copy waits up to 5 s for one that is ending (20 s after the menu's Restart), then says in a message box which process holds the folder and ends (a `--prepare-shaders` run or `HOS_HEADLESS`: an `@@error` line, and the prepare ends as one with no Vulkan). Other folders (the lab's copy, the owner's game) never meet. The log's `instance:` line says what this run did |
| `HOS_TRACE=1` | Every Horizon call logged, as `--trace` does |
| `HOS_SAVE_EXPORT_DIR=<folder>` | Where the System tab's Export save rows write their zip, in place of the Downloads folder (set in the environment; `tests/test_save_share.py` uses it) |
| `HOS_SAVE_LOCK=0`, `TRIFORCE_SAVE_LOCK=0` | (Triforce) The save's lock between the game and Setup: the game holds `<user>\save.lock` open without sharing from its first save mount to its end, and Setup's Convert saves holds it across its copy (`host/save_tx.h` `save_tx_lock`, `setup/Guard.cs` `SaveLock`). A running game makes Setup refuse the copy; a game started during one waits for it (30 s at most) before it reads its save. `HOS_SAVE_LOCK=0`: the game takes none; `TRIFORCE_SAVE_LOCK=0`: Setup takes none and only looks at the running programs, as before |
| `RX_BACKEND=<name>` | Unset or `vulkan`: `hookshot_vk.dll`; any other value but `eden`: the WebGPU renderer, when the exe was built with one |

### Input for scripted runs

| Switch | What it does |
|---|---|
| `HOS_INPUT_SCRIPT=<presses>` | Presses timed from the game's first controller read |
| `HOS_INPUT_LIVE=<file>` | A file the test appends lines of presses to, read every 30 ms; each line's times count from when it is read. A line `!cheat <n>` sends a Cheats page action, `!synclog` logs the NVN syncs (`!dump`, `!shots`, `!rdoc` and `!flasharm` are the WebGPU renderer's) |
| `HOS_INPUT_FRAME_CLOCK=1` | The script's time runs at 60 game frames a second instead of the wall clock |
| `HOS_INPUT_EXCLUSIVE`, `HOS_INPUT_BACKGROUND`, `HOS_INPUT_SCRIPT_BACKGROUND` (set) | As above; the last lets every device count while the window is behind another one |
| `HOS_INPUT_RECORD=<file>` | (Triforce; unset: off) An input recording (`host/input_movie.c`, the format in `host/input_movie.h`): what the game is given each game frame, after every device and filter (the controller word and sticks, the flight and free camera cheats' inputs, the gyroscope, the native mouse camera's turns, the menu mouse's choices, the Debug tools' actions and rows), with Link's position and the camera's state as fingerprints. From the frame after Link's first update; `HOS_INPUT_FROM=boot`: from the game's first read. `HOS_INPUT_ROUTE=1`: no guest words or addresses in it (`HOS_INPUT_SAVE=<name>` noted in its header) |
| `HOS_INPUT_PLAY=<file>` | (Triforce; unset: off) A recording given back in place of the player's devices, the script and the bridge, from the same point, then the player's input again; the log says where Link or the camera first left it, and its end how many updates did. With `HOS_INPUT_RECORD` (another file) the replay is recorded too. `HOS_INPUT_SYNC=<n>` (1; 0: never) puts the camera and Link back every n-th update where they left it, while Link is within `HOS_INPUT_SYNC_MAX` metres (1); past that the replay has diverged. Exact only at the 60 cap with `HOS_REALTIME_STEP=0`, `HOS_FIXED_TIME`, the same save and settings. A run that records or plays from its start has no movie pacing (`host/nn_vi.c`). The control bridge starts and stops either at run time (`triforce_ctl.py record` / `play`) |
| `RX_VK_MENU_SCRIPT=<seconds>:<key>,...` | Timed Insert menu keys: `INS`, `UP`, `DOWN`, `LEFT`, `RIGHT`, `ESC`, `ENTER`, `TABL`, `TABR` |
| `RX_VK_UI_PROMPTS=pad` or `keys` | The menu's button prompts for a controller or the keys, whatever was used last (screenshots) |
| `RX_VK_UI_GAME_FONT=0` | The menu, the counter and the start-up screen in the included Libertinus, not the game's own font |

The presses are `<seconds>:<button>[+<button>...][/<ms>]`, separated by commas, for example `2:A,5.5:LUP/1500,9:ZL+R`.
`MX=<counts>` and `MY=<counts>` in a press move the mouse that many counts each controller read while it is held, for
the native mouse camera (`3:MX=40/500`).
The buttons are `A B X Y L R ZL ZR PLUS MINUS LS RS LEFT UP RIGHT DOWN`, and the sticks pushed to their edge `LUP
LDOWN LLEFT LRIGHT RUP RDOWN RLEFT RRIGHT`. A press is held for 200 ms unless `/<ms>` says otherwise. The names are
case-sensitive, and an unknown one is ignored.

### Pictures and frames

| Switch | What it does |
|---|---|
| `RX_VK_SHOTS=<dir>` | A PNG every `RX_VK_SHOT_EVERY` presents (300) as `vk_<present>.png`, at `RX_VK_SHOT_W` x `RX_VK_SHOT_H` (1280x720) |
| `RX_VK_FLASH=1` | The flash detector, with `RX_VK_SHOTS` and `RX_VK_SHOT_EVERY=1`: writes the frames around a flash and dumps the next one |
| `RX_VK_FRAMELOG=<file>` | Every frame shown, at 320x180, while `<file>.go` exists; `python -I scripts/framelog.py <file>` finds the flashes |
| `RX_VK_DUMP=<present>[+<count>][,...]` | Every step of the frame after that present as PNGs, in `dump_<present>` under `RX_VK_DUMP_DIR` (or `RX_VK_SHOTS`, or the current folder); `RX_VK_DUMP_STEPS=<lo>-<hi>` and `RX_VK_DUMP_PROGS=<key>,...` narrow it |
| `RX_VK_PASS_TRACE=1` | The passes of the last frames, logged at a flash or a dark frame (`RX_VK_PASS_TRACE_AT=<n>`: every n-th present) |
| `RX_VK_PROBE=1` or `2` | The centre texel after every pass: `probe_<frame>.txt` at a flash (1), or `probe_all_<n>.txt` at speed (2); `python -I scripts/probe.py` reads them |
| `RX_VK_DUMP_FAILED=<dir>` | The native code of every program that does not translate, for `nx2/lower_test.py` (game data: keep it local) |

### Time and profiles

| Switch | What it does |
|---|---|
| `HOS_PROFILE=<seconds>` | Every interval, the busiest threads in the log; it also starts the samplers below |
| `HOS_SAMPLE=<thread>` + `HOS_SAMPLE_FILE=<file>` | One thread's stacks, sampled every millisecond (needs `HOS_PROFILE`); `python -I scripts/prof.py <file>` reads them |
| `HOS_CHAIN=<file>` | (Triforce, diagnostic, off unless set) While `<file>.go` exists, every guest wait that blocks (events, semaphores, message queues, mutexes, condition variables, multi-waits, the kernel's waits, a virtual core), every signal that can end one, every guest sleep and every present is recorded with the time, the thread, the object and the guest caller (`host/hos_chain.c`, about 2 million records); when `<file>.go` goes away they go to `<file>`. `scripts/chain.py <file> [<HOS_SAMPLE_ALL file>]` walks each frame back from its present (the thread that ran, the wait that stopped it, the signal that ended it, and on) and prints how much of the frame each thread's running, the wake-ups and the sleeps take; with `HOS_SAMPLE_ALL` beside it (Linux: each sample's own time), the functions on that path. Off: one load and a branch a wait |
| `HOS_SAMPLE_ALL=<file>` | Every game and renderer thread every 2 ms while `<file>.go` exists (needs `HOS_PROFILE`); `scripts/crit.py`, `scripts/vkprof.py` and `scripts/hotlist.py` read it (on Linux with `RX_MAPS=<game folder>`: they name frames from its `hookshot` and `hookshot_vk.so`) |
| `HOS_THREAD_TIMES=<seconds>` | Every interval, the threads that used over 5% of a core |
| `HOS_FPS_LOG` (set), `HOS_PRESENT_TRACE=<csv>` | The game's frame limiter, VRR flags and nominal rate every 300 presents; a CSV line per game present |
| `RX_VK_FRAME_LOG=<csv>` | A CSV line per present: frame, seconds, frame time in ms |
| `RX_VK_FRAME_TRACE=1` | A line a frame in `triforce_frames.csv` beside the game (the lab's scenarios keep it as `frames.csv`): present to present, the waits for shaders and pipelines, the uploads, and the replay's parts (`memsync_ms` ... `tex_lookup_ms`: the draws' parts are sampled and summed over every thread that records). (Triforce, wp20, `gpu/vk/frame_trace.h`) Then each frame split as the stall line splits a long one: `replay_ms` (the submits and the present phase), `present_ms`, `dispatch_ms`, `qsubmit_ms`, `par_wait_ms` (the replay thread's waits for its helpers' segments), `outside_ms` (the frame less the replay and the present: the game's own update), `game_wait_ms` (the game's fence waits), `captures_ms` (the captures at its submits), `wb_wait_ms` and `wb_held_ms` (the waits for write-backs, and their part after the writer's recording was done: `RX_VK_WB_WAIT_REC`), `qs_bg_ms` (the queue submits beside a background import), `gpu_waits_ms` ((Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's SYNC-27) every thread's time in calls that block on the GPU, `RX_VK_GPU_WAITS` below). `python -I scripts/frame_rank.py <run folder or csv>` ranks what made the frames over the 99th percentile and over 1.5x the median by these, per window (the Market, the tour's stays, the loads) |
| `RX_VK_SETTLE_GATE=1`, `=2` | (Triforce, 11-22, off by default; the performance audit's SYNC-29, after Source A's; `gpu/vk/settle_gate.h`) A CPU read that lets the stopped queues whose waits are met go on (the readback's look, which `RX_VK_RB_SETTLE=1` turns on, and the two looks made for a range a texture may be in) takes the order's lock whenever a queue is stopped, and that lock is held through whole replays and presents. With the gate a site looks only while a queue is stopped **and** the CPU has signalled an event since the last finished look, or a stopped queue waits on an event whose value is read from guest memory; any other met wait was met by a signal in the stream, and the thread that replayed it looked before it let the lock go. `1`: the readback's look; `2`: the texture sites' too (new ground: those looks order the exposure meter's readback, so `2` needs the lab's flicker count). Unset or `0`: every look as before. The `RX_VK_LAB_AB` knobs `settlegate` and `texgate` are `1` and `2` in their phases (`rbsettle+settlegate` is the pair the audit asks for). The 3000-readback line and the texture catch-ups' 300-call line count the looks made and skipped |
| `RX_VK_READBACK_LATE=<rule>[,<rule>]`, `RX_VK_LOG_READBACK_RANGES=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's SYNC-19, after Source A's; `gpu/vk/readback_late.h`) Ranges the CPU reads back **without** the wait for their write-back, from what guest memory holds (the last write-back made): a rule is a size in hex (`3980`: every range of 0x3980 bytes, the culling camera block Source A reads late by default) or `newest` (a range whose writer is the newest submission handed out: the game cannot have waited for it). **Unset: no rule**, every readback waits as before, until the lab has run it where the waits are long (a handheld, an AMD iGPU, a weak discrete card); `=0`: none either. The `RX_VK_LAB_AB` knob `rblate` is `3980` in its phases. A late read hands the game the frame before's values, and when the CPU resets a range read late the pending write-back lands over its reset (the exposure meter's flashes): `newest` can catch that meter, so it is never a default. `RX_VK_LOG_READBACK_RANGES=1`: with every 3000-readback line, the 12 ranges that waited longest (readbacks, waits, ms, how often their writer was the newest submission, how often read late), to find such ranges on other hardware; nothing is noted without it |
| `RX_VK_WB_WAIT_REC=1`, `=2` | (Triforce, off by default; the handoff's §12.9, `gpu/vk/wb_rec_wait.h`) A wait for a write-back still on the GPU (a capture's late pages on the game's submitting thread, a read or binding of a page put off) ends once the recording its submission was finished in is done on the GPU, not when the submissions' timeline reaches it: that timeline is held below any submission stopped at an event wait, and the lab's Market had 20-46 ms waits of the game's thread with the GPU 4 submissions behind. Whatever the switch, the wait for the recording comes first and the rest is counted: the stall line's `N of them X ms after their writer's recording was done`, the `late:` line's `ms of the wait after the recording that finished it was done`, the frame trace's `wb_held_ms`. Off: the captures' late-page wait orders something not known yet (`RX_VK_LATE_NEW`), so it is tried with the lab's flicker count first. (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's SYNC-122, after Source B's) The reads' side: a readback whose write-back is not recorded yet waited on the submissions' timeline with no bound and no second look; it is counted now (the 3000-readback line's second line), and the stall line names the held part by caller (takes, reads, binds, readbacks). The variable is a mode: `1` as above, and such a readback waits in 1 ms slices, looks its writer's recording up between them and ends with it; `2` is the check, where no wait ends earlier and the readback's slices count the part after the recording was done (what `1` would not have waited); unset, `0` or any other text: off, one wait as before. The check of the pages a later write-back changes (the audit's SYNC-101) is not built. `RX_VK_TL_REC=1` and the lab knob `tlrec` ended a capture's wait at a submission's first part, before its burst's write-backs were recorded: they now end it at the recording the submission was finished in, as `=1` here does; `RX_VK_TL_REC=2` keeps the old lookup |
| `RX_VK_LATE_QUEUE=1` or `0` | (Triforce, 11-20; 11-23) A capture's late pages (pages a submission still on the GPU writes back: OoT's exposure meter and the per-frame pools) waited for at the queue's next submit, not at the capture's take (`gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `late_queue_on`). Unset: at the queue, except while the replay's submits are held (`RX_VK_BATCH`, on by default with Mesa's RADV): there a held submit applies no fix, the fixes wait for the batch's flush and copy guest memory the game has written again for its next frame, and the replay reads those slots live meanwhile: the lighting and exposure flicker on Linux AMD from 50-90 s into play in v0.7.0. `1`: at the queue with held submits too (v0.7.0 as shipped); `0`: at the take on every driver, as before 11-20. The log says `vk: late pages' wait ...` when it is first asked and when the answer changes |
| `RX_VK_LATE_INLINE=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's MEM-80, after Source B's; `gpu/vk/late_inline.h`; **off unless set**, not yet run in the game) A late page (one the CPU wrote while a submission still on the GPU writes it back) is waited for and taken again by default (`RX_VK_LATE_QUEUE`: at the queue's next submit). `RX_VK_LATE_NEW=1` (knob `latenew`) turns four no-wait rules on, each of which brought brightness steps back at the Castle Town save. This turns on the partial rule alone (knob `lateinline`): a page only part of which a submission on the GPU writes back has its other parts synced from the capture, no wait; pages of pools with no mirror, texture storage, pages written back whole and pages whose write-backs' ranges are not known are waited for as by default. The partial rule, here and under `RX_VK_LATE_NEW`, now asks which ranges are still on the GPU against the progress the capture read before its copies, not a second read at the take: a write-back done between the two counts as in flight, and its range is not synced from the older copy. `RX_VK_LOG_LATE` shows the pages waited for |
| `RX_VK_PASS_TIMING=1` or `2` | GPU time per pass (2: per program too), logged every 600 presents |
| `RX_VK_SLOW_LOG=<ms>` | The replay's times for each frame that took longer than this |
| `RX_VK_STALL_LOG=<ms>` or `0` | On in every run (`gpu/vk/stall_watch.h`): every 600 of the game's frames a `vk: frame times` line (the longest frame and how many were over 33, 50, 100 and 250 ms, present to present and in the replay's own time), and for each frame of 50 ms or more a `vk: stall` line of where its time went: the replay's phases, memory sync's parts, the waits for write-backs still on the GPU (how far ahead, and whether behind a present's blit), the waits for shaders or pipelines, the uploads, the pools imported where they were bound, the GPU behind, the present (its image, fence, call and Reflex's sleep), the game's fence waits, the rest, and a pool's trial in guest memory if one is under way (`RX_VK_DEMOTE_QUICK`); and the new textures left for the next frame and whether the frame's picture was held (`RX_VK_HOLD_MS`), whose holds the 600-frame line counts too. At most 3 stall lines a second and 1500 a session. `<ms>`: the stall line's threshold; `0`: neither line |
| `RX_VK_LOCK_CENSUS=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's THREAD-68, after Source B's; off unless set) The lock census: who holds the lock a thread waits for. A lock that is busy when a thread comes for it notes the waiter, the thread that held it and how long the wait was, and every 10 s a thread of the census's own logs the 12 pairs that waited longest, as `vk: lock census: <waiter> <- <holder> (<lock>) x ms/s (n/s, y us each)`. Counted: `replay_mu`, `sync_order_mu`, `held_mu`, and in `vk_memory.cpp` `pools_mu`, `sync_mu` and `cap_mu`. Threads are named by their descriptions (the game's threads and the renderer's own have them). Off, each lock is taken exactly as before; on, a free lock is one try and a store, and only a busy one reads the clock. The holder is read without the lock, so one that lets go at that moment can be misnamed. With `RX_VK_LAB_AB` naming a knob `lockcensus` it counts only in that knob's phases. Only the counting of Source B's is here, not its sampling of the holders' stacks (`gpu/vk/lock_census.h`) |
| `HOS_STALL_HOST=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's OS-51, after Source B's; on whenever the stall line is) The stall line names a long frame's time on the replay's side and calls the rest outside the replay and the present. A present that comes `RX_VK_STALL_LOG`'s threshold (50 ms) or more after the last one also logs the host's part of that frame, a `vi: stall, the host's part` line: the game's file reads, writes, flushes and save commits (how many, MB, their time and the longest one), the discards, zero-fills and decommits of memory it let go and the give-back's passes, the waits for a virtual core summed over the game's threads, the process's page faults (soft and hard together) and its CPU time in the kernel and in its own code. Causes that did not happen are left out; `none of the host's causes` rules them out. At most 3 lines a second and 1500 in a session, as the stall line. It costs a clock read before and after each counted call and two system calls a present. `0`: none of it; so with `RX_VK_STALL_LOG=0` (`host/stall_host.h`, `host/mkw_glue.c` `hos_stall_host_presented`) |
| `RX_VK_SLOW_TAKE_MS=<ms>` or `0` | (Triforce, 20 by default) A submit-time capture take that long or longer (`gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `take_capture`): a `vk: slow capture take` line with where its time went (the marks and late pages, the pools lock, the retires, the stamps, the texture shadow, the buffer kept or given back), the first 200 of them. A Linux RTX 4090 near the Market stuttered with `memory sync [capture taken 30 to 110 ms]` while the 600-take average said 1.3 ms. `0`: none |
| `HOS_PGO=<file>` | In a run of an `RX_PGO=gen` build: `<file>.reset` clears the counters, `<file>.dump` writes them to `<file>`. It is read before `hookshot_env.txt`, so set it in the environment |

### The GPU

| Switch | What it does |
|---|---|
| `RX_VK_VALIDATION` (set) | The Khronos validation layer |
| `RX_VK_CHECKPOINTS=0` | No NVIDIA checkpoints (on by default; after a lost device they name the draw it stopped at). (Triforce) The boundaries between a frame's work have markers too: the write-back diff pass, each recording's start, uploads and end, the present's blit and its menu (`gpu/vk/checkpoint_kinds.h`); the report reads the two stages' last markers together, says what followed the last one reached, and logs the switches, the queue's timeline and the mirrors' piece binds the submits wait for. (P4) Each marking thread numbers its own markers in a ring of its own (15 of 4096; threads after the 15th share a 16th), no atomic shared by the recording threads; the report names a marker's ring and reads the markers around it in that ring |
| `RX_VK_HANG_DUMP=<file>` or `0` | The hang watch (Windows) is on in every run: once no frame was shown for `RX_VK_HANG_SEC` seconds (20) with the game's window in front, the top of every thread's stack goes into the log and the whole stacks into `triforce_hang.txt` in the game folder, once a stall and at most three times a run. `<file>`: that file instead, and every second counts whatever window is in front. `0`: no watch (Triforce) The same report can be asked for at any time: put a file named `triforce_hang.txt.now` in the game folder (the report's name with `.now`); within a second the watch takes it away and writes every thread's stack, for a game that goes on showing frames and gets nowhere (a loading screen that never ends). |
| `RX_VK_NOOPT` (set) | Pipelines built without the driver's optimisations |
| `RX_VK_MESH_OPT=1` or `=0` | (Triforce) Task and mesh pipelines on AMD. Unset: built without the driver's optimizer on AMD's own drivers (it hung the GPU minutes into Kokiri on an RX 6600) and with it on RADV (without it, the foliage culling's task programs timed out the GPU's ring at the save load on a Steam Deck); the driver's part is made once more on RADV with mesh shaders. `=1`: with the optimizer everywhere. `=0`: without it on every AMD driver, RADV too, as before |
| `RX_VK_GPU_CLOCK=0`, `=peak`, `=standard` | (Triforce, Linux) The Steam Deck's GPU held at amdgpu's peak stable power state while the game runs (`gpu/vk/vk_gpu_clock.cpp`): the Deck's automatic clock dropped while the frame waited on the CPU or a readback, and the GPU work after it ran slow (Kokiri handheld on a Deck OLED: 11.1 to 16.2 fps at the same 15 W; up to 4 W more where the APU was under its cap, so shorter battery life). Unset: only on the Deck's APU, and only while `power_dpm_force_performance_level` is `auto` (a level set by hand is left alone). The render node VK_EXT_physical_device_drm names is opened and the state set on an amdgpu context of the renderer's own (no root); the kernel gives the level back at the process's end, a crash too. The log's `vk: GPU clock:` line says what was done (AMD only). `=0`: nothing opened; `=peak` (or `=1`), `=standard`: that state on any AMD GPU, whatever the level |
| `HOS_HALF_RATE_SHADING=1` | (Triforce, Linux) Keeps `RADV_FORCE_VRS_CONFIG_FILE`, which Steam's Game Mode gives every game (its Half Rate Shading). By default it is dropped before Vulkan loads when it names a file that exists: while it does, RADV on RDNA 2 and newer puts a forced shading rate into every pipeline's key, whatever the file holds, so the pipelines prepared in Desktop Mode or by the builder all compiled again (over 25 minutes to the title on a Deck). The slider then does nothing for this game, little loss: RADV keeps fragment programs that read their position at full rate, and nearly all of this game's do. `RADV_FORCE_VRS` is left alone. The log's boot: line says what was done. Can be set in `triforce_env.txt` |
| `RX_VK_COMPUTE_PIN=all`, `lanes` or `shared` | (Triforce, the performance audit's PIPE-52, after Source A's and Source B's) Which compute programs' pipelines ask for subgroups of 32 lanes (`gpu/vk/compute_pin.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `pin_compute`). `all`, the default on every driver but Mesa's Intel one: every compute program, as before. `lanes`, the default on Mesa's Intel driver: only the programs that can tell how many lanes run beside them (a vote, a lane mask, the lane's index, a shuffle, FSWZADD, a matrix multiply, a barrier of Subgroup scope in the module, or shared memory in a workgroup of 32 invocations or fewer); the rest get the driver's choice, which Mesa's Intel compiler builds in a fraction of the time and memory (Source A, an Iris Xe: 0.8-1 GB over 23-25 s pinned, at most 300 MB unpinned). `shared`: as `lanes`, and any shared memory pins, whatever the workgroup. The log has `vk: compute programs held to 32 lanes: ...` where it is not `all` by default, and the pinned and free counts after the precompile. An unpinned program that did need 32 lanes computes wrongly: `=all` is the old pipelines. Not run on an Intel GPU here |
| `RX_VK_FALLBACK_PHI_LIMIT=<n>` | (Triforce, the performance audit's PIPE-56, after Source A's) On Mesa's RADV for GCN 1 to 4 GPUs (HD 7000 to RX 500: RADV without 16-bit storage input and output) and on Mesa's Intel driver only: a mesh-fallback program with a compute stage of more than `<n>` `OpPhi` instructions is not handed to the driver; it is left out as a program the driver refused is, with a log line (8 at most), and its draws are not drawn (`gpu/vk/fallback_size_policy.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `fallback_compute_pipelines`). Unset: 50,000 on GCN (Source A: RADV for GFX6-8 had not built a 79,947-phi stage after 45-90 s and was killed at 25 GB), 20,000 on Mesa's Intel driver. `0`: no limit. No other driver has a limit, whatever is set. Not run on either driver here |
| `RX_VK_FALLBACK_CALL_LIMIT=<n>` | (Triforce, PIPE-56) The same for a stage's `OpFunctionCall` instructions. Unset: 200 on Mesa's Intel driver (Source A, an Iris Xe: a 251-call stage unfinished after 27 minutes at 14 GB), none on RADV for GCN. `0`: no limit |
| `RX_VK_NO_SPILLING_RATE=1` | (Triforce, the performance audit's PIPE-55, after Source A's; Linux build only) The Linux build sets `shader_spilling_rate=2` in its environment before the Vulkan instance is made (`gpu/vk/mesa_spilling.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_main.cpp` `create_instance`), unless the player has set that variable. Only Mesa's Intel shader compiler reads it: a program whose registers do not fit is built in fewer, larger steps (Source A, an Iris Xe: one compute program not built after 20 minutes and 13 GB, 32 s and 1.7 GB at rate 2). `=1` sets nothing, so the driver keeps its own rate. On Mesa's Intel driver the log has `vk: Mesa's Intel compiler spills at rate ...`. The Windows build sets nothing: under Proton, add `shader_spilling_rate=2` to the launch options. Not run on an Intel GPU here |
| `RX_VK_DRIVER_FAULTS=0` or `2` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PIPE-57, after Source A's; `gpu/vk/driver_faults.h`) On AMD's own Windows driver only (`VK_DRIVER_ID_AMD_PROPRIETARY`), unless `0`: a program whose pipeline build faulted inside the driver is not asked of the driver again this run, and its key is appended to `triforce_driver_faults.txt` beside the executable (one line: vendor, driver version, program) by the fault's handler, before a fault during the start's shader work restarts the game, so the next start with that driver skips it instead of faulting again (`vk: driver faults: N programs ... kept out of it`; delete the file to try them again, another driver version ignores its lines). And one build of a program is inside the driver at a time: a second waits outside it, a probe of the caches is told a compile is required. `2`: on every driver (the lab's); `0`: nowhere. On other drivers nothing of it runs (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `fault_quarantine`, `build_graphics`, `guarded_graphics`) |
| `RX_VK_PASS_BARRIER=game` or `full` | The barrier a draw pass starts with (`gpu/vk/pass_barrier.h`). `game`: a pass right after another, with nothing recorded between (no barrier of the game's, no copy, clear, uniform update or dispatch), waits for the earlier work in its fragment stages only, so its vertex, task and mesh work may overlap the earlier pass's fragments; a pass the replay itself split off for a hazard (`a pass of its own` in the log) and `RX_VK_BARRIER_EVERY_DRAW`'s keep a full one. (Triforce, P4) "Nothing recorded between" is the command buffer's own count (`RX_VK_BARRIER_CONTEXT`), so beside the Replay helper threads too. `full`: a full barrier at every pass start, as before. Unset: `game` with NVIDIA's driver, `full` on every other card. The log's `vk: pass barriers:` line says which, and the frame statistics' `pass starts ordered in their fragment stages only` how many a frame |
| `RX_VK_CHUNK=<n>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's SYNC-66, after Source B's; `gpu/vk/barrier_rules.h` `part_draws`) How many draws and dispatches a part of a long submission has in a serial replay: the GPU starts on the first part while the rest is recorded, and each split costs a queue submit, two full barriers and the part's bookkeeping (about 27 a frame at 120). Unset: the driver's default, **120 on every driver** until the lab has measured one (Source B cuts at 128 where the GPU idles while the CPU records and at 4096 on desktop drivers); `0`: whole submissions, never split; `120`: today's size whatever a later default says. The size was read once into a static: now the `RX_VK_LAB_AB` knob `chunk:<n>` changes it in its phases while the variable is unset (`RX_VK_LAB_AB=chunk:480,chunk:240` with `RX_VK_PARALLEL=0`). The log's first `vk: a submission's parts:` line says the size in force and the driver's default |
| `RX_VK_SPLIT_BARRIER=pass` or `full` | (Triforce) A long submission goes to the GPU in parts of 120 draws and dispatches (`RX_VK_CHUNK`); `gpu/vk/barrier_rules.h`. `pass`: a pass open at a part's split goes on in the next part, begun again with the same targets behind a barrier of its attachments' outputs alone and with what its draws sampled and wrote, so the GPU does not drain between two draws of one of the game's passes; where the part's end records anything (its uniform updates, its write-backs) or an event's write is held, the full barriers as before. A parallel replay's segments are not split into parts: each begins and ends with a full barrier, so nothing goes on across a segment's edge. `full`: a full barrier at each part's end and the next one's start, as before. Unset: `full` on every card ((Triforce, 11-22, SYNC-66) this row said `pass` with NVIDIA's driver; `pass` has been off unless set since 11-16, when the lab's Market showed no gain from it: `barrier_rules.h` `split_in_pass`). The log's `vk: a submission's parts:` lines say which and, every 3,600 splits, how many went on in their pass and how many of those parts had uploads (whose own full barriers order them after the part before all the same) |
| `RX_VK_GPU_WAITS=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's SYNC-27; on by default; not yet run in the game) The host's calls that block on the GPU are made without being timed, as before. By default each is timed where it is wrapped (`gpu/vk/vk_work.h`, `gpu/vk/gpu_wait_kinds.h`) and summed by kind: `vkWaitForFences` and `vkWaitSemaphores` with a timeout above 0 (fences, timelines), `vkQueueWaitIdle` and `vkDeviceWaitIdle` (idle), `vkGetQueryPoolResults` with the wait bit (queries). Polls count nothing, a wait inside a wait counts once, and the low-latency sleep's wait is left out as pacing. After each 120-present line the log has `vk: host waits for the GPU: ... ms a frame (fences, timelines, idle, queries; all threads)`, which counts what that line's "GPU-bound wait" (the recording slots' waits alone) does not, and the frame trace has `gpu_waits_ms`. The cost is two clock reads a blocking wait. The `RX_VK_LAB_AB` knob `nogpuwaits` turns the count off in its phases |
| `RX_VK_ORDER_WAKE=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's SYNC-59; off unless set; not yet run in the game) A queue stopped at a wait for an event goes on when a submit finds the wait met. A signal of the CPU's (`nvnEventSignal`) wakes no one: the stop waits for the order thread's next `Sleep(1)`, or a game thread's wait yields up to 3 ms and then forces it. With the switch (`gpu/vk/vk_replay.cpp` `sync_watch`, `event_signal_queued`) the order thread waits on an auto-reset event, 1 ms at most, which the signal sets while a queue is stopped: one `SetEvent`, no lock and no replay on the signalling thread. And a game thread's wait for stopped work that sees a signal pending lets the queues whose waits are met go on first, as `sync_settle` does; nothing is forced. The order line counts the wakes and those that let work on: under about one a second, leave it off. The `RX_VK_LAB_AB` knob `orderwake` turns it on in its phases |
| `RX_VK_RELEASE_QUEUE=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's MEM-85, after Source B's idea; `gpu/vk/release_queue.h`; **off unless set**, not yet run in the game) Without the replay thread (`RX_VK_ASYNC` unset), a texture or program the game finalizes is let go by its own thread: held for the replaying thread only while a queue is stopped at a wait (PR 430), otherwise after `sync_drain` and the replay's lock, which a replay holds through whole submissions. With this on, every such release goes to a list the replaying thread applies at the start of the next submission's replay or present when no queue is stopped (after the stopped work otherwise, as PR 430's); a texture's release counts the game's writes from the write generation of its call, so writes after the call are still uploaded. The `RX_VK_ASYNC` job path is not changed. The `RX_VK_LAB_AB` knob `relq` turns it on in its phases. On in every run, a count: every 600 releases a `vk: releases` line when one of them waited 0.5 ms or more for the replay or its order (how many, the time in all, the longest) or went to the list. Not built: the entry's `rel_wait_ms` column in frames.csv |
| `RX_VK_UB_BATCH=1` or `2` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's SYNC-21; off unless set; not yet run in the game) A recording's uniform updates are written into guest memory at its end between two full barriers, one `vkCmdUpdateBuffer` an update (about 80 a frame) and nothing between two updates of the same bytes. `=1` (`gpu/vk/ub_batch.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_replay.cpp` `flush_ub_batched`): each update's bytes go into the recording's stream (256 bytes at least) and each run of updates into one buffer is one `vkCmdCopyBuffer` of many regions; a run ends where an update would write bytes the run already wrote, or where the destination buffer changes, and the next starts behind a full barrier, so overlapping updates reach memory in the stream's order. A piece that is not 4-byte aligned becomes a region too (it was a CPU write at record time); with the stream full a piece is written as before. `=2`: written as before, the overlaps counted (by guest address). Both log a line every 600 flushes: updates, runs ended by an overlap and by another buffer, copies and regions. Look at the counts first: a barrier at every run end costs GPU time if they are common. The `RX_VK_LAB_AB` knob `ubbatch` is `=1` in its phases |
| `RX_VK_SPLIT_BEFORE_DRAW=0` or `1` | (Triforce, the performance audit's REPLAY-14, after Source A's) A part of a long submission (and a helper thread's segment) ends only right before a plain draw that will draw (`NOP_DRAW`, `_IDX`, `_INDIRECT`; rendering on, no rasterizer discard), not after any draw or dispatch (`gpu/vk/barrier_rules.h` `split_before_draw`, `cut_ok`; `gpu/vk/vk_replay.cpp`). On by default on Intel's GPUs only: on an Arc B580 a command buffer that begins with mesh draws drew its later plain draws as exploded triangles (Source A: 40 shard frames of 1,121, then 0 of 1,101). `=0` off, `=1` on with any GPU. A run of mesh draws or dispatches is then not cut, so a part can be longer than `RX_VK_CHUNK`. Not run on an Intel GPU here |
| `RX_VK_SPLIT_UBFLUSH=0` or `1` | (Triforce, REPLAY-14) Whether a part's end writes the submission's uniform updates so far into the game's memory (`split_ub_flush`). On by default, as before, except on Intel's GPUs without mirrors of the game's pools (integrated ones), where only the submission's last part writes them, as an unsplit submission does: written at each part's end they reach the memory later parts' other reads and the game's CPU see (Source A: white blocks in 1 run of 3 on a Meteor Lake iGPU, then 0 of 3). `=0` off, `=1` on with any GPU. The log's `vk: a submission's parts: ended only right before a plain draw ...` line says both |
| `RX_VK_EVENT_BARRIER=late` or `full` | (Triforce) An event signal in the game's command stream (`gpu/vk/barrier_rules.h`). `late`: nothing is recorded at the signal and its pass goes on (the word's buffer is bound there, as before); the event's word is written right after the next full barrier on its command buffer (the game's next barrier, a dispatch's or pass start's), so after all the work before the signal, and a recording's end writes what is still held behind a full barrier before its write-backs copy the words to the game's memory; a wait the replay finds met for an event whose write is held gets a full barrier first. The game sees the word at that barrier rather than at the signal. `full`: the pass ended, a full barrier and the write at each signal, as before. Unset: with the pass barriers' game mode (`RX_VK_PASS_BARRIER`: NVIDIA's driver). Not with the parallel replay. The `vk: event signals:` line says which |
| `RX_VK_TAIL_EARLY=1` or `=0` | (Triforce, on by default since options_version 6; over the Debug tab's Start next frame early row, `tail_early=0` to turn it off; read at the start; after NX2navi's `tail_early`) The early tail (`gpu/vk/tail_early.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_replay.cpp`): the replay cuts a frame's recording at the gameplay upscale's dispatch (the game's network, or DLSS, DLAA, FSR in its place) and records the rest, the upscaler and the interface, as one command buffer; its event signals are held. At the recording's end a tail that wrote no binding (its inline uniform updates aside), bound no guest memory itself, ran no program of the game's that reaches guest memory through the page table or a texel buffer, and wrote no texture back has its event words written in its upload command buffer, after the scene's write-backs and ahead of its own commands: OoT's present poll then sees its frame's event while the GPU still runs the tail. Any other tail has the words written at its end, as before. Also with the parallel replay (its segments end at that dispatch) and with the lazy write-back. Not with `RX_VK_BENCH` or a frame dump. A `vk: the early tail` line at the start says on, and every 1200 tails how many went early and why the others did not. Not yet run in the game |
