| `RX_VK_DISPATCH_BARRIER=game` | (Triforce) Off unless set, not yet run in the game: a dispatch right after another, nothing recorded between, records no full barrier of its own (not an indirect one, nor a program a stand-in may take over), as NX2navi's `barrier_game` does; the game's own barriers order its dispatches. Only the game shows whether that is always so (a one-frame band or flicker where it is not): a switch for the lab. A `vk: RX_VK_DISPATCH_BARRIER=game:` line every 20,000 dispatches it left without one. (wp14, P4) It no longer stands aside while the Replay helper threads record (most frames at the Market, so the lab's earlier run of it measured little): "nothing recorded between" is the command buffer's own count of its commands (`gpu/vk/cmd_work.h`, `RX_VK_BARRIER_CONTEXT`), a segment being a command buffer of its own |
| `RX_VK_BARRIER_CONTEXT=0` | (Triforce, P4; after NX2navi's per-context barrier state) What the barrier rules ask, "was anything recorded into this command buffer since its last full barrier, its last pass's end, its last dispatch", is answered by each command buffer's own count of its commands (`gpu/vk/cmd_work.h`), where one count of every thread's commands served before (an atomic add on every command by every recording thread). So the rules hold while the Replay helper threads record: the left-out repeats of a full barrier, `RX_VK_PASS_BARRIER`'s pass starts and `RX_VK_DISPATCH_BARRIER`. A command buffer's begin starts its state afresh; each segment begins and ends with a full barrier, so no rule relaxes across a segment's edge. `RX_VK_SPLIT_BARRIER` asks also whether anything went to the queue between two parts (every submit is counted). The rules' defaults are as they were. `0`: the one count, and the rules standing aside while the helper threads record, as before. The `vk: barrier state:` line at the start says which; the frame statistics' `full barriers recorded, ... left out` and `pass starts ordered in their fragment stages only` show the difference |
| `RX_VK_BATCH_IDLE=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, off by default; the performance audit's SYNC-09, after Source A's; `gpu/vk/barrier_rules.h` `batch_idle`) With held submits (`RX_VK_BATCH`, the row below: on by default with Mesa's RADV only), a submit that joins the held batch sends it at once when the GPU has finished every batch sent; while the GPU is busy, submits still group. A held batch otherwise waits for a wait, a present, the 2 ms poll or the cap of 32 with the GPU idle beside it. Source A has this on wherever batching is, and off where guest memory stayed host-pointer imports (each submit there pays amdgpu's page check); here it is **off unless set on every driver** until a Linux RADV machine has run it, and no guard for host-pointer imports is built. Nothing changes without held submits (NVIDIA, AMD's and Intel's Windows drivers). The 120-present `vk: batched submits:` line counts its flushes as `idle` |
| `RX_VK_BATCH=parts`, `1` or `0` | Held submits (`gpu/vk/vk_replay.cpp` `batch_way`). `1` (the default with Mesa's RADV): every submit held until something needs the GPU's results or the queue's order. (Triforce) `parts`, off unless set: only a whole submission's recordings are held, and only while the game's submit is being replayed; a part goes at once with those held before it, as do 4 held (`RX_VK_BATCH_MAX`), and the held go when the replay of the game's submit returns. Neither changes the command buffers or their order on the queue. `0`: each submit at once (the default elsewhere). The `vk: batched submits` lines say which and how many command buffers went in each queue call |
| `RX_VK_PEND_HELD=0` | (Triforce, on by default) A flush for a fence wait or a progress poll looks at a flag before the queue's lock (`dev.present_mu`) and returns when nothing is held (always, with batching off): it took the lock to find nothing, behind a present or a late page's fix waiting on the GPU. A held submit's recording is made known to the waiters only once it is held with the flag raised, so a waiter that finds it flushes it (before, it could flush nothing and wait for the held signal until the next poll, present or cap); a recordings' wait whose recording the recordings' timeline has passed returns without a flush. The batched submits' line counts the looks that found nothing held. `=0`: the lock every time and the registration first, as before; `RX_VK_LAB_AB=nopendheld` the same in its phases (`tests/test_flush_nothing_held.py`) |
| `RX_VK_IMPORT_HIGH=<percent>` or `0`, `RX_VK_IMPORT_LOW=<percent>` | The idle imports' valve (`gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `imports_give_back`): past `HIGH` percent (85) of the system heap's budget, the imported pools bound least lately, idle for 600 submissions and without a mirror, are given back down to `LOW` percent (75; 10 to 95). On by default where that budget is under 12 GB (on Windows only with the memory handling on); `RX_VK_IMPORT_HIGH=85` turns it on at any budget, `=0` off altogether. Setting either keeps the whole budget as the measure (no cap, below) |
| `RX_VK_IMPORT_CAP_MB=<n>` or `0` | (Triforce, the performance audit's MEM-22, after Source A's) What the valve above measures the heap against, where that is less than the budget (`gpu/vk/import_budget.h`). Unset: 4,500 MB with Mesa's RADV at a budget of 12 GB or more, where the valve was off and live imports piled up for the session (Source A, an RX 9070 XT with 32 GB: 221 live imports and 7.4 GB after two hours against 50-64 and 2.3-2.7 GB capped); no cap on any other driver or at a smaller budget. `<n>`: that cap on any driver and budget, which turns the valve on. `0`: no cap, RADV's behaviour before. A pass against the cap gives back 1 GB at most. Not run on RADV here |
| `RX_VK_FOLIAGE_SHADOW_CULL=<layer>[f]` | (Triforce, after NX2navi's 754ded9) On from layer 1 unless set (`off`: as the game draws them): the draws of mesh programs into the sun's shadow cascades (depth only, square, wider than 768) from that layer of the cascades on (0 to 2; NX2navi's is 1) that cull neither face are drawn with their back faces culled (the front ones with `f`, as `1f`): the foliage's double-sided cards draw half their fragments there (`gpu/vk/foliage_shadow.h`). Not quite the same picture: a lone card facing away from the sun casts no shadow in the middle and far cascades (where cards stand in pairs or clumps the others cover it; the near cascade is drawn as the game asks). The log says `vk: RX_VK_FOLIAGE_SHADOW_CULL` at the start and names the first draws it culled (`vk: foliage shadow cull:`) |
| `RX_VK_FOLIAGE_SHADOW_VRS=<layer>` | (Triforce, after NX2navi's 754ded9) On from layer 2 unless set (`off`: the extension not asked for, no pipeline changed): the same draws of native mesh programs, from that layer of the cascades on, shade one fragment per 2 x 2 pixels (`VK_KHR_fragment_shading_rate`, where the device has `pipelineFragmentShadingRate` and native mesh shaders; elsewhere nothing changes): the leaves' alpha test runs a quarter as often, depth still per texel. The rate is dynamic state only in the pipelines of native mesh programs with no colour target and a depth one (and in their two shader libraries, keyed apart), which the driver builds again once: on such a device the driver's part of the preparation makes those libraries too (and the `RX_VK_LIB_CHECK` sample asks for them), once more at the first start after the update, before the game (the other libraries come back from the cache), and the light precompile builds each depth-only pipeline of a native mesh program once more; their keys, the program cache and every other pipeline stay as they were. The log says `vk: the far shadow cascades' foliage at 2 x 2:` at the start and names the first draws (`vk: foliage shadow 2 x 2:`) |
| `RX_VK_FOG_CULL=1` | (Triforce, after NX2navi's 0379b00) Off unless set: the volumetric fog's first compute pass skips, as the game does, the froxels behind a coarse depth table of the frame before and writes them as empty, which the fog's history smears into dark trails beside near objects (worst at the screen edges, in foggy places such as the Lost Woods and Kokiri Forest). By default those froxels are computed too: the comparison is made never true in that one module, found by its shape in the SPIR-V (`gpu/vk/spv_fog.h`: a block of five image writes, four of (0,0,0,1) and last 1/255, and the one branch to it an ordered greater-than decides). It is applied where the module is made (`make_module`), outside the translation stamp: the program cache's records stay as they were translated, so nothing is translated again, and only that one compute pipeline is new to the driver's caches. With the precompile keeping none (`RX_VK_PRECOMPILE_KEEP`, less RAM), its record of the compute programs built has the patch and the matcher's version in its key (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` kFogPatch times `spv_fog::kMatcher`, raised whenever the matcher finds modules it did not: a record written by the matcher before 11-17's, which patched none of Triforce's fog modules, is built again), so the first start with the patch, or with a new matcher, builds the listed compute programs once more before the game (every one but the fog's a cache hit, the fog's compiled into the play cache, `mkw_vk_pipelines_play.bin`, or the main one where there is none, and saved before the record says built) rather than compiling the fog's at its first dispatch; with `RX_VK_FOG_CULL=1` that key is the one it was. A little more GPU time in the fog's pass. The log says `vk: fog: module <crc>, the volumetric fog's first pass:` and what was done (also for a near miss: the exit found but not its comparison) |
| `RX_VK_FIFO` (set), `RX_VK_IMMEDIATE` (set) | The swapchain's present mode for one run, over the Insert menu's VSync row: FIFO, as with vsync forced by a driver; immediate, where the surface lists it. Unset, the row decides (`gpu/vk/present_mode.h`): On is mailbox where the driver has it (immediate on Windows where it has none, FIFO elsewhere), Off immediate wherever the surface lists it |
| `RX_VK_SKIP_PROG=<prefix>,...` | Leaves out the draws of these programs (default `53509044401d78d2`; `-` leaves out none) |
| `RX_VK_PIPE_SKIP=0`, `1` or `2` | Default/`1`: wait for shader translation; a pending pipeline can be skipped only for attachment-only draws whose targets hold an earlier frame. `0`: wait for every pipeline too. `2`: restore the old skip for untranslated draws with persistent targets; this can omit writes before the shader is known. Translated memory-writing draws still wait |
| `RX_VK_TRANSLATE_WAIT_MS=<ms>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PIPE-30, after Source A's; `gpu/vk/translate_wait.h`) Off by default: a draw whose program is still translating waits for it, as before. Set (1 to 100, digits only; 8 to try): a draw into targets that hold an earlier frame, of a program whose key's translated variants write no memory (noted at each new pipeline and at the precompile's loads), waits at most that many ms a frame in all, the translation hurried but not run on the draw's thread, then is left out; a spent budget leaves the next one out at once. A key never translated, one with a variant that writes memory, and a draw into new targets wait as before; `RX_VK_PIPE_SKIP=0` and `=2` keep their meaning. The log says how many waited and were left out (`vk: programs still translating, so far: ...`). More draws left out, mostly in load frames: for a lab to weigh (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `draw`, `translate_note`) |
| `RX_VK_PIPE_DEPTH_BUDGET=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PIPE-29, after Source B's; `gpu/vk/pipe_wait.h` `depth_budget`, `choose`) Off by default on every driver, Intel's too (the entry would have it on for Intel's Windows driver: that waits for the owner's word). Set: a depth-tested draw into a pass drawn once whose shaders write no memory, outside a samples-passed count and not into targets kept from an earlier frame (the item icon capture's), waits for its pipeline from the frame's wait pool (`RX_VK_PIPE_FRAME_WAIT_MS`, 50 ms) and is then left out; that pipeline is then left out at once until it is built (the list emptied every 600 presents). The first 16 left out are named, and a line counts them (`vk: depth-tested draws into passes drawn once, so far: ...`). A render into a new depth target left out can stay missing until it is drawn again: the trade Source B made for an Arc B580's 11-13 s freezes (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `draw`, `pipe_depth_budget`) |
| `RX_VK_PIPE_FRAME_WAIT_MS=<ms>`, `RX_VK_PIPE_ONESHOT_BUDGET=1`, `RX_VK_PIPE_SKIP_NEW=0` | A draw whose pipeline is not ready (`gpu/vk/pipe_wait.h`, NX2navi's rules). A full-screen draw (at most 6 vertices over half the pass or more: a lighting, fog or tone mapping pass) into targets that hold an earlier frame is left out only where the hold of the last picture takes its frame (`RX_VK_HOLD_PIPELINES`); where it cannot (at its bound, resting after one, or off) the draw waits for its pipeline up to this many ms a frame in all (50, at most 500), then 40 ms more, and is left out after both; its job goes to the front of the workers' queue meanwhile. `0`: left out, as before. `RX_VK_PIPE_ONESHOT_BUDGET=1` (off; NX2navi has it on): draws into targets drawn once and draws whose shaders write memory wait from the same budget and are left out once it is spent, instead of waiting until their pipeline is made. `RX_VK_PIPE_SKIP_NEW=0`: a pass whose colour target is drawn for the first time is kept by its depth target alone, as before (by default its draws wait, as for any target drawn once: the game's item icon capture). The log's `vk: pipelines not ready` line counts them |
| `RX_VK_PIPE_FAST_WORKERS=0`, `RX_VK_PIPE_LINK_MS=<ms>` | A pipeline whose stage libraries were cold has those libraries built by a worker first and is linked from them, ready with that link, and its whole optimised build follows as a job of its own (`gpu/vk/pipe_wait.h` `libs_first`, NX2navi's `pipe_fast_workers`); a draw that waits for it runs the libraries and the link, not the whole build. `0`: the whole build first and the cold libraries after it, as before. `RX_VK_PIPE_LINK_MS=<ms>` (0 by default, at most 100; NX2navi's 6): the links made on the draws' threads in a frame take at most this long; a new pipeline after that is linked by a worker, its draws left out or waited for meanwhile (needs the fast pipeline link and the first switch on) |
| `RX_VK_PRECOMPILE=0` or `1` | The shader preparation before the title off or on (on in builds made with `RX_RELEASE=1`, as the builders' are) |
| `RX_VK_PREPARE_THREADS=<n>` | The threads the shaders are translated and their driver's part made on before the title, 1 to 64. Unset: every processor, a thread past half of them for each GB of RAM free at the start, and half in a process started above normal priority (the log's "the shaders' preparation runs on" line says how many) |
| `RX_VK_WARM_COMPUTES=listed` or `all` | (Triforce, 11-22; `gpu/vk/warm_computes.h`, `tests/test_warm_computes.py`) The compute pipelines the shader preparation's translation pass has the driver compile. `all`: every compute program's texture variants (about 9,700), as before. `listed`: only the programs play dispatched (`triforce_computes.bin`, about a tenth of them; the precompile builds the same ones), the others translated as before and compiled at their first dispatch; with no list yet (nothing played) every one. Unset: `listed` on Intel's GPUs (an Arc A750 spent 5175 s of a 5858 s pass on them, an RTX 4070 SUPER 170 s on the whole pass), `all` on the others. No stamp holds it |
| `RX_VK_COMPILE_SHADERS=all` or `most` | (Triforce, 11-24; `gpu/vk/compile_level.h`, `tests/test_compile_level.py`) Over the Debug tab's Compile shaders row (`compile_shaders=` in `triforce_options.ini`: 0 All, the default; 1 Most). All: the shader preparation as before. Most: the predicted pipelines are not built before the title (only behind the game in light frames, `predict_background`), and the compute pipelines are those of the programs play dispatched and the shipped state list names (`RX_VK_WARM_COMPUTES` and `RX_VK_PRECOMPILE_COMPUTES` as `listed` where they are not set): a shorter first preparation, a hitch in play where a pipeline left out is first needed. All after a preparation on Most runs the prediction before the title once (`<notes>.predicted.most`). No stamp holds it |
| `RX_VK_FALLBACK_SHARED_RECORDS=0` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's MEM-154) With the compute fallback for task and mesh shaders, each recording thread keeps buffer pairs: 64 MB of records, the same size for every program, and a payload sized by the task program. A payload that fits no pair now makes a pair that takes the newest pair's records and only a new payload; before, 64 MB more of records each time, kept for the session. The log's `vk: mesh fallback: a buffer pair` lines say made or shared. `0`: its own records each time, as before (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `fallback_buffers_of`) |
| `RX_VK_TASK_MESH_FALLBACK=0` or `1` | Task + mesh draws through the compute fallback (1) or the driver's mesh shaders (0). By default the fallback is on for AMD's Windows driver for RDNA 2 (every RX 6000 card and that generation's integrated GPUs: the driver whose `vk: vendor fixes` line names the "AMD proprietary shader compiler"), where the native path timed out the driver on the RX 6700 XT and RX 6800 XT (issue 996) |
| `RX_VK_FALLBACK_LOADED=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PIPE-62, after Source A's) On the task and mesh fallback, the precompile is handed only the fallback programs whose records are still on the disk, as before. The default also hands it every fallback program already in memory that is ready and not failed (`gpu/vk/vk_shader.cpp` `program_cache_load_some`, in the part outside the translation stamp), so that their compute pipelines are built on the precompile's workers before the title: in a start that prepares, `state_list_prepare` has read the state lists' programs in before the precompile asks, and those got no pipelines until their first draw, which waited up to 50 ms on the recording thread and was left out until they were built. The log says how many: `vk: N mesh fallback programs read in earlier in this start are made ready with the rest`. Only GPUs on the fallback (no mesh shaders, RX 6000 and RDNA2 integrated GPUs on Windows, the menu's Compute row, `RX_VK_TASK_MESH_FALLBACK=1`); the preparing start's load grows by the compiles moved out of play |
| `RX_VK_MESH_AHEAD=0` | (Triforce, on by default, after NX2navi's `mesh_ahead`; `=0` as before) An indirect mesh draw's records are copied into a ring as `{count, 1, 1}` before it, outside the pass, so each one ended its pass and the next draw began it again (OoT's foliage, grass and leaves: about 16 a frame at the Market). Now the first indirect mesh draw of a run copies the records of the indirect mesh draws after it as well, in the same three barriers, and those stay in the pass: a run is the indirect mesh draws after it with only state, draws, counter reports and render conditions between (`gpu/vk/vk_replay.cpp` `mesh_run_kind`); a dispatch, a barrier, a copy, a clear, a uniform update, new targets or an event ends it, so no work of the game's between can write the records after they were copied. A draw takes only the entry copied for it (its op, records, count and stride); at most 32 draws and 1 MB of the ring a run (`gpu/vk/mesh_ahead.h`). A `vk: indirect mesh draws` line every 20,000 conversions says how many took their records from an earlier draw |
| `RX_VK_NO_PROGRAM_CACHE` (set) | Neither reads nor writes `hookshot_programs.bin` and the pipeline notes |
| `RX_VK_PROGRAM_CARRY=0` | The program cache (`triforce_programs.bin`) is good for one build of the translator only, as before: any change to its files, a log line or a host option's number in `gpu/vk/vk_api.h` too, translates every program again, and the pipeline cache, the pipeline notes and the prepare's record go with it. By default the cache is carried over an update that changed nothing of what translates (`gpu/vk/program_carry.h` has the rule, `tests/test_program_carry.py` holds it): the translation stamp (`nx2/build.py` `translation_stamp`) is the translator's files without `vk_api.h`, without the parts of `vk_shader.cpp` and `vk_bank.cpp` marked as outside it, and without comments, blank lines and spaces at lines' ends. The build log's `build: translation stamp` line says whether it is the last release's (`nx2/released_translation_stamp.json`, which players' caches are for, and which `tests/test_program_carry.py` holds this source to) or which stamped files moved it against that release; a second line, in brackets, compares it with the previous build in the same folder (its record: `translation_stamp.json` in the build folder, written once the objects are compiled). Both are about the build's part of the stamp: the runtime adds its own settings to it (`disk_stamp`). The log's `vk: program cache` line at the start says whether the programs were kept, carried over an update of the translator (and from which build), or translated again (and how many the cache had); while they are translated again a line now and then counts them. Switching it either way translates every program once more |
| `RX_VK_PREPARE_RECHECK=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, after Casual Gamer's #dev-fixes post) Both as before: a start whose record (`triforce_prepared.bin`) says the shaders are prepared takes its word, and the empty mark of a start that died in the driver's part without stage libraries (`triforce_prepared.bin.warming`) leaves that part out on any GPU and driver. By default (`gpu/vk/prepare_again.h`, `vk_draw.cpp` `prepare_run`): where the record says nothing to do, the program cache's head and size are read, and one made again since for another GPU or other settings (another stamp, or its head alone) holds none of the shaders: `preparing again` (logged), the stale record removed first; a cache carried over an update keeps the stamp and passes. And the mark holds what the part is made for (the GPU, the driver, the programs), as the libraries' journal does: another stamp's mark is another driver's, and the part goes on (`tests/test_prepare_again.py`) |
| `RX_VK_CACHE_SET=0` | (Triforce) No earlier set of the shader caches is kept or put back (`gpu/vk/cache_set.h` has the rule, `gpu/vk/vk_cache_set.cpp` the files, `tests/test_cache_set.py`). By default, when the caches' stamp changes and the build of the translator did not (a setting of the translation: the menu's Robust buffers row, the mesh shaders row, the GPU, an `RX_VK_` switch of the translation), the set there (the program cache and its index, the pipeline cache and the play file with their `.for`, the prepare's record with its `.check`, `.warming` and `.warming.skip`, the pipeline notes and what is kept beside them by the stamp) is renamed to `<name>.<stamp>.<ext>` when the disk has twice the set and 10 GB free, and `triforce_cache_set.kept.txt` lists it. One set is kept. A start whose stamp is the kept set's puts every piece of it back (a set with a piece missing is deleted), and the set it found is kept in its place; a start of another build deletes it. `triforce_cache_set.txt` names what the set there was made for, and the log's `vk: shader caches:` lines say which part of the stamp changed. A set put back while the player's `triforce_pipeline_states.bin` is not the one it was kept with leaves `triforce_cache_set.states.txt`, and the prepare before the title then goes through the state lists, so that what was met under the other set is built before the game. Files of a kept set's names that no record names (a start that ended in a swap) are deleted at the next start. `RX_VK_CACHE_SET=0` also deletes a kept set; `RX_VK_PCACHE_KEEP_OLD` and `RX_VK_NO_PROGRAM_CACHE` leave it off too (a kept set of this build stays, another build's is deleted) |
| `RX_VK_GPL=1` or `0`, `RX_VK_GPL_KEEP_LINKS` (set) | The fast pipeline link (`VK_EXT_graphics_pipeline_library`; `gpu/vk/gpl_rules.h`), on by default since options_version 5: a new pipeline is linked from its program's two shader libraries where the driver has them compiled, and the optimised build replaces it. The Debug tab's Fast pipeline link row is the same without an environment to set (`fast_pipeline_link=1`); the switch goes over the row. With it the preparation has its driver's part ("Compiling shaders"), which now goes on after a start that died in it, leaving out the programs that start had in the driver's hands (`triforce_prepared.bin.warming` and `.warming.skip`), up to three starts in a row. `RX_VK_GPL_KEEP_LINKS`: the linked pipelines the optimised ones replaced are kept for the run, as before, instead of destroyed 600 presents and 10 s later. `RX_VK_NO_WARM` (set): no driver's part; `RX_VK_GPL_SYNC` (set): a cold library is compiled in the draw; `RX_VK_GPL_NO_CACHE` (set): the libraries are not kept in the pipeline cache |
| `RX_VK_LIB_CHECK=0` | (Triforce, after NX2navi's e10ef60) On unless `0`. At a start whose record says the driver's part is made for these shaders and this driver, a sample of 12 kept program variants has its two stage libraries made as that part makes them, asking the caches only where the device can (the answer a draw's link gets; without the flag, `RX_VK_GPL_SYNC`, their time against a second making, which comes from the cache), twice, before the game (`gpu/vk/lib_check.h`). When more than half are cold (`mkw_vk_pipelines.bin` lost or refused, or the libraries built otherwise now without `kWarmRecipe` counted up) the driver's part is made again at once, as after a driver update, with its journal and skip list; warm, nothing more happens (some 50 library lookups). `triforce_prepared.bin.check` records that it did: a start that finds them cold again right after leaves them to the draws, as before (each try holds a start for minutes; remove the file to try again). A making again whose cache could not be saved is recorded the same way. A cache file over its bound is not made again at all. The log's `vk: stage libraries:` line says what was found |
| `RX_VK_BUILT_CHECK=0`, `=2` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PIPE-21, after Source A's and Source B's; `gpu/vk/built_check.h`) On unless `0`. A light precompile (`RX_VK_PRECOMPILE_KEEP`: none kept) records what it built for the caches in `<notes>.built`, and later starts left all of it to its first draw or dispatch on the record's stamp alone. Now, before the record is trusted, up to 24 of the recorded notes are asked of Triforce's own pipeline caches as a draw asks them (without compiling, where the device has `pipelineCreationCacheControl`; without it each is built twice and timed: a first build over 4 times the second and 3 ms more was compiled), and with the record's compute key up to 6 of the listed compute programs as a dispatch asks them. Under 16 answers nothing is said; half of them or more not there: the caches lost them, and everything noted and listed is built again before the title and recorded anew (`vk: precompile's record: ...`). Lost again right after that (`<notes>.built.check`): left to the draws, as before, with a hint about the driver's shader cache size. Also counted: play builds of recorded pipelines that took 40 ms or more and recorded compute programs a dispatch did not find (`vk: precompile's record: in play so far, ...`, at most every 10 s while it grows). `2`: built again at every start (the lab's). `0`: neither the check nor the counter, as before (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `built_check_cold`, `precompile_run`, `built_play_build`) |
| `RX_VK_SPV_PACK=0` | Every shader module kept for the whole run, as before. By default the SPIR-V of graphics and mesh programs is kept packed in memory (`gpu/vk/spv_store.h`, to about 0.37 of its size) and their shader modules, which the driver otherwise holds for the whole run, are made only for the time a pipeline is created with them, on the thread that builds it. Compute programs, the programs the preparation lets go and the programs a draw reads from the cache itself keep their modules. The log's `vk: shader code packed` lines say what it comes to and what it costs |
| `RX_VK_PCACHE_RAM=1` | Off by default (in its one session on, v0.4.0-test9, it gave back nothing the working set showed). With it, once the PC has had under 1.5 GB of RAM available at two looks a second apart, the pipeline cache (when it is 256 MB or more) is destroyed once no pipeline creation is using it (`gpu/vk/pcache_gate.h`): the pipelines built after that go without it and it is not saved again that session, so `mkw_vk_pipelines.bin` keeps its last save. The log's `vk: RAM short ...: the pipeline cache ... is let go` line says when and what the working set did. Whatever this switch says, the cache's save in play waits while a copy of it would not fit in the RAM available with 2 GB to spare (the save at the game's close does not), and the builder of the predicted pipelines behind the game rests while under 3 GB (or a tenth of a PC of more than 30 GB) is available (`gpu/vk/mem_parts.h`) |
| `RX_VK_WRITERS_WAIT_MS=<ms>` | (Triforce) How long the game's close waits for the files the renderer is writing on threads of their own (`gpu/vk/file_writers.h`; 2000 by default, `0`: no wait and nothing refused, as before). The close ends the process through ExitProcess, which stops every other thread where it is: the pipeline cache's save in play, the order file, a bug capture's or a debug picture, the hang report, the predicted pipelines' list, the frame log. At the close (`HOS_OPT_EXITING`) no such write begins any more and the ones under way are waited for; one still going at the bound is cut, and is one of the files written whole beside the old (`.part` / `.tmp`, then put in place: the prepared record `triforce_prepared.bin` and the `.for` files are now too), or a list whose reader takes whole records only. The log's `vk: exit: the files being written ... were waited for` line says how long, and what was cut |
| `RX_VK_OPTIONS_MERGE=0` | (Triforce) The menu writes its own values over `triforce_options.ini` in place at every change, as before. By default (`gpu/vk/ini_merge.h`, `vk_menu.cpp` `save`) each line it writes is merged by its key with the file as it is then: a line changed outside the game while it runs (a text editor, a tool) and not changed in the game since the game read or wrote the file is written as the file has it, so a change made outside and one made in the menu within the same second both stay (a row changed on both sides is the game's); the game takes such a line at its next start. The file is written whole beside the old one and put in its place (in place only where another program holds it open so that it cannot be replaced). The log's `menu: options: ... changed in triforce_options.ini outside the game` line names such lines |
| `RX_VK_MEM_PARTS=0` | No `vk: memory, the parts` line. By default every 600 presents it gives the working set, the game's own memory committed and in RAM (a sample of its pages), the rest of the working set in RAM by what it is (a sample too: the program's code, the driver's code, other code, mapped files and views, write-combined or uncached memory, the heaps and other private memory), the shader modules alive and the packed stages, the pipeline cache, the pipelines and stage libraries kept (how many a draw has had, how many were given back) and the allocator's blocks in system memory (`gpu/vk/mem_parts.h`), and what its walks and samples took. It is written on a thread of its own that nothing waits for; the guest window's walk is made again every eighth line |
| `HOS_MEM_ZERO_MARK=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's MEM-13; off unless set; not yet run in the game) Freed guest pages that a Vulkan import holds are zeroed in place, not discarded (`host/hos_mem.c` `discard_pages` and `FreeMemoryPages`, `host/hos_svc.c` UnmapPhysicalMemory). The renderer does not see a bare memset, so its mirror, its captures and the write-back's shadow keep the freed bytes, and a later write-back could put them back over the zeros. With the switch the fill (`host/hos.h` `hos_guest_fill`) marks the pages written, after the memset. Off by default until it is measured: the marked zeros (up to 5.2 GB over a tour) are pages the captures and the mirror syncs then copy, which could hitch an arrival. To measure: two runs a side, the log's "capture ... copied (MB)" and "mirror syncs ... dirty pages", and the frames over 50 ms in each arrival's first 5 seconds |
| `RX_VK_SWAP_RESERVE_MB=<MB>`, `RX_VK_TEST_SWAP_MB=<n>` | (Linux; Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's MEM-141, after Source A's; off unless set, not yet run on Linux) A memory pressure hold that counts the swap. The RAM rules look at `MemAvailable` alone, which a PC with swap keeps up by pushing the game's pages out (a 16 GB Steam Deck's swap filled while it said little). With the reserve set (Source A's is 2560), every 30 presents the PC is short when `MemAvailable` is under 1536 MB or `MemAvailable` and `SwapFree` together are under the reserve, and until both are 256 MB clear of their lines a hold is on: a capture buffer is kept as a spare only up to 32 MB of spares, the staging arenas the GPU is done with are freed as it begins and then after 5 s unused, `malloc_trim` is asked every 600 presents from 64 MB free, idle imports are given back (in all of one hold at most the bytes the PC was short at its worst, still only after 3,600 idle submissions), and an integrated GPU's texture budget is less by what the PC is short, never under 512 MB, coming back 256 MB a look. A PC without swap, or whose swap cannot be read, is never short by the sum. The log's `vk: swap hold: on` and `off` lines say when and what each gave. `0` or not set: no hold, as before; never on Windows. `RX_VK_TEST_SWAP_MB=<n>` plays that much swap free, as `RX_VK_TEST_AVAIL_MB` plays `MemAvailable` (`gpu/vk/linux_pressure.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `swap_hold_step`) |
| `HOS_MEM_CENSUS=<seconds>` or `0` | (Triforce) The guest memory census (`host/guest_census.h`), on by default every 5 seconds, on a thread of its own: a `mem: census` line with what the runtime handed out of the guest window by kind (the program and the system region, the heap, the address regions and the game's own pages in them, what lies outside them: the physical maps and first touches), the game's own count against the 9809 MB it is told it has, the pages the game let go that stay committed (zeroed under an import, or discarded with a freed region) and what the give-back gave back, the process's commit and working set, and the PC's commit and RAM; and a `mem: census, the renderer` line with the Vulkan back end's big holders (its imports, mirrors, VRAM in use, the allocator's blocks in system memory, the pipeline cache, the pipelines and stage libraries kept, the shader modules). A count of bits a 4 KB page, kept as each place commits, maps or lets go of guest pages. `0`: no line (and, with the give-back off, nothing kept) |
| `HOS_MEM_GIVE_BACK=1` or `2` | (Triforce) Off by default, not yet run in the game: the pages the game let go that stay committed because an import held them (`FreeMemoryPages`, `UnmapPhysicalMemory`) or because they were discarded with a freed address region are decommitted once nothing of the renderer imports them, from 4 GB up (below it pages are handed out without a commit), up to 256 MB a census pass, in runs of 16 MB at most; the renderer answers and runs the decommit with its import locks held (`gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `guest_release_unimported`), so no import can lock them between. `1`: every pass; `2`: only while the PC is short (its commit past 85% of its limit, or under 1.5 GB of RAM available). The census line's `given back` says how much |
| `HOS_MEM_MAP_RESULT=0` | (Triforce) `MapPhysicalMemory` answers success even where Windows refused the commit, as before. By default it answers Horizon's LimitReached (`0x10801`), which the SDK turns into its out-of-memory result, and the log says `mem: MapPhysicalMemory ...: the commit failed` |
| `RX_VK_PRECOMPILE_KEEP=1` or `0` | The precompile keeps every pipeline it builds for the session (1), or keeps none of them: they are built for the caches and let go, and a draw that first needs one builds it from there (0). Unset or `auto`: kept where the RAM available at the precompile and what the game holds already (less what reading the pipeline cache added, except on AMD's driver: `own_for_keep`) come to 22 GB or more, on a PC of 20 GB or more (`gpu/vk/mem_parts.h` keep_all_pipelines); the log's `vk: precompile:` line says which and why. (Triforce, 11-22, after Casual Gamer's #dev-fixes post) Unset (not `auto`): on a discrete GPU in a PC of under 20 GB every pipeline is kept while the game is told its mapped pages are used (`HOS_MEM_QUERY_USED`, `keep_all_small_pc`): a 16 GB laptop, ~0.6 GB more private memory, frames of 1 s or more 15 and 11 in two tours -> 3 and 6. A card the driver allows under 6.5 GB still leaves them to their first draw first; integrated GPUs keep the RAM rule; `auto`: the RAM rule, as before |
| `RX_VK_TEX_PIPES_AHEAD=0` | (Triforce, on by default; `=0` as before) The texture converters' compute pipelines (the GPU ASTC decoder, the BC encoder, the unswizzle and the swizzle back: `gpu/vk/vk_texture.cpp` `texture_compute_pipeline`, `texture_pipelines_warm`) made through the pipeline cache kept across runs, and all four before the game: in the precompile, right after the cache is read, or at the start when the preparation was skipped at the build. Each was made without a cache inside the first upload that needed it, which with an empty driver cache (a first start, a new driver) waited for the compile, a `vk: slow upload` of 100-320 ms. The ASTC decoder and the BC encoder only where the GPU does not sample ASTC. The `vk: texture converters ready in` line says what it took |
| `RX_VK_PIPE_RELEASE=0`, `RX_VK_PIPE_RELEASE_EVERY=<presents>` | Every kept pipeline stays for the session, as before. By default, while the PC's RAM is short (under 1.5 GB available at two looks in a row), every 300 presents up to 512 of the kept pipelines no draw has had yet are given back to the driver (`gpu/vk/pipe_release.h`); a draw that needs one later has it built again by a worker. The log's `vk: RAM short: ... kept pipelines no draw has had given back` lines say how many and what the working set did |
| `RX_VK_PCACHE_CARRY=0` | The pipeline cache's file carried over a stamp change (`gpu/vk/pcache_carry.h`): at an update's preparation (Setup's "Preparing shaders", `HOOKSHOT_PREPARE_ONLY`) a `mkw_vk_pipelines.bin` made for other shaders is read all the same when it is this device's and driver's, within its bound, and at most 1.25 times its base (the size of the last set built fresh, kept beside it in `mkw_vk_pipelines.bin.base` with the carries since): the pipelines of shaders the update did not change are hits instead of hours of compiling. The cap is over every carry since the base was built, not a quarter more each time, and a carried cache may never pass the file's bound either. The preparation looks at a carried cache's size every 10 s while it builds and at each save: past the cap (or the bound), nothing more is built into it, it is not written, `mkw_vk_pipelines.bin.nocarry` names the new stamp (a preparation begun again after a Cancel starts fresh), and the preparation builds the driver's part again into a fresh cache: the compute pipelines the carried run built, from the program cache, then the stage libraries, nothing translated again (from the driver's own cache for what it had just compiled), which gets a new base. A carried preparation that is stopped (a Cancel, a closed window) saves its cache under the new stamp with its history marked open in `.base`: the preparation begun again reads it as carried still, capped the same (or, when `.nocarry` names the stamp, not at all), until a carried preparation goes through to its end. The log says `pipeline cache carried over a stamp change (N MB, base M MB)`. `0`: a file for other shaders is never read, as before (the rule of 11-14). The play file keeps its own rule |

The log's `boot: room in the drivers' shader caches:` line gives both values. `HOS_DRIVER_CACHE_ROOM=0`, in the environment or `triforce_env.txt`: neither is set, as before. Nothing is done on Windows, where NVIDIA's driver takes its size from its control panel (`host/hos_main.c` `driver_cache_room`) |
| `HOS_DRIVER_CACHE_FOLDER=0` | (Linux; Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PIPE-98, after Source A's; not yet run on Linux) NVIDIA's Linux driver keeps no shader cache at all when `__GL_SHADER_DISK_CACHE_PATH` names a folder that is not there: it neither makes it nor falls back to its own folder, and every prepared pipeline is compiled again at each start. By default the game makes the folder the variable names, with its parents, for the user alone, before Vulkan loads (the builder's `--prepare-shaders` run too); the value is never changed, nothing is made when the variable is not set, and a `boot: __GL_SHADER_DISK_CACHE_PATH=<path> is not a folder and cannot be made one` line says when it could not. `HOS_DRIVER_CACHE_FOLDER=0`, in the environment or `triforce_env.txt`: nothing is made, as before. When the stage libraries are built into a cache carried over a stamp change, every 32nd program of the warm's order is built first, the others waiting for it, and the cache's growth over that sample, per program, times all the programs, is where it ends at this rate. With at least 1500 libraries in the sample, a projected growth of at least twice the room it had when they began (its cap, or the bound, less its size then) drops the carry at once, exactly as past its limit (not written, `.nocarry`, the driver's part built again into a fresh cache), a few minutes in rather than when it gets there: an update whose file was built by an older translator misses nearly every library and filled the cache for 34 minutes before it was let go. The sample is spread over the whole order, not its head: the notes' programs go first and the rest in the program cache's file order, and hits and misses come in stretches of it, so a head that misses while the rest hits would have been dropped for nothing. The notes' programs wait for the sample (a few minutes cold). The log says `the pipeline cache carried over a stamp change would grow to about N MB at this rate`. The compute pipelines' pass and the precompile keep the look past the limit alone. `0`: dropped only once past its limit, and the warm in its own order, as before |
| `RX_VK_PCACHE_MAX_MB=<MB>` | The bound on the pipeline cache's file, `mkw_vk_pipelines.bin` (`gpu/vk/pcache_bound.h`): a file over it is not read (the cache starts empty and the next save writes a new one), and data over it is not written (the file keeps its last save). Unset: an eighth of the PC's RAM, 2048 MB at least and 8192 MB at most. `0`: no bound, as before |

Recorded in `<file>.lean` for that file's life (a full file stays full until the next fresh one); not decided in a start that only prepares. `vk: pipeline cache: libraries only (...)`. `2`: lean at every fresh file whatever the PC (the lab's). Whole pipelines then load 3-4 times slower from the driver's shared, capped cache (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `pcache_libs_decide`, `build_graphics`, `pcache_play_open`) |
| `RX_VK_PCACHE_CARRY_MB=<MB>` | (Triforce, 11-18) The carry of the pipeline cache's file over an update that changed the translation (`gpu/vk/pcache_bound.h` `carried`): a file for other shaders is read all the same when it is no larger than this (1536 MB unset), leaves room under the bound for as much again and the PC has 31 GB of RAM or more (Windows), so the driver finds what it compiled for the shaders that did not change (AMD's driver keeps no cache of its own for these). `0`: never carried, the rule of 11-14 alone |

At a start whose play file has reached `RX_VK_PCACHE_FOLD_MB` (64) it is folded into the first cache, that session builds into it as before, and its first save writes it and removes the play file. Each goes through the preparation's own save: the file's bound, a carried session's cap with its history kept open as a stopped one's (`RX_VK_PCACHE_CARRY`), the file written whole with its `.for` and `.base`; it is left out while a copy of the cache would not fit in the RAM available with 2 GB to spare or the disk lacks its size and 1 GB (asked again a minute later), fetched with room for what is built meanwhile, written to the disk before it takes the old file's place, given up when the window is closed or Setup's Cancel is pressed (the stop's own save writes the cache), due only while the part moves (a stuck one goes silent), and it does not count the part as made (the next start still runs it).
