The ranges of the game's memory a run imported on a binding's own thread (8 to 17 ms each on an RTX 5090), whole as they were imported (their span over the game's pools included; the first version kept 16 MB chunks, which a binding across their ends imported again as one), are kept in `triforce_imports_ahead.txt` beside the game, at most 256, and the next runs import them on the background import thread as soon as the game's pools reach them, 4 at a look, at each change of the pool table and every 8 submissions (`gpu/vk/import_ahead.h`), each range once a session, and none while the PC's RAM is low (under 3 GB available, or a tenth of the RAM: the RAM's rule gives idle imports back then, and the two took turns on the same ranges in the owner's test9 session). A file of the first version's chunks is read as it is. Unset: off (it was on with NVIDIA's driver only, as `RX_VK_ASYNC_IMPORT` is); `1`: on any card; `0`: off. (Triforce) A race that fits the hang is closed since: the host decommitted the game's freed pages under an import the background thread had locked and not yet put in the pool table (`vk_memory.cpp` `bg_held`; `RX_VK_BG_IMPORT_HOLD=0`: not, as before, for the A/B); it stays off until a lab A/B with `=1` hangs no more. The log's `vk: imports ahead` lines count them and name the ranges sent, `vk: the game's pages ... are freed under a background import` the frees that window met; the stall line's "pools imported where bound" is what is left |
| `RX_VK_IMPORT_TOUCH=0` | Off: the pages of a range of the game's memory are not read before the driver imports it. On by default, on Windows, for the imports made on the background thread (`gpu/vk/import_touch.h`): the driver locks a range's pages inside `vkAllocateMemory`, faulting in the untouched ones, and a queue submit from another thread waits for it; the ranges imported ahead are up to 1 GB. Read first, outside the driver, the allocate of the 288 to 752 MB ranges went from 16 to 93 ms to 4 to 13 ms on an RTX 5090, and the longest queue submit beside an import in play from 71 to 93 ms to 10 to 12 ms. The log's `vk: import` lines give a slow import's parts: the first 12 of a session, and the first 48 of 64 MB or more. |
| `RX_VK_IMPORT_LEAVING=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, after Casual Gamer's #dev-fixes post) The game's freed pages under an import already given back whose memory is not freed yet (an evicted pool, a retired pool's import given back) are discarded or decommitted, as before. By default they are zeroed in place, as under a live import (`gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `memory_guest_imported`, `imports_leaving_overlap`): the driver keeps those pages locked until the GPU is past them, and a discard there lost the device (v0.4.0 on a 16 GB laptop: 2 tours of 5 without, 0 of 53 with). The log says `freed under an import still being given back` for the first 8 and every 64th (`tests/test_import_leaving.py`). |
| `RX_VK_KEEP_RETIRED=1` or `0` | A retired pool's import (a pool merged into a larger one) kept for the session (1) or given back once the GPU is past it (0; `gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `retired_imports_give_back`). Unset: given back on Linux and with NVIDIA's driver, kept on Windows with AMD's and Intel's (device losses right after they went back there, issues 17, 309, 571). (Triforce, 11-22, after Casual Gamer's #dev-fixes post) Unset, those kept are given back all the same past the import rule's high mark of the system heap's budget (`RX_VK_IMPORT_HIGH`, 85%) or for a minute after an import was refused for want of room (`gpu/vk/mem_parts.h` `retired_pressed`; the log says `retired imports given back after all`): on a Radeon 780M the retired imports filled that heap, and 24.9% of the draws were left out for want of their pools (0 with it; refused imports 16 -> 1). `=1` keeps them always, as before. |
| `RX_VK_CAP_SPARE_MB=<MB>` or `0` | The submit-time capture's spare buffers, kept for the next captures (12 at most, `gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `cap_spare_max`), held to that many MB; `0`: up to 768 MB, as before. Unset: 32 MB on a card the driver allows 6.5 GB or less, and (Triforce, 11-22, after Casual Gamer's #dev-fixes post) on a PC of under 20 GB of RAM (`gpu/vk/mem_parts.h` `spare_capped`; the log says `capture buffers: 32 MB of spares at most`): on a 16 GB laptop the spares held ~0.45 GB that Windows paged out and in again, capped 10-18% fewer page-ins. Not on AMD's cards, where a capture buffer made anew costs about 100 ms: they keep their spares |
| `RX_VK_IMPORT_RAM=1` | The imports' second trigger (`gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `imports_give_back`): with the PC's RAM short (under 1.5 GB available) every import unbound for ~10 s of play is given back, up to 1 GB a pass. (Triforce, 11-22, after Casual Gamer's #dev-fixes post) Off unless set: a 16 GB PC has under 1.5 GB available nearly all the time, so it fired every ~20 s and the imports it gave back came straight back (690 to 1216 imports in a 13-minute tour). The system heap's trigger (`RX_VK_IMPORT_HIGH`) stays as it is. |
| `RX_VK_KEEP_RETIRED=wait`, `RX_VK_RETIRED_REFUSED=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's MEM-21, after Source A's; `gpu/vk/retired_give_back.h`; **off unless set**, not yet run in the game) A pool remade larger is retired, and once the GPU is past it its import is pinned RAM nothing binds. Unset, as before: given back at once on Linux and with NVIDIA's driver on Windows, kept for the session with AMD's and Intel's on Windows (given back at once there, an Arc B390, an Ally X and an RX 6600 lost the device right after); `RX_VK_KEEP_RETIRED=0` gives back at once and any other value keeps, as before. `wait`, on any driver: given back `RX_VK_RETIRE_SECS` (30) seconds after the pool retired, `RX_VK_RETIRE_MIN_SECS` (2) while the system heap is past 85% of its budget; with it the imports given back and not freed yet are left out of what the pressure give-back (`RX_VK_IMPORT_HIGH`) reads. `RX_VK_RETIRED_REFUSED=1`: also the retired pools whose mirror was refused (`RX_VK_NO_MIRROR_POOLS`, a pool over 1 GB, video memory refused), never given back before on any OS; on with `wait` unless `=0`. The `vk: imports:` line every 1200 mirror epochs says how many retired pools are still imported |
| `HOS_CRASH_THREADS=0` | Off: a crash report has the faulting thread alone. On by default, on Windows (`host/crash_threads.h`): a crash or fatal error's report ends with one `CRASH: beside it, thread ...` line for every other thread, its host stack as module and offset, taken at the report's start on a helper thread; the threads running code come first, then the ones waiting in the system. For the crash while a save loads, which reads an object whose memory holds something else: the lines say what the other threads were doing. |
| `HOS_CRASH_TEST_S=<seconds>` | (the lab's) A host thread reads address 8 that long after the start: a crash report on purpose, to look at one from a run in the game (its other threads' lines above). Not for play. |
| `EDEN_NX2_GM_LEAN=1` | Only the global-memory helpers a program's instructions call are put into its SPIR-V (`gpu/vk/eden/shader_recompiler/backend/spirv/gm_lean.h`). A stage that reads or writes through a pointer no storage buffer was tracked to carried every helper, six and for a storing stage eighteen, each with a search over every storage buffer in it. Off unless set: the SPIR-V is then what it was. An `EDEN_` name: each setting has its own program cache. The translator's files are in the translation stamp, so a build with this code translates every program again once, whatever the switch says. |
| `RX_VK_UPLOAD_AGAIN_MS=<ms>` | A texture uploaded before whose memory the game wrote again, met by a draw after the frame's uploads took this long (16), is uploaded at the next frame instead; its last bytes are drawn meanwhile, at most 8 frames in a row (`gpu/vk/upload_owed.h` `later`). New textures are never left so (theirs is `RX_VK_HOLD_MS`'s rule). `0`: every one at once, as before. The log's "left for the next frame" lines and the stall line count them. `RX_VK_UPLOAD_AGAIN_FRAMES=<n>`: a texture's upload is left so at most that many frames in a row (2: its last bytes drawn a frame or two at most; `8`: as before). With 16 ms nothing was ever left in the lab's runs (the frames with re-upload bursts took 9.8 ms of uploads); `3` spreads a burst over the frames after it, its textures a frame or two late meanwhile, which is why it is not the default |
| `RX_VK_UPLOAD_TORN=0` | (Triforce, wp18-flash) A texture uploaded before, 1 MB or more, whose storage the CPU has rewritten since the copy its re-upload would read (a capture's copy of one of its whole pages, every 16th and the last, differs from guest memory at the draw's look) keeps its last bytes for that frame and is uploaded at the next frame with what that frame's capture took (`gpu/vk/upload_torn.h`): a copy taken while the game was still writing the texture was test29's white frames at the Market after a tour (BC6H bytes passing through, decoded to bright colours the bloom spread over the picture). At most one frame in a row, and not for a texture found so in the two presents before (one the CPU rewrites every frame is uploaded as before). Not a dispatch's upload. `0`: uploaded at once, as before. The log's `re-uploads of storage the CPU rewrote since its capture` lines count them, those found and those left (with `0` too: the measure of how often a copy may have been taken mid-write) |
| `RX_VK_REUPLOAD_EARLY=0` | The textures the CPU keeps rewriting (uploaded again from its writes twice or more within 120 presents, 1 MB or more, sampled in the frame or the one before, not drawn into by the GPU) are looked at once on the replay thread before a parallel replay's segments begin, after the dry pass, their uploads in the recording before the segments: not made by a segment under the recording lock, where the other recording threads queued behind them (lab S2 after a tour: 4 to 12 such uploads of 28 to 84 MB in ~13% of the frames, the descriptor time 24 ms over the threads against 3). `0`: the segments' looks alone, as before. The log's `textures the CPU keeps rewriting, looked at before` line says how many and how long (`gpu/vk/reupload_early.h`) |
| `RX_VK_ZERO_HOLES=0` | (Triforce, Linux, on by default; `=0` as before) The all-zero check of a big texture's storage (32 MB or more: cleared on the GPU when every byte is 0; `RX_VK_ZERO_UPLOAD=0` turns the check off) reads only what the guest memfd holds data for (`lseek` SEEK_DATA and SEEK_HOLE, `gpu/vk/guest_holes.h`), not its holes: a page the game never wrote reads as zero, but a read through the shared mapping made it RAM (the 288 MB shadow map at each Hyrule Field arrival, 200-300 ms on a Steam Deck). Where the window is not a memfd, or a seek fails, the whole storage is read. The `is all zero` and `is not all zero` lines say how many MB were read. `=0`: the whole storage always |
| `RX_VK_UPLOAD_SAME=0` | (Triforce) A texture uploaded before whose storage the CPU wrote again is checked against the bytes its image holds (`gpu/vk/upload_same.h`): the units of its storage the writes reached (each level's and layer's GOB-block rows, as its upload reads them) whose bytes hash as at their last upload are not uploaded, and when that is all of them nothing is. The upload of the rest reads the copy the check hashed, so that what is kept is what the image got; any other upload and any GPU write forget it. Textures of 16 MB or less uploaded on the CPU (block-compressed, or under 8 MB), not sparse; one whose checks find nothing the same 4 times in a row is uploaded unchecked for its next 64 re-uploads. Lab S2 after some tours: the game rewrote a few big BC textures the Market samples with the bytes they had, 40 to 60 MB every 4 to 5 frames (the Market at 62-78 fps against 83-96, ~26 GB of uploads in its stay). `0`: every re-upload made as before. The log's `re-uploads checked against the bytes their images hold` line says what it looked at, found the same and uploaded. `RX_VK_UPLOAD_SAME_KEEP=0`: the check hashes every byte it looks at; by default a page the submit-time capture keeps a copy of is hashed once and its hash kept with the copy until a capture copies other bytes into it (about half of those textures' pages passed through other bytes between two captures) |
| `RX_VK_UPLOAD_SAME_ASTC=0` | (Triforce) ASTC textures decoded on the GPU (kept as RGBA8, BC3 or BC1; 2D only) are checked too, by `RX_VK_UPLOAD_SAME`'s check: one unit a layer, its levels from the first one the image keeps, which is what the GPU decoder stages of a layer a write reached; the staging reads the copy the check hashed. Lab S2's Market after a tour: the game rewrote such textures with the bytes they had (1536 x 1536 ASTC 5x5 of 11 levels, 768 x 768 kept as BC3, 512 x 512), staged and decoded again unchecked, about 1.4 GB in 10 s (90 fps down to 72, the 1% low 57 to 33). `0`: ASTC re-uploads made as before, every layer a write reached staged and decoded. The `re-uploads checked` line says what of it was ASTC; the `re-uploads not checked` line every 600 frames counts the re-uploads made without the check, by route and by why |
| `RX_VK_TWIN_SAME=0` | (Triforce) The parallel replay's twin (a texture an earlier segment samples that a later segment of the same submission uploaded, uploaded again in the earlier segment's uploads so that its draws see the bytes as submitted) uploads only what that submission's uploads into the image asked for (`gpu/vk/upload_same.h` `note_upload`; the whole storage when one of them was whole or the image was made again), and where every unit it reads has a hash kept by `RX_VK_UPLOAD_SAME`'s check, reads a copy of them hashed as copied: when the copy holds the bytes the hashes are of, the hashes are kept. Before, the twin uploaded the whole storage and, unchecked, forgot the hashes the later segment's checked upload had just kept, so the next rewrite of the same bytes was uploaded again, the twin with it, every few frames: lab S2's Market after a tour, about 97% of what was still uploaded unchecked in those loops, up to 6.9 GB in one stay. `0`: the twin uploads the whole storage and forgets the hashes, as before. The log's `the parallel replay's twins` line every 600 frames counts them, the bytes uploaded against the whole, and how many kept the hashes |
| `RX_VK_UPLOAD_EARLIEST=0` | (Triforce) A parallel replay's upload of a texture, made by the worker of a later segment of the submission while an earlier one is still being recorded, is recorded into the init command buffer of the earliest segment still being recorded (submitted before that segment's uploads and commands; `gpu/vk/upload_earliest.h`, `vk_texture.cpp` `UploadRedirect`). Every segment before it has ended its recording and no longer samples the texture, and every one from it on finds the upload at or before its place in the queue, so no earlier segment uploads the texture again (the twin, `RX_VK_TWIN_SAME`): it is uploaded once, the same bytes. Lab S2's heavy Market loops (round 48) uploaded 2.1 GB of twins in their last minutes, the big streamed or new textures each uploaded once by a later segment and again by an earlier one; those twins took the frames' upload budget (`RX_VK_UPLOAD_NEW_MB`, `RX_VK_UPLOAD_NEW_MS`), and the new textures after them were left for the next frame, the picture held. Only an upload staged from the CPU as it is recorded is moved: not one read on the GPU (the GPU unswizzle, from the pool) or decided from live memory (the all-zero clear), which in the earlier segment's init would run before a segment between writes that memory on the GPU. Not for a texture the GPU drew (its contents are the GPU's; the CPU's writes over it, `RX_VK_CPU_OVER_GPU`, keep their own path) or a sparse one: those keep the segment's own uploads, and the twin. The uploads' GPU time in an init command buffer is not in the segments' GPU time. `0`: every upload in its segment's own uploads, the twin as before. The log's `uploads recorded into an earlier segment's init command buffer` line every 600 frames (with `0` too) counts them and the bytes, and how often and how long a segment's end waited for its init lock, each segment's own, held while such an upload (or a new image's first layout change) is recorded into its init (its submit waits as long) |
| `RX_VK_SHADOW_STAGE=0`, `RX_VK_CAP_UNCACHED=1` | (on by default; the row is Triforce's, 11-22, with the performance audit's MEM-39) A capture buffer asks for cached host memory, but a driver can hand out write-combined memory, which the CPU reads at about 0.3 GB/s. Such a capture's texture pages are copied into a staging block in ordinary memory first, and to the buffer from there, and every CPU read of them uses the staging (`gpu/vk/vk_memory.cpp` `capture_make`, `cap_page_bytes`): the shadow's copies, and since MEM-39 the `RX_VK_TEX_SAME` compare of each copied texture page with its shadow copy, which still read the buffer. `RX_VK_SHADOW_STAGE=0`: no staging, every read from the capture buffer, as before. Where the buffers are cached (the 600-take `shadow staging` line counts them) nothing is staged and nothing changes; the first buffer that is not cached is logged with its memory type. Lab only: `RX_VK_CAP_UNCACHED=1` asks for the capture buffers as memory written in order, which is uncached where the driver has such a type, to make the slow case on any PC, and the `RX_VK_LAB_AB` knob `texsamemap` reads the buffer in the compare in its phases |
| `RX_VK_SHADOW_KEEP=0` | (Triforce) A texture page the submit-time capture takes with the bytes its shadow copy holds (the copy the uploads read, `RX_VK_TEX_SAME`'s compare) keeps that copy as it is, not copied again, its kept hash with it (`gpu/vk/shadow_prev.h`). Lab S2's Market after some tours: about half of the ~400 texture pages a capture took, six captures a frame, held their copy's bytes. Only for a page the capture takes once; any other is copied as before, and the shadow holds what the capture holds either way. `0`: every page copied, as before. The log's `texture shadow (600 takes)` line counts the pages compared, left, given back and copied |
| `RX_VK_SHADOW_PREV=0` | (Triforce) A texture page the capture takes with other bytes keeps the copy it had, and that copy's hash, beside the new one (`gpu/vk/shadow_prev.h` `Prev`), and a capture that brings those bytes back gives it back, hash and all: no copy, and the re-upload check after it finds the hashes kept (lab S2's Market after some tours: the other half of those pages went back and forth between two versions, and a check hashed each again after every change, 0.2 to 0.3 ms a frame). A kept copy that does not come back within 8 changes of its page, or within 64 captures, goes; at most 64 MB are kept. `0`: none kept, a changed page copied over as before. The `texture shadow` line says how many are kept and how many went |
| `RX_VK_SHADOW_VERS` | (Triforce) The copies a texture page keeps beside its own in the shadow (`gpu/vk/shadow_prev.h` `Vers`): `RX_VK_SHADOW_PREV` keeps one, and a page whose captures bring more than two versions (A, B, C, A every few frames) is copied again for every version but the one before the last. `2` to `4` keep that many, newest first, and a capture that brings any kept version's bytes takes that one back, the copy it had becoming the newest, so each version a page cycles through while it is still among the kept copies is copied once: 4 KB a version, under the same 64 MB cap. Unset or `1`: one copy, as before. The `texture shadow` line counts the pages given a copy of their own, one before it, and two or more before |
| `RX_VK_SHADOW_CHECK=1` | (Triforce) Each texture page `RX_VK_SHADOW_KEEP` or `RX_VK_SHADOW_PREV` left uncopied is compared with the capture's copy again after the capture is taken, and the hashes kept with the shadow's copies are hashed again: the `texture shadow check` line every 600 captures counts the copies that differ and the hashes that are not of their bytes, both of which must be 0. Slow: for the lab's checks. Unset or `0`: not checked |
| `RX_VK_HOLD_MS=<ms>` | Hold the last picture (`gpu/vk/present_hold.h`, on by default): a new texture a draw samples after the frame's uploads took their budget (`RX_VK_UPLOAD_NEW_MS`, `RX_VK_UPLOAD_NEW_MB`) is uploaded at the next frame, the dummy drawn in its place, and that frame is not shown: the window gets the last complete picture again, the Insert menu, the counters and the notices drawn over it, until a frame is drawn with every texture complete. The game runs on underneath. No frame generation makes a frame from a held one, and the one shown after a hold starts its history anew (and the temporal upscalers theirs). `<ms>`: the hold's bound (250); at it the rest are uploaded at once and the next frame is shown whatever it holds, and no hold comes for 120 presents (twice as long after each hold at its bound that follows, up to 1920). `0`: no hold, no new texture left for later: the one frame that takes them all, as before. The log's `vk: hold:` lines, the stall line and the 600-frame line count them |
| `RX_VK_HOLD_PIPELINES=0` | A frame with draws or dispatches left out because their program, pipeline or compute pipeline was still being made is held as one with a texture still owed its bytes (`RX_VK_HOLD_MS`'s hold, with its bound and its rests; NX2navi's frame hold, `gpu/vk/pipe_wait.h`): by default the window keeps the last complete picture instead of showing one that lacks a pass. `0`: such frames are shown, as before |
| `RX_VK_UPLOAD_NEW_MS=<ms>`, `RX_VK_UPLOAD_NEW_MB=<MB>` | The new textures' budget a frame while the hold is on: once the frame's uploads took 12 ms or 64 MB, a new texture is left for the next frame (ASTC ones too; the frame's first upload always goes). 12 ms keeps an area change's frames near 16.7 ms with their draws and memory sync; 64 MB is what the GPU copies or decodes in a few milliseconds, so that no frame leaves it a backlog. `0`: that half of the budget off |
| `RX_VK_UPLOAD_SPLIT_MB=<MB>` | A new layered ASTC texture whose texels are more than this (128) has its first upload in parts while the hold is on, the texture's layers over at most 6 frames (`gpu/vk/layer_split.h`): its draws sample the dummy and its frames are held until the last part is in; for a dispatch or at the hold's bound what is left goes at once. In the owner's test7 session one 1365 x 1365 x 90 texture kept as BC3 took one frame's upload command buffer 114.8 ms of GPU time. `0`: whole, as before. The stall line counts the parts |
| `RX_VK_DUMMY_GEN=0` | A frame that left textures for later makes every texture view a draw resolved stale (`destroy_gen`), as before. By default only the looks that resolved to the dummy for want of their texture are (`texture_dummy_generation`, `vk_draw.cpp` `texture_view_of`); the stall line counts the views resolved anew |
| `RX_VK_VIEW_KEEP=0` | Any texture's end (an eviction, a dead render target let go, a texture made BC3 from BC1, the compaction moving one) makes every texture look of every recording thread stale, and each is resolved anew when next met (the slot, the cache, the view, under the parallel replay's lock), as before. By default a thread that finds the destroy generation moved keeps the looks of the textures whose views did not go: those are noted by their creation number before the generation moves (`gpu/vk/view_keep.h`, `vk_draw.cpp` `texture_view_of`); never a look of no texture or no view, nor a flat texture's 3D copy. More than 1024 gone since a thread's last pass: all resolved anew. The log's `vk: texture looks kept` lines count them, the stall line the views resolved anew. After NX2navi's `tex_gen_undo` |
| `RX_VK_DESC_KEEP=0` or `check` | The per-binding reuse of a draw's descriptors (`gpu/vk/desc_keep.h`, on by default): a draw that builds its descriptors takes, for each sampled texture, user uniform buffer, stage's constants and read-only storage buffer, what the program's last draw on that thread resolved it to, while everything it was resolved from is the same: the texture's handle, its slot's registration, the samplers', no texture destroyed or made again, no texture left for later and the same submission; the buffer's address and size, no inline uniform update, no pool moved or mirror made, the same recording. `0`: every descriptor resolved, as before (so also with `RX_VK_NO_DESC_REUSE`, `RX_VK_SAMPLER_CACHE=0` and the descriptor diagnostics). `check`: every descriptor resolved too, and one that comes out otherwise than what was kept for the same inputs is counted and logged (`vk: descriptor reuse check`): the way to try the rules. The frame statistics' `a frame's descriptors` line says how many were resolved and taken, how many pushes were whole, of the changed bindings or of nothing, and how many sets were written, through a template, or bound again |
| `RX_VK_DESC_SIG=0` or `check` | The whole-draw skip by the signature of a draw's descriptor inputs (`gpu/vk/desc_sig.h`, on by default with the per-binding reuse): a draw whose bindings moved, of the program whose descriptors its last draw pushed in that command buffer, first reads what each of them is resolved from (a texture's handle and its slot's version, a user uniform buffer's address and size, a read-only storage buffer's address and size words, a driver bank's stage generation) and the stamps the per-binding reuse gives each kind; when that equals the signature the pushed descriptors were built from, and their build kept every one, the build and the push are left out (a build would take each of them again and push nothing). Push programs only, of the kinds the per-binding reuse keeps: not those with storage buffers they write, tables, texel buffers or storage images, not the mesh fallback or RenoDX's. `0`: built, as before. `check`: built too, and one that comes out otherwise than what is pushed is counted and logged (`vk: descriptor signature check`). The frame statistics' `a frame's descriptor signatures` line says how many draws read theirs and how many were kept whole |
| `RX_VK_DESC_COUNTS=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's DESC-22, after Source A's; `gpu/vk/desc_counts.h`) `build_descriptors` walks the program's stages at every build to sum its buffers, images and texel views, as before. The default keeps the three sums packed on the program (`Program::desc_n`, 10 bits each, in the padding after `ready` so that `sizeof(Program)`, which the shader caches' stamp holds, stays what it is): stored at the program's first build once it is translated, read at every later one. A program with more than 1023 of a kind walks at every build. A build that went by kept counts and had one of its three vectors move is made again, walked, and that program walks from then on (one log line). The lab's knob `descwalk` (`RX_VK_LAB_AB`) is this switch in its phases |
| `RX_VK_PROF_ALL=1` (the dispatch's pick clock) | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's DESC-30, after Source A's and Source B's) Every profiling scope timed, and with it the clock pair around each compute dispatch's program pick, as before. Unset, the pick's time for the stall line is the change of the thread's exactly timed nanoseconds across the pick (its slow path is timed by `PROF_EXACT`), so a pick the memo answers reads no clock. DESC-30 has no switch of its own, the results being the same: besides the pick, `taau_key` answers from its list parsed once (it wrote the key out and walked the list as text several times a dispatch), `RX_VK_LOG_PUSH` formats its key only when set, the first-pipeline clock is read only when there is one to make, and `texture_frame` fills `RX_VK_LOG_BAKED`'s list only for that switch (read once) and finds its largest textures by a partial sort. The lab's knob `diagold` (`RX_VK_LAB_AB`) puts all of it back in its phases (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `dispatch`, `taau_key`, `dispatch_diag_old`; `gpu/vk/vk_texture.cpp` `texture_frame`) |
| `RX_VK_LAB_AB=e_draw:<ns>`, `e_dispatch:<ns>`, `d_present_after:<us>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's REPLAY-51, after Source B's; lab knobs of the in-run A/B, `gpu/vk/lab_ab.h`, nothing without the variable) The `d_<site>` knobs wait once a frame; `e_draw` and `e_dispatch` wait the knob's nanoseconds at every draw or dispatch, first in `draw()` and `dispatch()` so the profiles leave it out, on whichever thread records, and count each wait. The frame's change against the time spun says how much of that path is on the frame's critical path (200 ns at about 3,050 draws a frame is 0.6 ms of work). A phase's end logs `vk: lab A/B: phase e_draw:<ns> over:` with its waits and ms a second. `d_present_after` is `d_present`'s control: the same wait just after the present's `drain(0)`, where `d_present` is just before it. Pin `RX_VK_PARALLEL` with the `e_` knobs (the log warns otherwise): Automatic moves draws between threads. Off: one relaxed load a draw. `tests/test_lab_ab_each.py` |
| `HOS_OOT_SHADOW_STEP=<n>` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's GAME-05) The game gives each nearby character or object a square depth shadow map sized by its size on screen, 384 to 1,920 a side, so while anything moves each frame asks for depth textures of sides not seen before. The one read of the side is rounded up to a multiple of `<n>` (a power of two up to 256; 64 when not set): 25 sizes, which come round again, so the render targets' pool serves them. A map gets at most 63 more texels a side; its projection does not change. `0` or `1`: the game's own side, as before (`host/oot_shadow_step.h`, `host/oot_shadow_step.c`, `nx2/oot_patches.py` `patch_shadow_step`) |
| `HOS_SHADOW_BUCKET=<step>` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's TEX-85) The game sizes each character's shadow map (square, D32, one level) from the camera's distance every frame and places a frame's render targets first fit in its target heap, so a map a few pixels larger moved every target after it, and each moved one is a new image to the renderer. Such a map wider than 384 is answered the storage size and alignment of the next multiple of the step up (384: sides 768, 1152, 1536, 1920); the texture's own size and layout do not change, only the bytes the game sets aside, by 1.8 to 5.3 MB a map. `0`: every map its own size, as before. Not set: 384, and 0 where the target heap is pinned (`HOS_RT_POOL_MB`, or a 5120-wide output's 900 MB). The log says the step and counts the stepped answers (`host/shadow_slot.h`, `host/nvn_native.c` `shadow_slot_layout`) |
| `HOS_OOT_TEX_COMPACT_MS=<n>` | (the lab's) The game's texture data compaction (its `aglTextureDataMgr::Compaction` thread moves a texture's data in the game's heap, a cycle of about 18 frames; idle in a fresh Market, at work at every ask after a tour, where each move is a texture at a new address for the renderer to capture and check): at most one cycle started every n ms. `-1` (a test): none started. Unset or 0: as shipped. The cycles started are logged every 10 s either way ("oot: texture compaction in"). `host/oot_tex_compact.c`, `tests/test_oot_tex_compact.py` |
| `HOS_OOT_TEX_ASK_MS=<n>` | (Triforce) The game's texture data manager asks for a compaction round (above) at most once every n ms: its step, every frame, asks whenever no round is asked for and its flag says so, and each ask let through can start a round that moves a texture to a new address. Unset (or not a number): 1000. `0`: every ask, as shipped. `off`: none. A round started always finishes; an ask held back is made again at the next step. The asks let through and held are logged every 10 s ("oot: texture compaction asks in"). `host/oot_tex_compact.h`, `nx2/oot_patches.py` `patch_tex_ask`, `tests/test_oot_tex_compact.py` |
| `RX_VK_TEX_WARM=1` | Textures made and uploaded ahead, while the frames are light (`gpu/vk/tex_warm.h`; off unless set). A texture is made and uploaded where a draw first samples it, and the frame's budget for that (12 ms, 64 MB, the picture held) applies only to targets drawn in the frame before, so an area's first frame, whose targets are new, takes every texture of the area at once: 350 to 800 uploads of 250 to 390 MB, 100 to 180 ms, in the lab's tours. With the switch every registered slot is noted, and at the start of a game's submit that follows a light frame (under 400 draws and dispatches: a loading screen, a menu) the noted textures, 8 frames old at least, are made and uploaded, 6 ms a frame. Bytes the game writes afterwards are uploaded again by the write marks, as before. "vk: textures made ahead in light frames" counts them. `tests/test_tex_warm.py` |
| `RX_VK_TEX_WARM_LIST=1` or `2` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's TEX-23, after Source A's; off unless set, and only with `RX_VK_TEX_WARM=1`) The textures made ahead by a list of what was drawn (`gpu/vk/tex_warm.h`). Every registered slot made ahead needs the 40% rule, and on an 8 GB card that rule stops the warming at once. With the switch, a BC or ASTC texture a draw uses within 90 s of the game making it is learnt: its shape and eight 64-byte samples of its bytes, at its storage's address. The list is kept beside the game as `triforce_textures_drawn.bin` (32,768 at most; saved every 300 presents when it changed; a file cut short or of another version is not read). At a light frame the ripe slots the list names are made while the textures and 256 MB more fit their budget and the render targets' pool is not being given back; `1`: the others after them, by the 40% rule, once no noted slot is left ripe; `2`: the listed alone. The look itself learns nothing. "vk: textures made ahead by the list" counts listed, unlisted, learnt and no-room. Unset or `0`: every registered slot as above. Not measured: lab proof first (the entry's own default is `1`). `tests/test_tex_warm_list.py` |
| `HOS_MEM_LIMIT=1` or `<MB>` | (the lab's) The game's memory held to what it is told it has, as the console holds it. The game is told 9809 MB, but nothing was refused until the PC's own commit ran out, and it holds 11.7 GB by its own count in a fresh Market and 13.5 GB after a tour (the `mem: census` line), with its GPU heap within 15 MB of full and its texture compaction running every frame. `1`: `AllocateMemoryPages` and `MapPhysicalMemory` are refused past the 9809 MB, and `UsedMemorySize` is the game's count; a number above 1: refused past that many MB. Unset or 0: as before. What the game does when refused is what the lab is to find out. `host/hos_mem.c`, `tests/test_mem_limit.py` Refused, the game asks again thousands of times, each ask a region taken and freed; a region of the game's own freed with none of its pages in it is no longer discarded (`hos_region_discard_needed`), and the regions it frees with pages in them are discarded with the thread's virtual core given up. Those two were the stutter when walking with the limit on: in a three-area lab run the game's own part of the frame was over 8 ms in 24% of the frames before and in 0.3% after (as without the limit), 63.8 fps to 100.0. **`=1` does not fit this build:** the heap takes 6.9 GB of the 9.8 and an area's resources need 4.7 GB and more, so a save's load can starve and stay on its loading screen for good (the owner's, 2026-11-20, read with the hang watch's stacks: the game at 9,807 MB, its load threads idle). Use a size in MB: `12800` loaded and played four areas without reaching it. |
| `RX_VK_DESC_SET_KEEP=0`, `RX_VK_DESC_TEMPLATE=0` | A program over the driver's push limit (32 descriptors on NVIDIA's, AMD's and Intel's drivers) has a set written for each draw that builds its descriptors. By default a set whose descriptors are all as those of the last one written for that layout in the recording is bound again instead, while no texture was destroyed or made again and no pool moved, and a set written is written through the program's descriptor update template (Vulkan 1.1). (Triforce, after NX2navi's `desc_template`) A program within the push limit has its descriptors pushed whole (in a new command buffer, or after another program at its bind point) through a push descriptor template of its own (`vkCmdPushDescriptorSetWithTemplateKHR`, one a program and bind point, from the same entries and packed array as a set's), not with the write structs the driver walks one by one; a push of the changed bindings only keeps its write structs. The frame statistics' `a frame's descriptors` line counts the pushes made through it. With `RX_VK_RECORDER` the packet copies the packed array (`gpu/vk/vk_recorder.h`). `RX_VK_DESC_SET_KEEP=0`: a new set each time; `RX_VK_DESC_TEMPLATE=0`: sets written and pushes made with the write structs; both as before |
| `RX_VK_CREATE_NEW_MS=<ms>` | While the hold is on, a sampled texture seen for the first time once the frame's makes took this long (8), or its uploads their budget, is not made yet (`vk_texture.cpp` `create_later`): the draw samples the dummy, the frame is held, the next frame makes it. The frame's first make always goes; never a dispatch's. `0`: made at once, as before (a texture left for later was made all the same). The stall line counts the textures made, their time, those not made yet, and the images made |
| `RX_VK_UNSWIZZLE_MT_MB=<MB>` | A block-linear level unswizzled on the CPU is split over several threads (a thread for each 2 MB, 8 at most) from this size (4); `16`: from 16 MB, as before |
| `RX_VK_PROGRAM_READAHEAD=<n>` | After a draw reads a program from the program cache itself, or takes one read ahead, workers read the next `n` programs (32) and keep them made for the draws that come to them, 512 at most (`gpu/vk/program_readahead.h`). `0`: none, as before. The log's `vk: programs from the cache` lines and the stall line count them |
| `RX_VK_PROGRAM_READ_UNLOCKED=0` | A draw that reads a program from the program cache itself, or waits for one the read-ahead's worker is reading, lets the shaders' lock go meanwhile, so that the replay's helper threads go on picking their own programs (they queued behind it: "the program picks waited 169 ms for the shaders' lock" at the Market's arrival); a second draw of that program waits for the first's result, and the record stays listed until it is read (`gpu/vk/program_read_once.h`). A program's release waits until no pick has the lock let go. `0`: read and waited for under the lock, as before. The stall line counts the waits for another draw's read, and the picks' waits to take the lock again with their waits for it |
| `RX_VK_STAGE_CODE_CACHE=<MB>` | What a program pick made of a stage's bytes (the control section and the code area read from guest memory: its code key, the instructions lowered to Maxwell) is kept by those bytes, 64 MB at most, the least recently used going first: a program object met for the first time whose stages' bytes were met before (the game makes them again at loads) takes a copy instead of decoding them again under the shaders' lock (`gpu/vk/stage_code_cache.h`; an entry is taken only for the same bytes, byte for byte). The save's load-in frame said "the program picks read the stages' code 45.6 ms": a stage was some 60 us to read and decode, and is 3 us to find. `0`: none, each stage decoded again for each program object, as before. The log's `vk: the stages' code read for the program picks` lines count them |
| `RX_VK_STAGE_CODE_LAZY=0` | A program pick reads the stages of a program object it meets for the first time without lowering their code: their keys, NVN stages and headers (what finds the program) come from the control sections and code areas, and the code is lowered (and kept, `RX_VK_STAGE_CODE_CACHE`) only when the pick translates a variant. A program found in memory, read ahead or in the program cache was made from stages that lower (its key names them); one not found has its stages lowered before it is translated, and should one not lower, the pick starts again from those that do: the program it was before (`gpu/vk/stage_code_cache.h`). The save's load-in frame lowered every stage of each program object met, 48 of a stage's 60 us, for programs all prepared before the title. Not with `RX_VK_REJECT_STAGES`, `RX_VK_REJECT_KEYS`, `RX_VK_OLD_LOWERING`, `RX_VK_DUMP_NATIVE` or `RX_VK_DUMP_FAILED`, which act in the lowering. `0`: each stage lowered as it is read, as before. The log's `vk: the stages' code read for the program picks` lines count the stages read without lowering and those lowered since |
| `RX_VK_STAGE_CODE_UNLOCKED=0` | A program pick reads the stages of a program object it meets for the first time (guest memory, their keys, the stage code cache) with the shaders' lock let go, as a draw's own read of a program (`gpu/vk/program_read_once.h`: a program's release waits until no pick has the lock let go), so that the replay's helper threads go on picking their own programs meanwhile; two picks of one object at once each read it, and the first list put stands. `0`: read under the lock, as before. The stall line's "the program picks read the stages' code" counts the read and the lock's taking again |
| `RX_VK_PROGRAM_ORDER=0` | Each session writes the order its draws first needed their programs in (read from the program cache, or translated), the first 4096, into `triforce_program_order.bin` beside the cache now and then (this session's first, then those of the sessions before it has not met). The next session's read-ahead workers, from the first draw on (the title and the file select), read those programs in that order whenever no draw asks them for anything, the whole order, and keep them made apart from the shelf until a draw takes them (`RX_VK_PROGRAM_ORDER_KEEP` below; `gpu/vk/program_order.h`): the save's load-in frame read some 100 programs on the draws' threads before. Not with the RAM low. `0`: neither read nor written, as before. The stall line says why the draws read what they still read themselves (named by no order, read ahead and let go before, not read ahead yet) and how many they took in this order; the log says once when the whole order has been gone through, and how many programs it keeps |
| `RX_VK_PROGRAM_ORDER_KEEP=0` | A program read ahead in the last sessions' order stays until a draw takes it, whatever the draws meet meanwhile, and nothing in the order is passed by: the workers read all of it (4096 at most) while the title and the file select stand still, and a program a draw's ask read that the order names is kept with these, not on the shelf, which lets its oldest go. Only a RAM gone short lets them go (all of them). The pipelines' warm keeps its window: the first 512 kept and not taken are read with it, those beyond without, each warmed as the draws take warmed ones (the nearest first). Some 40 to 50 KB a program packed: the owner's order of 2411 programs some 100 to 120 MB, 4096 at most some 200 MB. The owner's test37 session (SDR, where the order's sessions were HDR Native) took another path through the order and its load-in frame read 115 programs itself, 64 of them read ahead and let go as behind where the draws were, 51 passed by. `0`: the rules before: 512 at most, each until the draws are 256 places past it (where the draws are: the middle place of the last 16 programs they needed first), what lies behind not read |
| `RX_VK_PROGRAM_READAHEAD_NOTES=0` | The read-ahead's next programs are those after it in the pipeline notes' order (the order earlier sessions first built their pipelines: an area's programs lie together), 4 workers, the compute programs too (in the order play first dispatched them, `triforce_computes.bin`), and a full shelf lets its oldest go; the program cache file's order only for a program neither names. `0`: the file's order (since the shaders are prepared before the title, the preparation's), one worker, no compute programs, and nothing more read once 512 wait, as before (test16's file select load took 7 of some 600 programs read ahead that way) |
| `RX_VK_PIPELINE_WARM=0` or `1` | Where the precompile keeps no pipelines (the RAM spared) and the fast pipeline link is on, the read-ahead's worker that has just read a program makes, before any draw can have it, the stage libraries of the pipelines the pipeline notes name for it (asked of the caches only: one the driver would have to compile is left to its draw) and links them; the draw takes the link made in place of linking its own, and the whole build follows as before (`gpu/vk/pipe_warm.h`). At most 32 notes a program variant, 512 links held for draws that have not come (the oldest let go beyond) and 1024 libraries no draw has had; nothing made while the RAM is low or short, and what is held goes back to the driver while it is short. `0`: none, as before; `1`: the libraries only. The log's `vk: pipelines made behind the draws` lines count them |
| `RX_VK_PIPELINE_WARM_FOLLOW=1` | Off by default (test19: the lab's tour showed no gain). `1`: the warm follows the draws: a program read within 64 places (in the notes' order) after one the draws consumed in the last 2 s has its libraries made and linked, one within 192 its libraries only, one farther (or once the draws stopped taking) nothing; and the libraries no draw has had are kept up to 2048, the oldest let go beyond (with the links made from it), so the bound never stops the warm (`gpu/vk/pipe_warm.h`, test18: 2266 links made, 276 taken, the libraries at their bound from the load on); it spends at most 250 ms of the workers' time a second (500 saved up), and never makes again a library it let go. Off: every program read is warmed whole and the libraries stop at 1024, as before |
| `RX_VK_COMPUTE_WARM=1` | Off by default (test19: the lab's tour showed no gain). `1`: where the precompile keeps no pipelines, the read-ahead's worker that has just read a compute program play dispatched (`triforce_computes.bin`) makes its compute pipeline, asked of the caches only, as its first dispatch would; the dispatch that takes the program has it. One a program while it waits on the read-ahead's shelf, destroyed when the shelf lets the program go and given back while the RAM is short; once a program variant a session. Off: each compute pipeline is made at its first dispatch, as before |
| `RX_VK_PIPELINE_ORDER=0` | Each session writes the order its draws first needed their pipelines in (a new one, or one the precompile kept that no draw had), the first 4096, into `triforce_pipeline_order.bin` beside the pipeline notes now and then, from the presents on a thread of its own (this session's first, then those of the sessions before it has not met). Where the pipeline warm runs (`RX_VK_PIPELINE_WARM`), the next session finds each one's note before play and the warm makes, for a program variant the read-ahead has read, the pipelines that order names for it and no other (32 at most a variant; the notes' other pipelines are not made then, and the warm does not follow the draws): their stage libraries asked of the caches and their links, held for the draws by their place in the order, 1024 at most (beyond, the farthest ahead goes, and none is made farther), and 2048 libraries no draw has had at most. Where the draws are: the middle place of the last 16 pipelines they needed first; a link held 512 places behind it goes with the libraries only it held, and none is made there (`gpu/vk/pipe_order.h`): the save's load-in frame made 86 to 92 ms of pipelines on the draws' threads, the warm's links from the notes let go before the load came (lab round 37: 1964 made, 54 taken). The first session after this writes the file; the next one has it. `0`: neither read nor written, the warm from the notes as before. The stall line counts the pipelines new to the draws, those linked ahead (of them in this order), and the links and stage libraries the draws made themselves; the log's `vk: pipelines made ahead in the order` lines the rest |
| `RX_VK_PIPE_AHEAD=<places>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PIPE-81, after Source B's: its first step; `gpu/vk/pipe_ahead.h`) A count, nothing built: with the last sessions' pipeline order in use (light mode), each pipeline the draws first need is counted by its place against where the draws are: within this many places ahead (1024 unset, at most 4096), further ahead, behind, not in the order, and those within the window that the warm had linked. A line every 600 presents while it grows (`vk: pipelines first drawn, by their place in the order: ...`) says whether a builder of whole pipelines just ahead of the draws would meet a third of them or more, the entry's rule for building it at all. `0`: not counted (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `order_met`) |
| `RX_VK_VRAM_MB=<MB>` | The texture cache's budget as a fixed size, whatever the card. Unset: on a card the driver allows more than 8.5 GB, no size (`RX_VK_TEX_PRESSURE`); on a smaller one 45% of what the driver allows; with `RX_VK_TEX_PRESSURE=0` 70% of it above 8.5 GB, at most 6 GB (the rule before) |
| `RX_VK_TEX_PRESSURE=0` or `=<pct>` | (Triforce, after NX2navi's `TEX_VRAM_PRESSURE`) On a card the driver allows more than 8.5 GB, with VK_EXT_memory_budget, not an integrated GPU, without `RX_VK_VRAM_MB` and without a quality preset whose budget applies, the texture budget follows the live use instead of the fixed 6 GB: textures go once the driver's count, less the free space in the blocks they can take back, is past 80% of what the driver allows now, the least recently used first, each unused 2 s or more, 64 a pass, down to 2% under that line (`gpu/vk/tex_pressure.h`); the 90% and 97% rules and the compaction's lines stay. While the system's commit is low the fixed size comes back (`RX_VK_TEX_COMMIT_FREE_MB`). `0`: the fixed size, as before; `<pct>`: that line (50 to 95). The log's `vk: texture budget` line at the start says which, and the `vk: VRAM:` line every 600 presents the budget (by live use: what the textures may hold before the line) and the line |
| `RX_VK_TEX_KEEP_TAKEN=0` | (Triforce, after NX2navi's `TEX_KEEP_TAKEN`) While VRAM is short, a texture uploaded from memory that the game let go 30 presents ago goes first (`RX_VK_LET_GO_PCT`); one the game has taken back before waits 8 times the longest it stayed let go before a take-back, when that is longer (`gpu/vk/tex_pressure.h`): made again, the frame that wants it draws the dummy (NX2navi: Kakariko's haze gone every few seconds). The eviction by age still takes it. `0`: the 30 presents alone, as before |
| `RX_VK_TEX_COME_BACK=0` | (Triforce, after NX2navi's pause for drops that come back) Where the budget follows the live use: when in 240 presents 64 or more textures the line let go went and half or more of them were made again within 600, the line stands aside for 600 presents, doubled at each such period up to 7200; a period with fewer back ends the doubling (`gpu/vk/tex_pressure.h` `ComeBack`). The 90% and 97% rules and the compaction's lines still hold meanwhile. The log says when a pause begins. `0`: no pause |
| `RX_VK_TEX_STEP=<n>` | The live textures one pass of `texture_sweep` over the budget lets go of at most (64 where the budget follows the live use; the render targets drawn over since do not count), the rest at the next look two presents on, not ten. Set, on any card, the fixed size's too; `0`: no step, one cut as before |
| `RX_VK_TEX_COMMIT_FREE_MB=<MB>` | Where the budget follows the live use: while less of the system's commit than that is free (the default: the larger of 4 GB and a tenth of the limit, NX2navi's), the fixed size is the budget again (Windows charges a discrete card's memory to the commit, and an allocation that fails there ends the game). `0`: never. Not on Linux |
| `RX_VK_TEX_COMMIT_RESERVE_MB=<MB>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's OS-05, after Source A's; off unless set, not measured here) On any card, while less of the system's commit than that is free (the page files' room to grow counted in, below), the textures are held to what they hold less the shortfall, never under 1 GB, as the 97% rule takes its excess: the cache's passes give blocks back before an allocation is refused at Windows' commit limit (`HOS_COMMIT_WAIT_MS` waits such a refusal out). Source A's is 2560, on by default there; one `vk: commit limit near` line when it begins and ends and every 600 presents while it holds. Not set or `0`: no cut, as before. Not on Linux (`gpu/vk/commit_room.h`, `gpu/vk/vk_texture.cpp` `texture_frame`) |
| `RX_VK_TEX_COMMIT_GROWTH=0` or `=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, OS-05) Whether the room the page files still have to grow (each one's largest size less its size now, a drive's page files capped together by its free space, asked of Windows at most once a second) counts as free commit, for the reserve above and for `RX_VK_TEX_COMMIT_FREE_MB`'s fall-back alike: Source A's first rule, without it, retired textures for 90 minutes while a page file Windows manages could still grow. Read only while less is free than the higher of the two lines. Not set: counted where `RX_VK_TEX_COMMIT_RESERVE_MB` is set, else not (as before); `0`: never; `1`: also without the reserve (`vk_texture.cpp` `commit_now`, `page_file_growth`) |
| `RX_VK_SWEEP_ALL=1` | Every texture of the cache ordered by its last use for every pass of `vk_texture.cpp` `texture_sweep`, as before. By default a pass under the budget (every 10 presents, and the dead render targets' passes between them) orders only the textures it can take: those the GPU drew and those the game let go; the others it passes over all the same. Over the budget every texture is ordered. The log's "passes for the dead render targets between the looks" line says what a pass costs |
| `RX_VK_TEX_RATE_HIGH=0` | The texture eviction's waits (`vk_texture.cpp` `texture_sweep`: 60 presents unused over the budget, 30 after the game let a texture go, 600 or 3600 for one the GPU drew, the compaction's 600 and 1800) count presents above 60 a second, as before. By default they count their seconds at any rate up to 480 a second (`gpu/vk/present_rate.h`, after NX2navi's M19): at the Market's 94 a second "60 presents" were 0.64 s. Below 60 they count seconds unless `RX_VK_TEX_RATE_LOW=0`. The log says the first time a run goes above 68 a second |
| `RX_VK_TEX_DETAIL=full`, `reduced` or `low` | The texture detail for one run, over the Insert menu's Texture detail row: whole textures, or the large ones without their largest level, or without two (`gpu/vk/tex_detail.h`). Unset, the row decides, and its Automatic goes by what the driver allows: full from 10,240 MB, low under 6,144 MB, never full on an integrated GPU |
| `RX_VK_TEX_BC1_PARTS=0` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's TEX-50) An ASTC block of two to four partitions with a colour endpoint mode each is refused unread by the opaque-texture scan, as before, so a texture with one stays BC3. The default reads those modes as the decoder does (their bits below the weights) and takes the block as opaque when every partition's mode has no alpha and is not HDR; the blocks the decoder takes for an error are still refused. The `kept as BC1` log lines count the blocks read this way. And a first start, with no options file, now keeps opaque textures as BC1 as the row's default says: it started at off (`gpu/vk/astc_opaque.h`, `vk_menu.cpp` `tex_bc1_at_start`) |
| `RX_VK_TEX_BC1=0/1` | Opaque ASTC textures kept as BC1 instead of BC3 for one run, or not, over the Debug tab's Opaque textures as BC1 row (on by default since options_version 5): a texture the renderer keeps as BC3 whose every block the GPU decoder writes opaque is encoded as BC1, half the video memory, the same texels (`gpu/vk/tex_bc1.h`, `gpu/vk/astc_opaque.h`). One whose memory later holds blocks with alpha, or that a copy on the GPU writes, becomes BC3 for good; the log counts both |
| `RX_VK_ASTC_BC_ALL=1` or `0` | Large decoded ASTC textures are kept as BC3, a quarter of RGBA8: the coarse blocks (6 x 6 texels and up) from 2 MB as RGBA8 (`RX_VK_ASTC_BC_MB`), the finer ones (UI, characters) only from 64 MB. Where the driver allows under 8.5 GB the finer ones go from 2 MB too (`gpu/vk/astc_bc_policy.h`). (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's TEX-12, after Source A's) And on every integrated GPU, whatever it is allowed: its allowance is a share of the system's RAM (a 16 GB handheld reported 10,361 MB), so it kept those textures as RGBA8 in RAM the game needs too. The log says `all large ASTC textures kept as BC3`, with `an integrated GPU` there. `1`: on any card; `0`: the finer ones as RGBA8 on any card, which is an integrated GPU allowed 8.5 GB or more as it was before |
| `RX_VK_BC_SMALL_LAYERS=<n>` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's TEX-09) The layers one run of the ASTC-to-BC3 re-encode takes for a level of 1024 x 1024 or less, 1 to 8, through a small scratch of its own (1024 x 1024 x 8, 32 MB, and 4 MB more of output buffer). Unset: 8, and 1 on AMD's cards until the picture is checked there (one layer a run was for dark lines between Hyrule Field's ground tiles on an RX 6600). A terrain array of 90 layers and 11 levels was about 990 runs of a decode, an encode, a copy and three full barriers; its levels from the second down are now about 210. Each layer of a run is copied by a region of its own. No small scratch at reduced or low texture detail. `1`: none, every run one layer, as before (`gpu/vk/bc_scratch.h`, `vk_texture.cpp` `bc_small_layers`, `bc_encode_into`) |

Its bound is a twelfth of what the driver allows, 256 to 768 MB (`<MB>` sets it; 0: no pool); it was a fixed 256, which starved at 2560 x 1440. It is trimmed oldest first, whatever the shape. A shape nothing has taken from the pool goes after 8 presents (`RX_VK_POOL_IDLE`), one that was taken before after 120. Above 90% of the allowance it holds half its bound, above 97% nothing, unless a floor of the targets' blocks is kept. `RX_VK_TARGET_POOL_TRIM=0`: emptied above 90%, as before; with `RX_VK_TARGET_POOL_MB=256 RX_VK_POOL_IDLE=120` the pool as it was. | `RX_VK_TARGET_REUSE_MEMORY=1` / `=2`, `RX_VK_TARGET_TYPE_CHECK=0` | (Triforce) Off by default. `=1`: a render target whose shape misses the pool takes the memory of a pooled image it fits in (its memory type and alignment, at least its size and at most 1.5 times it, waited a present), where it was a new allocation; on an integrated GPU the pool's short wait is then 64 presents, not 8 (`RX_VK_POOL_IDLE` still sets it). `=2`: also makes the pooled kind on allocations of a size class (at most a quarter over the size rounded to 64 KiB), so the next one a little larger fits; not while VRAM is short. OoT sizes each character's shadow map anew every frame. Measured on a Steam Deck OLED at Render 50% in a Hyrule Field camera turn (`=2`): 33.6 fps against 32.6, the 1% low 15.7 against 13.1; not separated at Render 100%, no gain on a Steam Deck LCD, not run on a discrete card. The line `vk: render targets by size` says what it took and why it missed (`target_pool.h`, `vk_texture.cpp` `pool_take_memory`, `vk_vma.cpp` `create_image_in_class`). On by default whatever the switch: a render target goes into the targets' blocks only when their memory type is one it can use (VMA does not check that for a pool); `RX_VK_TARGET_TYPE_CHECK=0`: not checked, as before |

An arena is taken again only once the GPU is done with the submission that read it, so frames that stage in a row use two in turn, and with 64 MB kept the one that had its turn was freed at every second present of a load and made again the present after. An idle arena staged into in the last 2 seconds now stays while the arenas add up to 256 MB (`gpu/vk/staging_keep.h`), unless VRAM is over 90% of the allowance. Nothing changes where 256 MB are kept anyway. The log's `vk: memory:` line counts the arenas made so far and their time. `0`: as before |
| `RX_VK_WROTE_REGIONS=0`, `=2` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's TEX-02) Every sampled, uploaded texture is asked once an epoch whether the GPU wrote into its storage since its upload, which took a lock and walked the map of written ranges; nearly every answer is no. One bit a 2 MB region of GPU addresses, set where a GPU write ever reached, answers no without the lock when the storage touches no set region; a set bit asks the map as before. `0`: the lock and the walk each time; `2`: a check, both asked and compared, the count of disagreements (which must be 0) logged (`gpu/vk/wrote_regions.h`, `vk_texture.cpp` `texture_gpu_wrote`, `gpu_wrote_into`) |

| `RX_VK_PREDICT_CHECK=0`, `=2` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's PIPE-68, after Source B's and Source A's; `gpu/vk/predict_check.h`) On unless `0`. The predicted pipelines are built once for the driver's own cache and a record says which programs are done; a driver can lose them all the same (NVIDIA's cache cap, a cleared cache folder, AMD's Windows driver keeping a third). At a start whose record says all are built, the builder's idle thread, after its delay and only while the frame shown is a light one, samples up to 12 of them spread over the plan: each built for the driver's cache alone and timed, then once more as the warm reference; cold is a first build over 4 times the second and 3 ms more, and the sample ends once its majority is decided. Cold: the record is removed, so they are all built again behind the game at light frames, and `<notes>.predicted.check` takes a strike for these shaders and this driver; cold again after that, the log says this driver does not keep them and no later start checks. Warm: the strike is forgotten. `2`: checked at every start, a recorded stop too. Also counted: the builds in play of programs the plan predicts that took over 20 ms (`vk: predicted pipelines: N builds in play of predicted programs took over 20 ms`, at most every 10 s while it grows). `0`: neither the check nor the counter, as before (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `predict_check_cold`, `predict_background`, `predicted_play_build`) |

Off by default because it changes which pipelines are built, and it applies to a plan made after it is set (a plan is made once for the shaders). `0`: every predicted pipeline built, as before. The predicted lines say how many were left out as built already, how many programs the programs' file did not lend, the notes of variants it has no shaders for and the layouts that did not fit (`gpu/vk/vk_predict.cpp` `predict_pipelines`, `gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `predict_first`) |

With the switch and a driver that has `VK_EXT_memory_priority`, the game ranks its memory: the render targets' blocks 1.0 (kept first), the sampled textures' 0.25 (moved out first), everything else the driver's own 0.5 (`gpu/vk/memory_priority.h`); `<targets>,<textures>` sets the first two, each from 0 to 1. The textures then come from their own pool also where it is not compacted (`RX_VK_COMPACT`), and a sparse texture's chunks name the textures' rank. The log says `vk: memory priorities on` with the three numbers, or that the driver has no such extension. Source A measured it on an RTX 4090 with the video memory taken by other programs: with 1.3 GB moved out, a median GPU frame of 24 to 29 ms against 109 to 179; with room on the card, no difference. Not run on a small card, and a driver may ignore it: off by default, and without the switch the extension is not asked for. `0`, `off`, `no` or a value that cannot be read: off |

Windows only. On AMD's driver the row alone leaves it off, as NX2navi did after games ended in play there; `=1` turns it on anyway. `=2` is the self-check: every copy is still made, the pages are protected and paid as if not, and each payment compares what it would have copied with guest memory: a `MISMATCH at guest` line is a page it would have got wrong. The log says `vk: lazy write-back on` (or why off) at the start, and every 600 submits `vk: lazy write-back (600 submits)`: the MB not copied, the pages owed, the pages paid by a fault, a host call, a GPU read, a mirror given up and a recording's submit, the payments and their time, and what failed |
| `RX_VK_LAZY_PAYERS=<1..4>` | (Triforce, 4 by default; `=1` as before) How many of the lazy write-back's payments copy at once (`gpu/vk/wb_lazy_payers.h`, NX2navi's `wb_lazy_unlocked`). A payment's copy goes onto the queue after everything submitted so far and its thread waits for it (0.5 to 3 ms each in the owner's sessions, up to 21 ms at a load); with one at a time, a second thread that touched another owed page, or the replay giving a mirror up, waited for the first one's copy to come back before its own went onto the queue. Each payment holds a slot of its own (a command buffer and a fence); the pages of two payments are never the same ones (`gpu/vk/wb_lazy_state.h`), and each copy still goes onto the one queue after everything before it. The 600-submit line `vk: lazy write-back (600 submits)` ends with the payments that waited for a free slot and the most slots in use at once |

While it draws, DLSS, FSR 3.1 and FSR 4 step aside (the game's own as far as they are concerned) and the game's SMAA is left out (`RX_VK_AA` still goes over that); not on a RenoDX run's RGBA16F scene. `RX_VK_N64_LINES` sets the grid's lines. (Step 3) The texture blur: the scene's tiled material textures sampled that many mip levels coarser (Faithful +2.5, Remembered +1.5, never past the card's `maxSamplerLodBias`), on the upscalers' scene-only path (not the shadow maps, the HUD or the output's passes); `RX_VK_N64_LOD=<levels>` over the preset's, `0` none. (Step 4) The TV at the window, in the filter's place at the window's own pixels, HUD and all (the FPS counter is drawn after it; the options' own screens go under it, below): the screen's curve, the composite colour bleed, the beam across 240 TV lines (wider where bright), the mask (Auto: the slot mask on a picture placed 1440 lines tall or more, none below), the glow from the picture at 1/8, the vignette; not on HDR Native's PQ picture, nor under DLSS frame generation, nor with `RX_VK_UPSCALE=0`. `RX_VK_N64_TV=0` leaves it out, `RX_VK_N64_MASK=<0..4>` (Auto, none, aperture grille, slot mask, shadow mask) picks the mask. The log says `vi: N64 Classic: frames paced at` at each change. (Step 8) At F7 or a change of the row, half a second of the TV switching off and on (the picture squashed to a bright line, then a dot, and opened again), the 3D, the TV and the frame rate changing at its middle (`n64_tick` once a present); not on HDR Native's picture nor under DLSS frame generation (the choice at once there), and not at a start. `RX_VK_N64_TV_ANIM=0`: the choice at once. (11-22) The options' own screens (the open menu, our tabs over the game's Options screen, the Mods entry) are drawn into a layer of the picture's place that the TV lays over the picture before its steps, so they bend, scan and bleed as the game's own screens under them (`vk_menu.cpp` `menu_tv_layer`, `vk_imgui.cpp` `record_layer`), and the glow's 1/8 copy is made again with them over the picture (else it blurred the game's own panes through their plates); the FPS counter, the notice and benchmark mode's panel stay over it, flat; with HDR on the options stay over it too (their pictures are in HDR10's codes, not the SDR picture's). The mouse is taken through the TV's curve to where the options and the game's own menus were drawn (`n64_rules.h` `screen_point`, `vk_n64.h` `n64_screen_point`; the game's through the renderer's `screen_point`). `RX_VK_N64_TV_MENU=0`: the options over the TV, flat, as before. The log says `vk: N64 Classic:` at its first grid and each new size |
| `HOS_EVIL_HD=0`, `=1`, `HOS_EVIL_HD_DIST=<1..4>` | (Triforce, Evil HD) Over the Graphics tab's Evil HD row (`evil_hd`, off unless chosen): on or off for one run, and how far its render distance goes, a factor from 1 to 4 (4 when not set; `1`: the game's distances, as before, with the row on). It draws the foliage farther: the grass and undergrowth (40 and 60 m as shipped), flowers and stones (35 m), the low trees of the distance (135 m) and their shadows (40 m), with the fades between the trees' levels, the factor times as far. They are the defaults of the game's foliage LOD settings (`game::foliage::ResLodSetting`, eight floats at main+0x3eefd38 that the game's parameter code reads by address when an area loads); `host/evil_hd.c` writes them each time the factor changes, after checking they are this game's. The foliage system builds an area's distances from them once a run, the first time it enters it, so the row applies from the start of a run (the menu asks to restart; a change mid-run reaches only the areas not visited yet). Seen 11-24 (headless, 1920x1080, Hyrule Field from the Market's drawbridge at noon): at 4x the grass tufts and small plants cover the meadows out to the ranch and up the hills, where off they stop at about 40 to 60 m; at 2.5x part of the way; with all eight at 0.5 m the ivy and grass by the Lost Woods bridge go. Cost there, uncapped on an RTX 5090 PC whose frame the CPU limits (20 s each): off 147 and 128 fps, 2.5x 128, 4x 124 and 123, about 0.8 ms a frame at 4x, the draws the same (about 2100). Areas whose data sets their own (the Graveyard, Zora's River and Domain, Hyrule Castle's flowers, Kokiri Forest handheld) keep theirs. Draw only, and not changed: the camera's far plane (25 km in play, near 0.1 m: not what limits the view; 1000 m is the title screen's camera), the model LOD coefficient (`game::gfx::ModelLodParam`: at 0.05 no visible change), the actors' presence radius (`DisplayRadius`, which creates and deletes them) and their update radius (`CalcRadius`). The log's `evil hd:` line says each change (`host/evil_hd.h`, `tests/test_evil_hd.py`) |
| `RX_VK_STUB_SAME_SIZE=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's GPUWORK-21, after Source B's and Source A's; `gpu/vk/stub_same_size.h`) On unless `0`. A gameplay upscale network that cannot run (left out or not translated: `RX_VK_NO_F16`, a card or driver that cannot build it) is stood in for by its input drawn into its output (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `upscale_stub`) also where the two are one size: `9dc98871eb7fc101`, the network of Render resolution 100%, was declined as "no larger than its input" and skipped whole, a black 3D under the HUD. Both gameplay networks now take the typed upscale first (it reads the scene's shared-exponent picture and undoes the network's square-root encoding; it was the 3/4 network's alone), then the blit, which warns of wrong colours for both, then nothing. The picture is the upscaler Off's: softer, none of the network's anti-aliasing. Only where the network cannot run. `0`: the decline, and the 3/4 network alone in the typed upscale, as before. `RX_VK_NO_UPSCALE_STUB=1` still turns the whole stub off; `RX_VK_FORCE_UPSCALE_STUB=2` sends every network through it, for a look |

Only for the one upscaler library whose layout is known, FSR SDK 2.4's `amd_fidelityfx_upscaler_vk.dll` by its SHA-256 (`gpu/vk/fsr4_models.cpp` `kKnown`, the hash `scripts/fsr_sdk.py` pins): it is loaded from its full path before AMD's loader asks for it, and its providers' IsSupported answers are hooked in this process; any other file is neither loaded nor scanned, and the row is not shown. Off unless set: FSR 4 stays off on such a GPU, as before |

Measured 8.97 -> 4.04 ms on an RX 6700 XT at 720p -> 1440p, bit for bit AMD's output on Adrenalin 26.10.1 (other drivers: the same maths, not necessarily the same bits). Where the library binds its buffer without the tail, or destroys it, AMD's own shaders are bound for that context and the log says so (`AMD's own shaders in place of the tuned ones`). `RX_VK_FSR4_UNROLL=0`: all of that but the unrolling. Frame generation's library is never tuned |
