Not set: the core is given up for the discard's own kernel call, and only with the memory limit on (`HOS_MEM_LIMIT`), as before. `0`: a freed region's thread gives its core up before the whole of the discard and takes it back once, after the region is unlisted and never under the regions' lock; the discard's own call gives it up without the limit too (`FreeMemoryPages`). Source A at its Market: core waits of 50 ms and more 287 to 19, the longest 177 to 45 ms, frames unchanged; the freeing thread once waited 124 ms for its core back, in a load. `1`: the core kept through every discard, with the limit too. With `0` the log counts such frees and the longest take-back every 1,000 (`host/hos_mem.c` `discard_core`, `hos_region_free`, `discard_pages`) |
| `HOS_SHORT_SLEEP_TIMER=1`, `=0` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's OS-31) How sleeps under a millisecond wait. Not set: with 6 or fewer logical processors in the process's mask (Windows, one processor group) the game's own short sleeps wait on the high-resolution timer, which wakes about 0.5 ms late, and the host's own yield until their time; with more, all yield, as before. The game's idle job workers sleep 20 us and look again about 7,600 times a second, and a yielding sleeper keeps a processor busy. `1` (any value but 0, off, no, false): every short sleep on the timer, as the switch did before; `0`: every short sleep yields. The log's `sleep:` line says which (`host/short_sleep.h`, `host/hos_thread.c` `sleep_for`) |
| `HOS_SHORT_SLEEP_POLLERS=<n>`, `=auto` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's OS-33, after Source A's; off unless set: it changes how the game's threads wait and was not measured here) A cap on the game threads that yield through a sleep under a millisecond at once. Where those sleeps yield (more than 6 logical processors, `HOS_SHORT_SLEEP_TIMER` not set) every idle job worker keeps a processor busy; with the cap, a game thread that would be one more than `<n>` yielding waits on the high-resolution timer for that sleep instead, about 0.5 ms late. `auto`: a quarter of the process's logical processors (2 with 8, 4 with 16, 6 with 24), Source A's rule, whose provisional numbers are 2.7% less CPU a frame at the same frame rate with 16 processors, and 1.7% of the frame rate lost in a 24-thread run held to 2. The game's MainThread and Presentation Thread and the host's own sleeps are never counted and always yield. Not set, `0`, `off`: no limit, as before. The start's `sleep: at most` line says the cap, and with `HOS_SLEEP_LOG=1` a `sleep:` line every 5 s counts the sleeps that took the timer for it (`host/short_sleep.h` `short_sleep_pollers`, `short_sleep_poll_enter`, `host/hos_thread.c` `sleep_for`) |
| `HOS_OS_YIELD=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's THREAD-41, after Source A's; off unless set: it changes how the game's threads wait and was not measured here) The game's `YieldThread` hands its virtual core to a waiting game thread of equal or better priority and then also gives the processor up (`SwitchToThread`, a call into the kernel, thousands of times a second from the game's job system). `0`: only the virtual core's hand-over, the console's yield; Windows still takes the processor at the end of a time slice. Source A, the game held to 4 processors: half of its six executors' CPU time was inside that call, and without it the mean frame was 0.26 to 0.34 ms shorter; with 16 processors no difference. The risk is a thread yielding in a loop for one that waits on its processor, on PCs with 2 to 4 logical processors. Not set or `1`: both, as before. With `HOS_VCORES=0` the processor's yield stays whatever this says; `SleepThread(0)` and the short sleeps keep theirs (`host/hos_thread.c` `os_yield`, `YieldThread`) |
| `HOS_VCORE_SPIN_US=<us>`, `HOS_VCORE_SPIN_MAX=<n>` | (Triforce, on by default; `HOS_VCORE_SPIN_US=0` as before) A game thread that waits for a virtual core it asked a holder to give up spins for it, up to 250 us, before it sleeps (`host/hos_thread.c` `vc_spin`, after NX2navi's `core_take`): the holder gives the core up at its next checkpoint, mostly within microseconds, and the waiter then runs without an OS wake. Who gets a core does not change, only how soon. At most `HOS_VCORE_SPIN_MAX` threads spin at once and the rest sleep at once: by default a quarter of the logical processors beyond 8, at most 4 (none with 8 or fewer, 1 with 12, 2 with 16). The `vcores:` line (every 5 s) counts the waits that ended in the spin and those that slept, the time spun, the spins left out for the cap, and how soon a granted core ran after a spin and after a sleep; the start's `vcores: a waiter spins up to` line says what is in effect. A yield with no thread waiting for a core takes no lock (NX2navi's yield fast path) |
| `HOS_VIDEO_ACQUIRE_TRIES=1`, `HOS_VIDEO_KEEP_CORE=1` | (Triforce, on by default; each `=1` as before) A movie's Acquire (`host/nn_video.c` `dec_acquire`) waits for a picture up to 100 ms by the clock, and not at all after a Stop or Flush with nothing submitted since: the old wait, 50 sleeps of 2 ms that each took 4-5 ms on Windows, ran out only after ~250 ms once a movie had stopped, a frame that long at every load from the title. It also decodes without the calling thread's virtual core, taken back only after the decoder's lock is let go, so other game threads run meanwhile. `HOS_VIDEO_ACQUIRE_TRIES=1`: the 50 sleeps; `HOS_VIDEO_KEEP_CORE=1`: the core held throughout |
| `HOS_VCORE_WIDE=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's GAME-22, after Source B's; off unless set, never measured there or here) A game thread whose core mask names two or more cores takes any free virtual core, not only those. Source B's reason: the game's two main threads may run on two of three cores only, where per-core job workers of better priority preempt them while the other cores stand idle. A thread with a one-core mask, which guards the game's lock-free per-core data, or with no mask, stays where it was; no two threads ever run under one core number either way. The risk is the one `HOS_VCORE_ANY=1` (every thread on any core, which crashed) has, for fewer threads: a thread that reads its core number, blocks and goes on under another. The log's `vcores: ... asks for ideal core ... and core mask` lines name every thread whose mask has two or more cores (all masks when the switch is set to anything): with no such line the switch changes nothing (`host/hos_thread.c` `vc_allowed`, `core_mask_note`) |
| `RX_VK_HELPER_SPIN_US=<us>` | (Triforce, on by default; `=0` as before) The parallel replay's spins (`gpu/vk/helper_spin.h`, after NX2navi's replay workers): a helper thread with no segment to record spins up to 100 us for the next before it sleeps, and the replay thread spins as long for a segment a helper records before it sleeps for it. The frame statistics' `its spins` line, after the parallel replay's parts, says how often each ended in the spin or slept and the time spun |
| `HOS_X3D=0` | (Windows) The game's threads and the replay's core are not kept on the die with the largest L3 cache. By default they are (the replay's core only where one is kept there: `HOS_RESERVE_CORE`), on a processor whose L3 caches differ in size and whose largest serves 8 or more cores (AMD's two-die X3D parts: a 7950X3D's or 9950X3D's 3D V-Cache die; `host/hos_x3d.h`), and the log's `cpu: logical processors ... share the largest L3 cache` line says so. On every other processor nothing changes and the log has no such line |
| `HOS_X3D=0` | (Windows) The game's threads and the replay's core are not kept on the die with the largest L3 cache. By default they are, on a processor whose L3 caches differ in size and whose largest serves 8 or more cores (AMD's two-die X3D parts: a 7950X3D's or 9950X3D's 3D V-Cache die; `host/hos_x3d.h`), and the log's `cpu: logical processors ... share the largest L3 cache` line says so. On every other processor nothing changes and the log has no such line |
| `HOS_SIM_CPU=<cores>/<threads>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's OS-49, after Source B's; a lab switch, off unless set; Windows) A PC with fewer cores, simulated: the process is held to `<cores>` of the PC's physical cores (the die with the largest L3 first, then the fastest class, then the last ones, so that the replay's kept core is among them; one core always stays out; `host/hos_sim_cpu.h`), set before any thread, and the renderer sizes its pools and thread counts by `<threads>` (`gpu/vk/cpu_count.h`: an affinity mask alone does not change `hardware_concurrency`). `6/12` and `4/8` play as those PCs' pools do; `6` alone is 6/6. The log says `cpu: HOS_SIM_CPU=` with the mask, or that it was not applied (then the variable is cleared and nothing changes). Not run in the game. `tests/test_sim_cores.py` |
| `RX_VK_SIM_GPU_SLOW=<factor>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's OS-49, after Source B's; a lab switch, off unless set) A slower GPU, simulated (`gpu/vk/sim_gpu.h`): for each ms of work the recordings measure, (factor - 1) ms more, from over 1 to 10 (`2.5` is read). A one-invocation compute loop (`shaders/sim_gpu_burn.comp`) ends the replay's own recordings between full barriers, inside the GPU time they are measured over, so the frame's GPU time and Resolution Auto see the slower card; a third timestamp a recording measures what the burn took and the difference is carried (owed time held between -50 and 100 ms, burns of 1 to 16 ms, the loop's time calibrated 0.8/0.2). A line every 10 s: work, added, the factor reached, dropped. It repeats the fast card's relative answer only: no bandwidth, driver or upscaler cost. Not run in the game. `tests/test_sim_cores.py` |
| `HOS_POWER_THROTTLING=1` | (Windows) Windows' power throttling of a window not in front left as it is (for A/B runs). By default every run, the shader preparation's too, opts out of it at the start: full speed, not the efficiency speed (EcoQoS) Windows 11 gives such a process, and the 1 ms timer resolution kept, which Windows 11 may otherwise ignore for it (Windows 10: full speed only). The log's `power:` line says what Windows took |
| `HOS_HOST_ZSTD=0` or `2`, `HOS_HOST_ZSTD_AWAY=0/1/2` | The game's files (`.zs`) are decoded by the host's zstd (`third_party/zstd`) instead of the game's own translated decoder: the host decodes what `host/host_zstd.h` takes (whole buffers, and the loading threads' streams with their content size and room for it), the game's code the rest, errors included. (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's IO-01) On when not set: the host is given the content's exact size to write into (libzstd may park literals in spare room past a block, where the game's decoder never writes), and check mode ran clean in the game. `0`: the game's own decoder for everything, as before. `2`: check, the game's code decodes everything and the host beside it, and the log's `zstd check: ... MISMATCH` and `wrote past the end` lines name every difference (its `zstd:` lines count what was compared). `HOS_HOST_ZSTD_AWAY`: which decodes give their virtual core up: `1` (not set) whole buffers with 128 KB of input or room, streams never; `0` none; `2` whole buffers and stream feeds from 64 KB, as it was. Game build `e0f3ccfa` only. The lift needs both of its calls (`nx2/oot_patches.py` `patch_host_zstd`): a build log that says `the host's zstd decoder's calls were found at 1 of 2 places` has one only, and the switch then covers only that function |

### Building

A build reads and writes tens of gigabytes. When the game folder or the build files folder is on a hard drive,
`make_game.py` warns that an SSD is much faster and builds anyway (the builders ask before they start).

| Switch | What it does |
|---|---|
| `HOOKSHOT_JOBS=<n>` (or `--jobs`) | Compilers at once (default one per CPU thread, each started when the free memory holds it; `HOOKSHOT_MEM_GATE=0` turns that check off) |
| `HOOKSHOT_LOW_PRIORITY=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's OS-57) (Windows) The builders start the build tools (`make_game.py` and everything it runs) below normal priority, as every build was before. The default is normal priority: Source A reports, without a measurement, that below normal Windows kept the steps that are one process (the read, the lift) on a hybrid Intel CPU's efficiency cores while the performance cores idled, and a first build took over an hour. The compilers then compete with the desktop like any program (`HOOKSHOT_JOBS` bounds them). Read by Triforce Setup (`setup/Setup.cs`), the launcher's build (`launcher/Logic/Build/BuildRunner.cs`) and the two older builders (`builder/HookshotBuilder/Runner.cs`, `builder/py/hookshot_builder/core.py` `build_priority`); Linux sets no nice value either way. The shaders' preparation at a build keeps its workers below normal in the normal-class process (`gpu/vk/vk_draw.cpp` `prepare_priority`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_LIFT_JOBS=<n>` | Processes that write the lifted C. Forked on Linux and macOS: default the cores, at most 16, one per 2 GB of free memory. Spawned on Windows (`nx2/emit_pool.py`), where they also cut the big chunks and share the page marks: default half the processors, at most 8, one per GB of free memory. `1`: one process, on every system; the C is the same whatever the count |
| `HOOKSHOT_KEEP_BUILD_FILES=1` | Keeps the lifted C and the objects after a build, so that the next one compiles only what changed |
| `HOOKSHOT_LIFTED_C_REGEX=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's IO-40) The lifted functions' definitions are found by the patterns' own `finditer`, tried at every line of the lifted C, as before. By default `nx2/lifted_c.py`'s `definitions` and `noted_starts` jump from one `"\nvoid f_"` to the next and try the pattern only there, for `build.py`'s memory estimate, `chunks.py`'s costs and cuts and `split_hot.py`'s pieces: the same matches (`tests/test_lifted_c_scan.py`), so the same files. `lifted_c.py`, `chunks.py` and `split_hot.py` are in the lift key: the first build with this change translates the game once more. The other half of the entry, translating while the package's romfs is still being extracted, is not here |
| `HOOKSHOT_KEEP_LIFT=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's LIFT-27) The translated game is kept as little as before: its key (what decides whether a build translates the game again) hashes every `A64_`, `OOT_` and `RX_` variable and the folders' paths, and `nx2/released_translation_stamp.json`, and any failed build forgets it. By default the key takes of the environment only what shapes the C, the lifter's `A64_` switches and `RX_TARGET` (`tests/test_lift_key_env.py` holds the lift's files to that), and leaves out the release's shader translation stamp, which `build.py` alone reads; and a build that failed in its compile, only in the program's own files (`host/`, the renderers) and in nothing the lift wrote, keeps the key once, so that the retry compiles the rest without translating the game again (a second such failure in a row forgets it, as a generated header cut short fails the program's own files too). The key changes once with this, and the two values of the switch have different keys. Not here yet: keeping the translated game after a player's finished build, which is the owner's decision (`nx2/make_game.py` `lift_key`, `keep_lift_after_failure`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_EARLY_COMPILE=0` | (Windows) Not compiled beside the lift: libopus and the two renderers, which need no translated code. By default `make_game.py` starts `build.py` with `RX_EARLY=1` for them while the game is translated (the lift's own processes take at most half the processors there), and the build proper finds their objects made |
| `HOOKSHOT_MEM_GIANT=1` or `0` | Whether the memory gate counts a function of 1.2 MB or more by the square of its size (a chunk that is one 5 MB function takes 6.2 GB, not the 1.9 GB it is counted at otherwise). By default only on a PC with less than 14 GB free when the compile starts: with more, counting the giants in full halves the compilers running at once for no need |
| `HOOKSHOT_MEM_LOG=1` | For each file compiled, `@@mem <file> <peak MB> <counted MB> <seconds>`: the most memory its compiler was seen holding, beside what the memory gate counted it at (`mem_need` in `nx2/build.py`). For fitting the gate after a change to the lift or the compiler |
| `HOOKSHOT_PROMPTS_FOLDER_KEY=0` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's IO-43) A game built from romfs and exefs folders has its button prompts drawn at every build and update, as before. The default keeps them when nothing they are made from changed: with no unpack stamp to tell the dump by, the key is the content of the game's files the tool reads (the font, the textures, the layouts, the camera's pack, the size table, the dictionaries and every language's messages). Every game's key also holds `host/prompts.c` and the tools' release (`nx2/make_game.py` `prompts_key`, `prompt_game_files`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_PROMPTS_WIDEN_EACH=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's IO-42) The button prompts' tool widens the "lines that stay" image (a 9 by 9 maximum, what a leader line pays to cross them) at every route, as before. By default the widened array is kept for the last image asked, and every route of one line is given the same image: 4 or 5 widenings a controller face where there were 35 or 37, each a filter over a 2-megapixel image (the five faces' lines on blank stand-in images: 21 s where they took 42). The lines drawn are the same (`nx2/prompt_assets.py` `guide_widened`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_DUMP_LINKS_LSTAT=1` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's IO-41) A build from romfs and exefs folders checks them for links with an `os.lstat` for every entry, as before. The default reads each entry's kind and attributes from the folder's own listing (`os.scandir`), 1.3 to 1.5 s where the one-by-one check took 36 to 49 s over the game's 186,583 files, and stops the build at a folder it cannot list, which the walk passed over. The copy that follows makes each folder once and lets `os.link` say that a file is there already (`nx2/make_game.py` `refuse_dump_links`, `link_or_copy`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_NUP_IN_PLACE=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's IO-51) A `.nup` package's unpack writes each file to `<name>.part` and renames it, and resolves the output folder and looks at every folder above the file for every file, as before. The default writes each file straight to its name (one `os.lstat`, then a create that fails if anything is there) and looks at each folder once: under a virus scanner on NTFS the temporary's create and rename and the repeated looks were most of a small file's cost over the game's 186,583 files. A file that is there whole is kept, one of another size is unlinked and written again, one whose write fails is deleted; the files written are the same (`nx2/nup.py` `extract`, `Destination`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_FINISH_COPY_ALL=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's IO-45) The end of a build copies every file into the game folder as `<name>.new` and swaps it in, as before, and keeps no note. By default the finish notes in the game folder (`.triforce_installed.json`), for each file it installed, the SHA-1 of the source and the installed copy's size, modification time, file number and mode, and the next finish passes over a file whose source hashes the same and whose installed copy is still as noted, without reading it: `image.bin` and the prebuilt DLLs at an update that did not change them. The exe and the renderer are linked again at every build with the link's time in them, so they are still copied. A file not noted, changed by hand, on a file system without file numbers (FAT, exFAT), or under a note that does not read is copied; deleting the note copies everything (`nx2/make_game.py` `INSTALLED_NOTE`, `installed_same`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_LINK_REPRO=1` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's IO-45) (Windows) The game's exe and the renderer are linked with `/Brepro`: lld writes a hash of the file where it otherwise writes the time of the link, so two links of the same objects are the same bytes, and the finish above passes an unchanged exe over instead of copying its 460 MB. Off unless set: it changes the bytes of every linked file once (`nx2/build.py` `repro_link`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_MINGW=<dir>`, `HOOKSHOT_MSVC=1` | The llvm-mingw to build with; the Visual Studio route instead |
| `HOOKSHOT_WORK_LOCK=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's IO-47) `make_game.py` takes no lock on its work folder, as before. By default it holds an OS lock on `<work>/make_game.lock` for as long as it runs (`msvcrt.locking` on Windows, `flock` elsewhere), taken after an earlier build left running is stopped and before the stale objects go: a second build on the same folder stops at once with the first one's process id instead of compiling the same objects again and racing on the staged game folder, which nothing prevented on Linux (two builders, a builder beside the launcher, `--cli`). The lock ends with its process, so a build that crashed never blocks the next; an error other than "held by another" is said and the build runs without the lock (`nx2/make_game.py` `lock_work`) |
| `HOOKSHOT_SINGLE_INSTANCE=0` | (Triforce) Hookshot Builder takes no lock on its build tools, as before. By default a second builder for the same tools folder (`builder/HookshotBuilder/Instance.cs`, named after `Tools.Root`), its `--cli` included, waits up to 5 s for one that is ending, then says so and ends: two of them built into one work folder and stopped each other's build. Triforce Setup has its own lock (`setup/Guard.cs` `Instance`) |
| `TRIFORCE_LEGACY_PATHS=1` | (Triforce) Triforce Setup keeps .NET Framework's legacy path checks, which refuse paths of 260 characters and more whatever its manifest says (an exe csc builds is taken as written for .NET 4.0). By default `setup/Setup.cs` `Main` sets the two switches 4.6.2 defaults to (`Switch.System.IO.UseLegacyPathHandling`, `Switch.System.IO.BlockLongPaths`) before any path is used, so that with Windows' own LongPathsEnabled on Setup reads and writes deep paths |
| `HOOKSHOT_SEGMENT_HEAP=0` | (Triforce, Windows; on by default since 11-22: the lab's play A/B, 81.4 against 80.6 fps) The exe's manifest asks for Windows' segment heap (`heapType SegmentHeap`, Windows 10 2004 and newer) for the whole process, its DLLs and the graphics driver too, in place of the NT heap, whose lock AMD's driver's compile threads waited on through the shader preparation (`nx2/win_manifest.py`, `tests/test_segment_heap.py`). The heap is fixed when the process starts: a build with it and one without are the two arms. `0`: the NT heap, as before. The log's `the shaders' preparation runs on` line names the process heap (`segment` or `NT`) |
| `RX_CPM=<dir>`, `WGPU_NATIVE=<dir>` | The CPM cache (Vulkan and SPIR-V headers, VMA, FFmpeg) and wgpu-native |
| `RX_TARGET=windows` | On Linux: the Windows game, with llvm-mingw's Linux build in `HOOKSHOT_MINGW` |
| `RX_RELEASE=1` | A release build (`make_game.py` sets it): no console window, the shaders prepared before the title |
| `RX_VK_PGO=gen`, `=<file>`, `=0`; `RX_VK_PGO_FILE=<path>` | (Triforce, 11-21, the performance audit's LIFT-10) A play profile for the renderer's own code (`gpu/vk/*.cpp`; not the vendored recompiler, ImGui or zstd). `gen`: a build whose renderer counts its branches; run it with `RX_VK_PGO_FILE=<path>`, make `<path>.reset` once the scene to record is loaded and `<path>.dump` at its end, then `llvm-profdata merge -o work\triforce_vk.profdata <path>`. `<file>`: built with that profile (its contents are in the object keys, its path is not). Not set: `make_game.py` passes `work\triforce_vk.profdata` when the tools have one and the compiler can read it; `0`: none. A profile from another version of the renderer is taken function by function, the changed ones built without it. Record it again for each release, and compare against `RX_VK_PGO=0` before shipping one (`nx2/build.py` `vk_pgo_flags`, `host/pgo_rt.c`) |
| `RX_PGO=gen` or `RX_PGO=<file>` | The hot code counting its branches for a profile, or built with one (`make_game.py` passes the shipped profile) |
| `A64_HOT_STICKY=0` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's LIFT-30, after Source A's; read when the game is translated, and in the lift key) `nx2/split_hot.py` deals the hot functions out afresh and gives every hot piece code_hot.c's whole preamble, as before. By default `<work>\image\hot_pieces.txt` keeps each hot function's piece: the next translation leaves the functions it knows where they were and puts a new one in the piece it shares the most calls with, else in the cheapest, unless that makes the costliest piece more than 1.15 times a fresh split's (then the split is the fresh one); and each piece keeps only the declarations of `f_` and `hos_` names it uses. An update that changes a few hot functions then compiles one or two hot pieces of about ten MB again, not up to eight. The functions' text is the same either way, and the hot profile goes by function name, so it fits both. The file is not in the lift key (`tests/test_sticky_hot_pieces.py`) |
| `RX_COLD_OPT=O2` or `=<file>`, `RX_COLD_PART_MB=<n>` | (Triforce, 11-22, the performance audit's LIFT-17, after Source A's; opt-in) The hot pieces are compiled at -O2 with the play profile and every other lifted chunk at -O1. `RX_COLD_OPT=O2`: every other lifted chunk at -O2 too; `=<file>`: only the chunks the file lists, one file name a line (`code_012.c`). Unset, empty or `O1`: as before. Each -O2 chunk is compiled as parts of `RX_COLD_PART_MB` MB by the lift's own estimate (6 when unset; `0`: no cut), cut only where a function begins and written under `<build>/split` with a key file a chunk, so a second build writes nothing (`nx2/cold_parts.py`, `tests/test_chunk_parts.py`): -O2's time grows with the square of a function's size. The lifted files themselves are not touched. Source A's figures: +4.4% and +5.5% fps where the game's update bounds the frame, for about twice the lifted compile's processor time (23 minutes against 5 without the cut); not built or measured with this game. The names begin with `RX_`, so setting them translates the game again once |
| `RX_MARCH=<level>` | The CPU level to build for (default this PC's: `x86-64-v3`, else `x86-64-v2`, else `x86-64`) |
| `RX_VK_ONLY=1` | Only the Vulkan back end, with no lift |
| `OOT_WORK`, `OOT_DUMP`, `OOT_EXEFS`, `OOT_ROMFS` | The work folder and the dump's folders, over `work/dump.txt` |
| `HOS_MODS=<folder>` | The mods folder, in place of `mods` in the game folder: for the running game (RomFS mods, cheat files) and for a build (the code patches it applies) |

## Mods: code patches and cheats

The game loads RomFS mods at its start (`host/mods.c`). The two kinds of mod that change what the game *does* go other
ways, because the game's code is C compiled ahead of time and cannot be patched while it runs.

**The mods folder**, for the game and for a build alike (`host/mods.c` `find_mods`, `nx2/code_mods.py` `find_mods`,
which must stay the same walk): a mod is a folder under `mods` that holds a `romfs`, `exefs` or `cheats` folder, at
most four levels down, and nothing below a mod is looked at. A folder whose name starts with `-` or `_` is off, one
that starts with `.` is skipped. Mods are ordered by their path under `mods`, ASCII letters' case ignored, and the last
one wins. Triforce Setup's mod manager can switch a mod by renaming its folder and order mods by a prefix, and needs
no other file.

**Code patches** (`<mod>/exefs/*.ips`, `*.pchtxt`), `nx2/code_mods.py`:

- `make_game.py` calls `build_step` once its copy of the game's code (`<work>/exefs`) is there and checked, after the
  copy the game folder keeps for updates (`.triforce_exefs`) is made and before the lift key is taken. The patches go
  into that copy, a module with a patch written again as a plain NSO, and the lift reads it: `lift.py`, `chunks.py`
  and the rest of the translator know nothing of mods.
- The lift key hashes the files of that copy, so the patched bytes are in it: a patch added, taken out, switched off
  or edited translates the game again, a patch that changes no byte does not, and neither does a change to
  `code_mods.py` itself (`LIFT_KEY_NOT`).
- A patch names its module by build id (the `.ips` file's name, the `.pchtxt` file's `@nsobid-`, trailing zeros left
  out or not) and may be for any module of the exefs. Offsets count the NSO's 0x100-byte header, as Atmosphere and the
  emulators read them; `.pchtxt` knows `@flag offset_shift`, `@enabled`, `@disabled`, `@little-endian`, `@big-endian`
  and `@stop`. Mods in order, a mod's files by name, the later write wins and the build says which file wrote over
  which.
- A file is refused whole, with a line that says why: another build's, cut short, a value that cannot be read, a write
  into the header, past the image or between the segments. The last leaves out code caves, which the translator has
  not been tried on.
- The build writes `triforce_code_mods.txt` into the game folder when it is done: a line per patch file, tab
  separated, `applied` or `refused`, the file's FNV-1a 64 and size, its path under the mods folder, then the module
  and the bytes changed, or the reason. The next build (the game's update offer runs `--update`) reads the mods folder
  again, so the same patches are built in again, and says which of the record's are gone, changed or refused now. The
  game reads the record at its start (`host/mod_code.h`) and logs for each patch file whether it is built in or waits
  for a build, and which built-in patches are gone from the folder.

**Cheats** (`<mod>/cheats/<first 16 digits of main's build id>.txt`), `host/mod_cheats.h` and `host/mod_cheats.c`:

- Read at the start, run by an interpreter once a game frame, at the game's present and on its thread
  (`hos_mod_cheats_frame`). Nothing is compiled in, so a cheat file needs no build. NX2navi compiled in the cheats that
  write instructions; Triforce's translator has no switchable patches, so such a cheat (an opcode 0 into a module's
  `.text`) is refused and belongs in a code patch.
- The opcodes it runs are listed at the top of `host/mod_cheats.h`: 0 to A and C0 to C3 of Atmosphere's cheat machine.
  Any other is refused when the file is read, the whole cheat, by name. Registers are shared by one file's cheats within
  a frame and cleared between frames; a master code runs first.
- Memory is the program image and the heap, each access checked (`mod_cheat_readable`); the modules' code
  (`hos_code` in the generated `hos_image.c`) is never written. Region 0 is the module main's base (`hos_main_base`),
  1 and 2 `HOS_HEAP_BASE`, 3 `0x08000000`, as `host/hos_svc.c` tells the game.
- Each cheat that is not a heading has a row on the System tab, 96 at most (`gpu/vk/mod_cheat_rows.h`; option ids 130
  to 133 in `gpu/vk/vk_api.h`). The rows are not in the options file: `triforce_cheats.txt` in the game folder has a
  line for each cheat that is on, its number (FNV-1a 32 of `<mod>/<name>`) and that text, and a cheat listed there is
  on at the start.

## The options file

`triforce_options.ini`, in the game folder, holds the Insert menu's rows as `<key>=<number>`: the row's name in lower
case with `_` for spaces (`frame_generation=2`, `invert_camera_x=1`), except Output resolution, which is `output_res`.
`gpu/vk/vk_menu.cpp` writes the whole file at every change (`save`; (Triforce) merged by key with a change made outside the game meanwhile, and written whole: `RX_VK_OPTIONS_MERGE` above), with `options_version=10`,
`microphone_device=<name>` when a microphone is chosen by name, and the controls (`pad_<button>` and `key_<button>`:
the README's [Controls](../readme.md#controls)). A value outside a row's range is clamped when it is read. Where the
number is not the obvious one:

| Key | Values |
|---|---|
| `gpu_device` | 0 Auto, n the n-th card with Vulkan 1.3 as the row lists them |
| `frame_generation` | 0 Off, 1 Auto, 2 to 8 the multiplier, 9 AMD FSR frame generation (the made frame by AMD's frame generation library, FSR 4's or FSR 3.1's by the card, with the picture without the HUD; paused with HDR Native or RenoDX's RGBA16F scene, where the renderer's own makes the frame as at 2x: `gpu/vk/vk_fsr4.cpp` `fsr4fg`; where that cannot run the value stays in the file and the row shows and runs 2x: `gpu/vk/framegen_rows.h`) |
| `dlss_frame_generation` | 0 Off, 1 2x, 2 3x, 3 4x: NVIDIA DLSS frame generation, the Frame generation row's NVIDIA DLSS values (`gpu/vk/merged_rows.h`; read at the start, `gpu/vk/vk_dlssg.cpp`). The row sets `frame_generation` to 0 with it |
| `frame_rate_cap` | 0 60, 1 90, 2 120, 3 144, 4 165, 5 240, 6 the monitor's refresh rate, 7 uncapped; -1 45, -2 40, -3 30 |
| `frame_pacing` | 0 Off, 1 frames on an even line. With `steady_frame_rate`, the Frame pacing row (`gpu/vk/merged_rows.h`): Off is 0 and 0, Even 1 and 0, Steady (any rate) 1 and 1, Steady (refresh steps) 1 and 2 |
| `steady_frame_rate` | 0 Off, 1 any rate (the cap, then 200, 180, 160, 144, 120, 110, 100, 90, 80, 72, 66, 60, 55, 50, 45, 40, 36, 30 under it), 2 whole divisions of the display's refresh rate under the cap, from 30 up (`host/pace_steady.h`) |
| `resolution` | 0 Auto, 1 100%, 2 75%, 3 67%, 4 50%, 5 58%, 6 33% (with DLSS and FSR: 3 Quality, 5 Balanced, 4 Performance, 6 Ultra Performance; their Native (100%) is `dlss` 2, 5 or 6) |
| `output_res` | 0 2048x1152, 1 2560x1440, 2 3840x2160, 3 1920x1080, 4 5120x1440, 5 5120x2880, 6 5120x2160, 8 Screen, 9 Custom (`output_width` x `output_height`), 7 1536x864 (the handheld profile; before `options_version` 8 it was `handheld_profile=1`, read as 7) |
| `ultrawide` | 0 16:9, 1 16:10, 2 21:9, 3 32:9, 4 4:3 (before `options_version` 6, 1 was the monitor's shape: read as the nearest of the first four, `gpu/vk/ultrawide_rows.h`) |
| `hdr`, `renodx_hdr` | the HDR row (`gpu/vk/merged_rows.h`): `hdr` 0 Off, 1 Expanded, 2 Native (the game's own); `renodx_hdr` 1 with `hdr` 2 is Native + RenoDX |
| `nvidia_reflex`, `amd_anti-lag_2` | the Low latency row by the card's maker: 0 Off, 1 On, 2 On + Boost (NVIDIA alone) |
| `output_width`, `output_height` | Custom's size in pixels (`output_res=8`): a connected screen's as chosen in the menu, or any size set with the Output width and Output height rows, shown while it is the choice: 640 to 7680 and 360 to 2880, in steps of 8 in the menu; 2560 and 1440 until they are set |
| `hdr_paper_white`, `hdr_peak_brightness` | nits: 80 to 400, and 400 to 10,000 |
| `renodx_tonemapper` | 0 Vanilla, 1 PsychoV35, 2 RenoDRT |
| `renodx_gamma_correction` | 0 Off, 1 2.2, 2 BT.1886 (2.4) |
| `renodx_match_tone_slope` | 0 Off, 1 On (as out of the box): PsychoV35's rows, with `renodx_tone_slope` and `renodx_hue_clip` |
| `renodx_tone_slope`, `renodx_highlight_saturation` | 0 to 100, 50 unchanged: the first PsychoV35's, the second RenoDRT's |
| `renodx_blowout`, `renodx_flare` | 0 to 100, 0 none: RenoDRT's |
| `renodx_hue_clip`, `renodx_hue_correction`, `renodx_color_grading` | percent, 0 to 100: 50, 100 and 100 out of the box; the first PsychoV35's, the others RenoDRT's |
| `dlss` | the Upscaler: 0 the game's own, 1 NVIDIA DLSS, 2 NVIDIA DLAA, 3 AMD FSR 3.1, 4 AMD FSR 4, 5 AMD FSR 4 Native AA, 6 AMD FSR 3.1 Native AA. The row names the engine; 2, 5 and 6 are its Native (100%) on the Render resolution row (`gpu/vk/upscaler_rows.h`) |
| `anti-aliasing` | 0 the game's own SMAA, 1 Off, 2 FXAA, 3 SMAA Ultra |
| `hud_placement`, `hud_health_x` ... `hud_minimap_scale` | the HUD placement row (`host/hos_wide_ui.c`, the groups' transforms): 0 the game's, 1 Custom; each group's `_x` and `_y` -15 to 15 (percent of the picture, right and up), `_scale` 75 to 125 percent. Read at each present (`HOS_OPT_HUD_PLACEMENT` to `HOS_OPT_HUD_MINIMAP`); the minimap's through `nx2/oot_patches.py` `patch_gameplay_hud_screen` |
| `texture_detail` | 0 Automatic, 1 Full, 2 Reduced, 3 Low |
| `fps_counter` | 0 Off, 1 On, 2 Full (NX2navi's full performance overlay in the counter's place: `vk_menu.cpp` `draw_perf`, the frame times and the figures per frame `gpu/vk/perf_stats.h`, the machine's numbers sampled twice a second on a thread of their own `gpu/vk/perf_machine.cpp`) |
| `opaque_textures_as_bc1` | 0 Off, 1 On |
| `sharpness`, `rumble_strength`, `motion_sensitivity_x`, `motion_sensitivity_y` | percent |
| `mouse_sensitivity` | hundredths: 100 is 1.00 |
| `left_stick_dead_zone`, `right_stick_dead_zone` | -1 Default (XInput's ~24%), 0 to 50 percent (before `options_version` 8 a `stick_dead_zone` of 1 to 5, 5, 10, 15, 20 or 30% for both sticks, is read into each stick's own where it had none) |
| `vibration_curve` | hundredths, 20 to 150: the power on the motors' rumble, 100 is 1.00 (none) |
| `walk_key` | 0 Off, 1 Caps Lock, 2 Shift, 3 Ctrl, 4 Tab |
| `walk_scale` | hundredths, 20 to 90: 51 is 0.51 of the stick's travel |
| `robust_buffers` | 0 Auto (on), 1 Off, 2 On |
| `game_speed` | 0 Per frame, 1 Real time |
| `text_speed` | 0 Normal, 1 2x, 2 4x, 3 Instant |
| `achievements` | 1 on, 0 off: whether anything is earned (`host/achievements.c`, option id 175); at once |
| `achievement_notices` | 1 on, 0 off: an earned one's notice and its sound (option id 176); it is earned and listed either way |
| `menu_sounds` | percent, 0 to 100: 80 unless set, 0 silent. The settings' own sounds, the game's interface sounds from its RomFS (`host/menu_sound.c`, `gpu/vk/menu_sound_events.h`; option ids 135 and 136), under `master_volume` |
| `microphone` | 0 Off, 1 Default, 2 and up a microphone (the name in `microphone_device` decides which) |
| `mouse_camera` | 0 As the stick, 1 Native |
| `layout_preset` | 0 Nintendo, 1 Xbox / PC, 2 Swap Face, 3 Custom |
| `button_prompts` | 0 Auto (the controller there at the start, XInput's without a name the Xbox's; none: the device used last in the run before, `<user>/prompts_auto.txt`, else the keyboard and mouse), 1 Switch, 2 Xbox, 3 PlayStation 4, 4 PlayStation 5, 5 Steam Deck, 6 Keyboard & mouse (`host/prompts.c`, `nx2/prompt_keys.py`) |
| `evil_hd` | 0 Off (the game as shipped), 1 On: Evil HD, the Graphics tab's row (`host/evil_hd.c`, option id 420, Evil HD as it is now: the row, F2, `RX_VK_EVIL_FLIP`; read each frame; `HOS_EVIL_HD` over it for the foliage). Its render distance reaches the areas entered after it is turned on (from the start of a run: all) |
| `replay_helper_threads` | 0 Off, 1 to 7 the parallel replay tried on 2 to 8 threads, 8 Automatic (the default; the Debug tab). A file that says 0 stays Off: delete the line, or choose Automatic on the Debug tab, for the default |

The Game language row is not in this file: its choice is the code line of `language.txt` in the game folder, which the
menu rewrites in place (`gpu/vk/game_language.h`: the code alone on that line, the rest of the file as it was, and only a
code the file itself names) and the host reads at the start (`host/nn_applet.c`). Nor are the Mod cheats rows: which
of a mod's cheats are on is the host's to keep, in `triforce_cheats.txt` (*Mods: code patches and cheats* above).

(Triforce) `options_version=10` (11-24) changed the keys alone: Plus is Tab, and no longer Enter too, the key controller
software (Steam Input's Desktop layout, for one) sends for a pad's A, which paused the game at every A press. A file
before 10 whose `key_plus` is exactly the old default pair, `9,13`, is read as `9,0` and written so at the menu's next
save; any other `key_plus` was chosen and is kept, as is `9,13` in a file of 10 or later (`host/nn_hid.c` `binds_load`).
Every other rule reads a 10 as it reads a 9, the output sizes' schema (`gpu/vk/output_sizes.h` `OUTPUT_OPTIONS_VERSION`),
and the launcher brings older files up to 9 and leaves the keys to the game.

(Triforce, 11-24) The ocarina's five note controls are five more buttons of those lines, after the left stick's and the
camera's directions: `note_l`, `note_r`, `note_a`, `note_y`, `note_x` (named by the button each note is in the game),
each a `pad_` line (one gamepad input, -1 none) and a `key_` line (two keys, 0 none; never a mouse button). A file
without them, and a new one, has them unset: the game's own buttons play the notes. A bound row's input is a note only
while the game waits for a note button (the field ocarina, a song lesson, the skull kids' songs, the scarecrow's
recording and its recall), where it presses nothing else and that note's own button no longer plays (in a song lesson it
still does); a note's input can be another control's too, the one rule the other rows do not have (`host/nn_hid.c`
`oc_notes`, `nx2/oot_patches.py` `patch_ocarina_wait`, `tests/test_ocarina_notes.py`).

(Triforce) `options_version=8` grouped the rows and merged those that chose one thing twice (`gpu/vk/merged_rows.h`; the
436f9ac-based builds wrote 7 for it, which v0.5.1 had already given to Frame pacing's default: its 7 still has the old rows'
lines, so a file before 8 is moved, and one of those builds' 7, without them, keeps what it has). A file
from before keeps its values; these lines are read once and not written again, their rows' jobs done otherwise:
`quality_preset` (the textures' and the mirrors' memory budgets are the card's own, from its video memory), `upscaling`
(the window's filter follows the sizes: `gpu/vk/upscaler_rows.h` `auto_filter`, AMD FSR 1 when the picture is enlarged, the
plain copy otherwise and always with HDR Native), `reflex_frame_limit` (off, the old default, until Reflex holding the cap
with DLSS frame generation has been played: `RX_VK_REFLEX_LIMIT=1` for a run), `handheld_profile` (an `output_res`) and `stick_dead_zone` (each
stick's own).

The other rows are 0 Off and 1 On. Some are read at the start, before the menu exists, by the code that needs them:
`output_res` (`host/nn_applet.c`; 7 is the handheld profile), `ultrawide` (`host/hos_main.c`), `frame_rate_cap`
(`host/nn_vi.c`; the monitor's rate is the display's the window opens on, rounded, 30 to 240, and 60 when it cannot be read), `robust_buffers`
(`gpu/vk/vk_main.cpp`), `texture_detail` (`gpu/vk/vk_texture.cpp`, at the first texture) and the controls (`host/nn_hid.c`).

The `renodx_` rows other than `renodx_hdr` are read at every frame (`gpu/vk/vk_renodx.cpp` `fill_settings`), and the
menu shows the rows of one tonemapper while it is the one chosen. RenoDX's mod for this game has Vanilla and PsychoV35;
RenoDRT and the gamma correction come from `gpu/vk/shaders/renodx_glue`, three files of Triforce's own that
`scripts/renodx_shaders.py` compiles together with RenoDX's scene shaders and its output encoder as the build fetched them
(nothing of RenoDX is in the repository or changed). RenoDRT calls RenoDX's library; the gamma correction is on the
finished picture, HUD included, and the tone mappers are given a peak with it taken out, so that the brightest light
still lands on the HDR peak brightness. A change to a glue file changes the stamp of `renodx_spv.h`, which the next build
then compiles again.

RivaTuner Statistics Server has no row: where it is installed and running it is the game's frame limiter, and an options
file that still has the test builds' `rivatuner_limit=` is read by nothing (the next save drops the line). A thread of
`gpu/vk/rtss_limit.cpp` looks twice a second: it puts the limit (`gpu/vk/pace_rule.h` `pace_rtss_want`: the cap in
effect, Monitor's rate and the caps under 60 included, times the frames shown for each of the game's with frame
generation 2x to 8x or AMD FSR 4) into RTSS's profile for the executable through RTSS's own hooks library
(`RTSSHooks64.dll` from RTSS's folder: `FramerateLimit` and `FramerateLimitDenominator`, nothing else, no other profile),
reads it back, and once RTSS is seen holding the game to it (`gpu/vk/rtss_decide.h` `rtss_decide`) the host's wait steps
aside (`gpu/vk/vk_pace.cpp`), the in-house frame generation's display thread leaves its own spacing of made frames to
RTSS, and Reflex is given no frame limit. A limit lower than the profile's waits 5 s before it is written, a higher one
1 s, so a multiplier that changes in play does not rewrite the profile each time. What the profile held is kept in
`triforce_rtss_profile.txt` in the game folder before the first change (one line per executable, the others' lines
kept, a file that does not read left alone) and goes back at the exit; the next start puts it back after a kill or a
crash, unless the player changed the profile since. The game's own wait holds the frames instead with frame generation
on Auto (its presents follow the screen's refresh, not the game, so no limit on them holds the game) and with DLSS frame
generation (Streamline presents on a pace of its own: `HOS_RTSS_DLSSG=1` tries RTSS there), during movies (the profile
keeps the play limit), while no picture went out for 200 ms (a minimised window), while RTSS is not installed, not
running or has not hooked the game, and when the profile does not keep the limit (no administrator rights) or RTSS
refuses it, unless a limit the player set in RTSS, at or under the game's, holds the game (then RTSS holds movies too).
A hold needs a present since the frame start two frames back, so a window that stops showing gives the frames back
within two frames. RTSS's count past 1.05 times the limit three
periods running, 30 s hooked with the limit and no hold, or the game's frames coming sooner than the cap give the frames
back; RTSS is tried again a minute later (three times), and at once when RTSS starts again or another limit is wanted.
The game never starts RTSS, installs nothing and asks for no rights. `vk: pace:` lines in the log say which limiter is in
force and why; the Full overlay's Frame limit line names RivaTuner or NVIDIA Reflex after the rate when either holds the frames.

## Layout

| Path | What |
|---|---|
| `docs/design.md` | why mkw-recomp's pipeline, the architecture, MKW's lessons as rules, stages, testing |
| `nx2/` | the lift pipeline and its tests (mkw-recomp's, made game-neutral; `kit.py` has the paths), and `make_game.py`, which the builders run |
| `host/` | the standalone Horizon runtime (TOTKPort's `hos/` as adapted by mkw-recomp), with the native NVN2 layer `nvn_native.c`; `host/posix` has the Win32 calls on Linux |
| `gpu/vk/` | the Vulkan back end (`hookshot_vk.dll`, `hookshot_vk.so`), with Eden's shader recompiler and sirit |
| `gpu/render/`, `gpu/third_party/` | the WebGPU renderer the Windows exe falls back to when it was built with wgpu-native, its op stream (`rx_nvn.h`), suyu's decoders |
| `builder/` | Hookshot Builder: the Windows app (.NET 8 WPF), its setup and MSI, and the Linux builder and launcher scripts |
| `setup/` | Triforce Setup, the program players run (.NET Framework 4.8, WPF): `Setup.cs` installs, updates and runs `make_game.py`, `Shell*.cs` and `Main.xaml` are its window, `Guard.cs` is what keeps a run from damaging an install, `Mods.cs` the mods folder as the Mods page lists and changes it, `Steam.cs` and `SteamArt.cs` the Add to Steam shortcut and its pictures, `Sound.cs` the game's own decide and cancel sounds as Setup plays them (decoded in memory from the player's game folder: no sound is shipped; `TRIFORCE_SOUND_LOG=<file>` writes down, in a test run, the sounds that would have played) ([CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md#triforce-setup)) |
| `scripts/` | test runs, the profile and log readers, the save copier (`oot_saves.py`), release tooling, `scripts/linux` (dependencies, AppImage) |
| `tests/` | the game-free checks, all run by `tests/run_all.py` |
| `third_party/` | a64-kit (Hookshot's few changes marked `(hookshot)`), libopus, zbic (Atmosphere's zstd with ZBIC, for unpacking only), SDL2's licence, Dear ImGui and its Vulkan backend, zstd's decoder, the Libertinus fonts, SMAA's reference shader and lookup textures (the Anti-aliasing row's SMAA Ultra) |

License: GPL-3.0-or-later, as mkw-recomp: a64-kit's translator is suyu's (GPL-2.0-or-later) and Eden's code is
GPL-3.0.

### Native ultrawide on 0.6

`options_version=9` keeps output 7 for handheld and uses 8 for Screen, 9 for Custom.
The menu lists connected monitors by name and saves their size, so unplugging or
reordering monitors does not change the picture. Custom retains the entered pair
when choosing a monitor; Use current window size copies the drawable pixel size.
A non-16:9 output owns the picture shape; the Aspect ratio row keeps the upstream
four shapes for 16:9 outputs. The HUD, menus and story movies retain 16:9 geometry.
The title movie fills through 12:5 (+1% rounding tolerance); wider titles keep bars.

Both stock 0.6 and native 0.5.2 wrote version 8. Native files are identified by
`output_width` or `output_height`, which their writer always emitted. Their old
Screen 7 and Custom 8 become 8 and 9. Stock handheld 7 stays 7. Native version-8
fixed wide pictures stay wide; stock fixed wide outputs migrate by their old
Aspect ratio choice. Files before version 8 retain the earlier shape migrations.
The host, menu and native launcher apply these rules before saving version 9.
The generic manifest cannot migrate a whole file, so offers only fixed values
that mean the same under all file versions; use the menu or native launcher for
handheld, Screen and Custom.
